A smooth family of G 2 \textup{G}_{2} -instantons over a generalised Kummer construction
Dominik Gutwein

Abstract
We construct a smooth 1-parameter family of G 2 \textup{G}_{2} -instantons over a generalised Kummer construction desingularising the G 2 \textup{G}_{2} -orbifold discovered in [ Joy96b , Example 18] . For this we extend the gluing construction for G 2 \textup{G}_{2} -instantons developed in [ Wal13a ] to Kummer constructions resolving G 2 \textup{G}_{2} -orbifolds whose singular strata are of codimension 6 and to connections (and entire families of connections) whose linearised instanton operator has a non-trivial cokernel. In order to overcome the corresponding obstructions, we utilise a ℤ 2 \mathbb{Z}_{2} -action on the ambient manifold. More precisely, we perturb the (family of) pre-glued almost-instantons inside the class of ℤ 2 \mathbb{Z}_{2} -invariant connections, which has the advantage that only the ℤ 2 \mathbb{Z}_{2} -invariant locus of the cokernel needs to vanish. We then prove that the instantons that we construct over the resolution of the orbifold in [ Joy96b , Example 18] are all infinitesimally rigid and non-flat. Moreover, we show that the resulting curve into the moduli space of G 2 \textup{G}_{2} -instantons modulo gauge is injective, that is, no two distinct instantons within the family are gauge-equivalent. To the author’s knowledge this is the first example of a smooth 1-parameter family of instantons over a compact G 2 \textup{G}_{2} -manifold.



1
Introduction

A
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton is a connection
A
on a principal bundle
P\to Y
over a
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifold
(Y,\phi)
whose curvature
F_{A}\in\Omega^{2}(Y,\mathfrak{g}_{P})
satisfies the equation

F_{A}\wedge\psi=0

where
\psi\coloneqq*\phi
is the coassociative 4-form on
Y
. These
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons first appeared in the physics literature
[
CDFN83
]
and are absolute minima of the Yang–Mills functional (and therefore Yang–Mills connections). A slightly alternative (but equivalent) perspective is that
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons are the critical points of a
\textup{G}_{2}
-analogue of the Chern–Simons functional on 3-manifolds
[
DT98
]
. Since its suggestion by Donaldson and Thomas
[
DT98
]
, one of the driving themes in the theory of special holonomy has been the possibility of constructing enumerative invariants based on such instantons (formally mirroring the familiar Casson-Floer picture for low dimensional manifolds; cf.
[
DS11
]
). However, a rigorous construction of such an invariant (even a complete understanding of the list of its ingredients; cf.
[
DS11
, Section 6.2]
,
[
Wal13b
, Chapter 6]
,
[
Hay19
]
,
[
DW19
]
) is met with great analytical difficulties mainly arising from various non-compactness phenomena (cf.
[
Tia00
]
,
[
Wal17
]
,
[
Ber23
]
).

At the same time, the construction of concrete examples of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons, which could shed light onto the shape of such a potential invariant and could one day help to compute it, remains a difficult task. In this article we add to the list of known examples by constructing a smooth 1-parameter family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons over a compact
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifold that arises via Joyce’s generalised Kummer construction. The deformation theory of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons is governed by an elliptic operator, which in the examples constructed in this article has a 1-dimensional kernel (due to the deformations within the family). Since the Fredholm index of any elliptic operator over an odd-dimensional compact manifold is zero, the instantons constructed in this article have therefore a one-dimensional obstruction space. However, the obstructions are anti-invariant under a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action on the underlying manifold and the constructed instantons are therefore ’
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-unobstructed’. To the author’s knowledge this is the first example of a smooth 1-parameter family of instantons over a compact manifold and shows, in particular, that in the presence of non-trivial group actions instantons may not lie isolated in their moduli space (even when equivariant transversality holds).

The generalised Kummer construction:
This is historically the first of currently three construction methods (
[
Joy96b
]
,
[
Kov03
]
, and
[
JK21
]
) for producing compact
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifolds and was developed by Joyce in
[
Joy96a
,
Joy96b
]
. It starts with a flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold, whose singular set is well-behaved (see
Section 2.1
). The construction then requires a set of resolution data, which in the context of this article roughly consists of a collection of Calabi–Yau 3-folds – each one resolving the transverse singularity model for one singular stratum of the orbifold – with an asymptotically locally Euclidean (ALE) geometry. At the topological level, the generalised Kummer construction produces a smooth manifold
\hat{Y}
by replacing a neighbourhood of each singular stratum by a neighborhood in a certain fiber bundle whose fiber consists of the respective Calabi–Yau 3-fold in the resolution data. Geometrically, it first produces a
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure
\tilde{\phi}\in\Omega^{3}(\hat{Y})
with small torsion which is then corrected to a torsion-free
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure
\phi\in\Omega^{3}(\hat{Y})
via a singular perturbation. In order to apply this perturbation-scheme the fiberwise geometry of the glued-in Calabi–Yau bundle needs to be scaled down. In fact, by decreasing the scale-factor further, the Kummer construction produces a 1-parameter family of compact
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifolds
1
1

1

Technically, the smooth manifold
\hat{Y}
in the construction also depends on
t
. However, since its diffeomorphism-type
remains constant for
t\in(0,\varepsilon)
, we will regard it in this section as one fixed manifold.

(\hat{Y},\phi_{t})_{t\in(0,\varepsilon)}
that degenerates for
t\to 0
back to the original orbifold.

Summary of results:
This article constructs
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons over generalised Kummer constructions by perturbing connections which are in a quantified sense already close to being instantons. These almost-instantons are constructed from so-called gluing data (specified in
Section 4
), which roughly consist of a flat connection over the orbifold and an asymptotically flat Hermitian Yang–Mills connection over each resolving ALE Calabi–Yau manifold. In fact, we allow for a family of such gluing data, producing a smooth family of almost-instantons. Moreover, when deforming these almost-instantons to genuine instantons we make use of the fact that many generalised Kummer constructions admit finite group actions preserving the coassociative 4-form (cf.
Section 2.1
). If the gluing data is compatible with such a group action (in the sense of
Section 4
), then the almost-instantons are also invariant and can subsequently be deformed inside the class of invariant connections. This has the advantage that in order to perturb these connections, one only needs to show that the invariant part of the obstruction space associated to the linearised instanton operator vanishes. Summarising this discussion, our main theorem can schematically be stated as follows:

Theorem A

(
Section 3.2
,
Section 4
,
Section 4
, and
Section 5.2
)
.

Let
(\hat{Y},\phi_{t})_{t\in(0,\varepsilon)}
be a degenerating family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifolds arising from the generalised Kummer construction resolving an orbifold whose singular strata are all of codimension 6.
2
2

2

We concentrate on orbifolds with codimension 6 singularities in order to complement the constructions in
[
Wal13a
]
and
[
Pla24
]
. However, most of our results can easily be adapted for orbifolds with codimension 4 singularities.
Furthermore, let
H
be a finite (possibly trivial) group acting on
\hat{Y}
as in
Section 2.1
. For an
\mathfrak{F}
-family of
H
-equivariant gluing data (as defined in
Section 4
,
Section 4
, and
Section 4
), where
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}
is a compact interval
3
3

3

We restrict to intervals because this amounts to a somewhat simpler notation and is the relevant case for our example. However, a generalisation of these results to any compact manifold
\mathfrak{F}
(possibly with boundary) is straight-forward.
, there exists for each
t\in(0,\varepsilon)
a bundle
\pi\colon\thinspace\hat{P}\to\hat{Y}
together with a smooth
\mathfrak{F}
-family of
H
-invariant connections
(\tilde{A}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\hat{P})
which are (in a quantified sense) close to being
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons over
(\hat{Y},\phi_{t})
. If the gluing data additionally satisfies
Section 5.2
with respect to the action of
H
, then there exists an
\varepsilon^{\prime}
such that for each
t\in(0,\varepsilon^{\prime})
, the family of connection
(\tilde{A}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}
can be deformed to a smooth family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons.

We then apply this theorem to prove the following:

Theorem B

(
Section 6.4
,
Section 6.4
, and
Section 6.5
)
.

Let
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
be the
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold constructed in
[
Joy96b
, Example 18]
and let
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus\pi\mathbb{Z}
be a compact interval. There exists a
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifold
(\hat{Y},\phi_{t})
arising from
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
via the generalised Kummer construction and an
\textup{SO}(14)
-bundle
\pi\colon\thinspace\hat{P}\to\hat{Y}
such that for sufficiently small
t>0
there is a smooth
\mathfrak{F}
-family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons
(A_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\hat{P})
. All instantons in this family are infinitesimally irreducible (in the sense of
Section 3.2
) and non-flat. Furthermore, the induced curve
\mathfrak{F}\to\mathcal{A}(\hat{P})/\mathcal{G},\mathfrak{f}\mapsto[A_{t,\mathfrak{f}}]
into the space of connections modulo gauge is injective.

Comparison to previous constructions:
To the author’s knowledge, there are three prior methods for constructing
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons over compact
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifolds developed in
[
Wal13a
]
,
[
EW15
]
,
[
Pla24
]
. Two of these methods (
[
Wal13a
]
and
[
Pla24
]
) focus on manifolds arising as orbifold resolutions and are in spirit very similar to our construction. In fact, in this article we extend the construction method originally developed in
[
Wal13a
]
(which in the situation that we are considering becomes the same as the method in
[
Pla24
]
) to Kummer constructions resolving orbifolds with codimension 6 singularities, to families of connections, and, in particular, to connections whose linearised instanton-operator has a non-trivial cokernel. As described above, we overcome the corresponding obstructions in the perturbation-scheme by utilizing symmetries of the underlying manifold. A somewhat similar technique has recently been developed to produce associative submanifolds in
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifolds arising out of the generalised Kummer construction
[
DPW23
]
. The fact that we perturb families of connections causes additional work compared to the construction in
[
Wal13a
]
. This can for example be seen in
Section 3
where additional work is needed to prove the smoothness of the deformed family (regarded as a map from an interval
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}
into the space of connections equipped with its Fréchet space structure) and to derive estimates on the derivatives of this family. These estimates are needed later in
Section 6.5
to prove that the constructed path into the space of connections modulo gauge is injective.

Outline of the paper:
In
Section 2
we recall the required background on the generalised Kummer construction, the construction of ALE Calabi–Yau 3-folds, and asymptotically flat Hermitian Yang–Mills connections over such ALE Calabi–Yau 3-folds.

In
Section 3
we prove with
Section 3.2
an abstract existence theorem for
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons over generalised Kummer constructions. It roughly states that whenever an
\mathfrak{F}
-family of
H
-invariant connections
(\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}
(for
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}
an interval and
H
a finite group acting on
\hat{Y}
preserving
*\phi_{t}
) is in a quantified sense sufficiently close to being a family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton and if the
H
-invariant cokernel of the linearised instanton operators associated to each
\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
vanishes, then
(\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}
can be perturbed to a nearby family of
H
-invariant instantons. Moreover, we prove that this family of instantons has the same order of differentiability (seen as a map from
\mathfrak{F}
into the space of connections) as the initial family of connections and establish estimates on the derivatives of this family. Ultimately, we give a sufficient condition for the perturbed connections to be infinitesimally irreducible.

In
Section 4
we then give the construction of the family of model connections to which we later apply
Section 3.2
.
Section 4
and
Section 4
also prove some of the estimates required for the application of
Section 3.2
and
Section 3.2
.

Section 5
studies the linearised instanton operator associated to the connections constructed in the previous section. It ultimately establishes the last missing condition of
Section 3.2
(the linear estimate) under
Section 5.2
on the gluing data.

Section 6
considers the
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold discovered in
[
Joy96b
, Example 18]
, finds a resolution thereof together with an
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action, and a 1-parameter family of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant gluing data to which
Section 1
above can be applied. Furthermore, it proves that the resulting family of instantons consists of elements that are non-flat, infinitesimally rigid, and pairwise not gauge-equivalent.

1.2
Notation

In this article we use the following notation:

•

If
E\to Y
is a vector bundle, then we denote for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
by
C^{k,\alpha}(E)
the space of sections of
E
which are of regularity
C^{k,\alpha}
.

•

For two Banach spaces
B_{1},B_{2}
we will denote the Banach space of bounded linear maps
B_{1}\to B_{2}
by
\textup{Lin}(B_{1},B_{2})
.

•

c>0
will denote a generic constant whose value may change from one appearance to the next. This constant will always be
t
-independent, except once in the proof of
Section 3.2
where this dependence is noted.

•

If
Y
carries a Riemannian metric
g
, then for any vector
v\in TY
we will denote the associated covector by
v^{\flat}\coloneqq g(v,\cdot)\in T^{*}Y
. Similarly, we will denote for a covector
a\in T^{*}Y
by
a^{\sharp}\in TY
the vector defined by
a=g(a^{\sharp},\cdot)
.

2
Background

In this section we first review the necessary background on Joyce’s generalised Kummer construction and explain in
Section 2.2
a method for finding ALE Calabi–Yau 3-folds, which play an integral part in this construction. In the last section we discuss Hermitian Yang–Mills connections over ALE Calabi–Yau 3-folds which are needed for the construction of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons later in this article.

2.1
Joyce’s generalised Kummer construction

This section explains the generalised Kummer construction, a method developed (and extended) by Joyce
[
Joy96a
,
Joy96b
,
Joy00
]
to produce
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifolds as desingularisations of certain flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifolds. This section follows the presentations in
[
DPW23
, Section 2]
,
[
Gut24b
, Section 2.1]
, and
[
Gut24a
, Chapter 2]
very closely. The following class of examples fixes our conventions and serves as model for the
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifolds (and orbifolds) considered in this article:

Example 2.1
.

Let
(Z,\omega,\Omega)
be a Calabi–Yau manifold or orbifold of complex dimension 3 with Kähler form
\omega\in\Omega^{2}(Z)
and holomorphic volume form
\Omega\in\Omega^{3}(Z,\mathbb{C})
. These are related via
\frac{1}{6}\omega^{3}=\frac{i}{8}\Omega\wedge\bar{\Omega}
.

1.

The product
\mathbb{R}\times Z
carries a torsion-free
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure defined by

\phi\coloneqq\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s\wedge\omega+\textup{Im}\thinspace\Omega\in\Omega^{3}(\mathbb{R}\times Z)

(2.1)

where
s
denotes the coordinate on
\mathbb{R}
. The corresponding coassociative
4
-form is given by
\psi=\frac{1}{2}\omega\wedge\omega+\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s\wedge\textup{Re}\thinspace\Omega
.

2.

Assume that
\textup{Crys}<\textup{O}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\mathbb{R}
is a crystallographic group that acts on
Z
via
\rho\colon\thinspace\textup{Crys}\to\textup{Isom}(Z,g_{\omega})
such that for any
(\pm 1,v)\in\textup{Crys}

\rho(\pm 1,v)^{*}\omega=\pm\omega\quad\textup{and}\quad\rho(\pm 1,v)^{*}\textup{Im}\thinspace\Omega=\textup{Im}\thinspace\Omega.

(2.2)

The
3
-form
\phi
is invariant under the product action on
\mathbb{R}\times Z
and descends therefore to a torsion-free
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure on the quotient
Y\coloneqq(\mathbb{R}\times Z)/\textup{Crys}
. By a slight abuse of notation we denote the corresponding
3
-form on
Y
by
\phi
as well. Note that whenever
Crys
is a Bieberbach group (i.e. a 1-dimensional lattice) then the action is free and taking the quotient does not introduce additional singularities in
Y
.

This article concentrates on
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifolds that satisfy the following assumption:

Assumption 2.2
.

Let
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
be a compact flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold and denote by
\mathcal{S}
the set of connected components of the singular set of
Y_{0}
. We assume that for every
S\in\mathcal{S}
there exist

1.

A finite subgroup
\Gamma_{S}<\textup{SU}(3)
which acts freely on
\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
, a lattice
\Lambda_{S}<\mathbb{R}
, and a group action
\rho_{S}\colon\thinspace\Lambda_{S}\to N_{\textup{O}(\mathbb{C}^{3})}(\Gamma_{S})/\Gamma_{S}\subset\textup{Isom}(\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S},g_{0})
that satisfies (
2.2
). Denote by

(Y_{S}\coloneqq(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S},\phi_{S})

the corresponding
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold from
Section 2.1
.

2.

An open set

\mathcal{V}_{S}\coloneqq(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0)/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}\subset Y_{S}

for a fixed
\kappa>0
and an open embedding
\mathtt{J}_{S}\colon\thinspace\mathcal{V}_{S}\to Y_{0}
with
S\subset\mathtt{J}_{S}(\mathcal{V}_{S})
and
\mathtt{J}_{S}^{*}\phi_{0}=\phi_{S}
. The parameter
\kappa
is chosen such that
\overline{\mathtt{J}_{S_{1}}(\mathcal{V}_{S_{1}})}\cap\overline{\mathtt{J}_{S_{2}}(\mathcal{V}_{S_{2}})}=\emptyset
for any two
S_{1}\neq S_{2}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Remark 2.3
.

From the classification of finite subgroups of
\textup{SU}(3)
(see
[
YY93
, Chapter 1]
and the references therein) follows that if
\Gamma_{S}<\textup{SU}(3)
acts freely on
\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
, then
\Gamma_{S}
is in fact abelian (see
[
YY93
, Theorem 23]
).

Definition 2.4

(cf.
[
Joy00
, Definition 11.4.1]
and
[
DPW23
, Definition 2.6]
)
.

Let
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
be a flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold satisfying
Section 2.1
. A set of resolution data consists for every
S\in\mathcal{S}
of the following:

1.

A fixed choice of
(\Gamma_{S},\Lambda_{S},\rho_{S},\mathtt{J}_{S})
as in
Section 2.1
.

2.

An asymptotically locally Euclidean Calabi–Yau manifold
(\hat{Z}_{S},\tau_{S},\hat{\omega}_{S},\hat{\Omega}_{S})
(also ALE Calabi–Yau manifold for short) asymptotic to
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S}
. That is, a resolution
\tau_{S}\colon\thinspace\hat{Z}_{S}\to\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S}
together with a Calabi–Yau structure
(\hat{\omega}_{S},\hat{\Omega}_{S})
on
\hat{Z}_{S}
that satisfies

\big|\nabla^{k}\big({\tau_{S}}_{*}\hat{\omega}_{S}-\omega_{0}\big)\big|=\mathcal{O}(r^{-6-k})\textup{ as $r\to\infty$}

(2.3)

where
\omega_{0}
denotes the flat Kähler structure on
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S}
. The norm and covariant derivatives are hereby taken with respect to the flat metric on
(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})/\Gamma_{S}
.

3.

A group action
\hat{\rho}_{S}\colon\thinspace\Lambda_{S}\to\textup{Isom}(\hat{Z}_{S},g_{\hat{\omega}_{S}})
which leaves
(\hat{\omega}_{S},\hat{\Omega}_{S})
invariant (in the sense of (
2.2
)) and makes
\tau_{S}
equivariant.

Remark 2.5
.

Regarding the previous definition:

1.

In the definition of an ALE-manifold one can relax the condition on
\tau_{S}
to be defined on the entire manifold
\hat{Z}_{S}
. In fact, it suffices that
\tau_{S}\colon\thinspace(\hat{Z}_{S}\setminus\hat{K}_{S})\to(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus B_{R_{S}}(0))/\Gamma_{S}
is a diffeomorphism defined outside a
\Lambda_{S}
-invariant compact set
\hat{K}_{S}\subset\hat{Z}_{S}
.

2.

The condition
\frac{1}{6}\hat{\omega}_{S}^{3}=\frac{i}{8}\hat{\Omega}_{S}\wedge\bar{\hat{\Omega}}_{S}
determines the holomorphic volume form
\hat{\Omega}_{S}
on
(\hat{Z}_{S},\hat{\omega}_{S})
only up to a constant
\theta\in S^{1}\subset\mathbb{C}
. Since
\tau_{S}\colon\thinspace\hat{Z}_{S}\to\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S}
is holomorphic, we have a holomorphic function
f\colon\thinspace\mathbb{C}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}
such that
(\tau_{S})_{*}\hat{\Omega}_{S}=f\Omega_{0}
(where
\Omega_{0}
is the standard holomorphic volume form
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}z_{1}\wedge\mathop{}\!\textup{d}z_{2}\wedge\mathop{}\!\textup{d}z_{3}
on
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S}
). The ALE-condition (
2.3
), the identity
\frac{1}{6}\hat{\omega}^{3}_{S}=\frac{i}{8}\hat{\Omega}_{S}\wedge\bar{\hat{\Omega}}_{S}
, and the maximum principle then imply that
f
is a constant function of norm 1. Thus, after modifying
\hat{\Omega}_{S}
, we can assume in the following that
(\tau_{S})_{*}\hat{\Omega}_{S}=\Omega_{0}
holds.

For a given orbifold
Y_{0}
, a set of resolution data, and a positive parameter
t>0
we define the following sets:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\kappa/8}\coloneqq\bigsqcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa/8}\quad

\displaystyle\textup{for}\quad\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa/8}\coloneqq(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa/8}(0)/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}\subset(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}\coloneqq\bigsqcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa}\quad

\displaystyle\textup{for}\quad\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa}\coloneqq(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0)/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}\subset(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa/8}^{t}\coloneqq\bigsqcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa/8}^{t}\quad

\displaystyle\textup{for}\quad\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa/8}^{t}\coloneqq(\mathbb{R}\times(t\tau_{S})^{-1}(B_{\kappa/8}(0)/\Gamma_{S}))/\Lambda_{S}\subset(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})/\Lambda_{S}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}^{t}\coloneqq\bigsqcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}\quad

\displaystyle\textup{for}\quad\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}\coloneqq(\mathbb{R}\times(t\tau_{S})^{-1}(B_{\kappa}(0)/\Gamma_{S}))/\Lambda_{S}\subset(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})/\Lambda_{S}

Denote by
\mathtt{J}\colon\thinspace\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}\to Y_{0}
and
t\tau\colon\thinspace\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}^{t}\to\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}
the maps induced by all
\{\mathtt{J}_{S}\}_{S\in\mathcal{S}}
and
\{t\tau_{S}\}_{S\in\mathcal{S}}
, respectively.

Definition 2.6

(
[
Joy96b
, Proof of Theorem 2.2.1]
)
.

Given a flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
and a set of resolution data, Joyce defines a 1-parameter family of smooth manifolds by

\hat{Y}_{t}\coloneqq\big(Y_{0}\setminus\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{\kappa/8})\big)\cup\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}^{t}/\sim

where
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}^{t}\setminus\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa/8}^{t}\ni y\sim\mathtt{J}(t\tau(y))\in\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathcal{V}_{\kappa/8})
.

Joyce (cf.
[
Joy96b
, Proof of Theorem 2.2.1]
) then equips
\hat{Y}_{t}
for sufficiently small
t
with a closed
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
which we explain next.

For
S\in\mathcal{S}
let
(\hat{Z}_{S},\tau_{S},\hat{\omega}_{S},\hat{\Omega}_{S})
be the ALE Calabi–Yau 3-fold used to resolve
S
. There exist
\sigma_{S}^{t}\in\Omega^{1}((\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})/\Gamma_{S})
with
|\nabla^{k}\sigma_{S}^{t}|=t^{6}\mathcal{O}(r^{-5-k})
such that
(t\tau_{S})_{*}(t^{2}\hat{\omega}_{S})-\omega_{0}=\mathop{}\!\textup{d}\sigma_{S}^{t}
(cf.
[
Joy00
, Theorem 8.2.3]
). Let
\tilde{\phi}_{t}\in\Omega^{3}(\hat{Y}_{t})
be defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{t}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\phi_{0}&\textup{ over $Y_{0}\setminus\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{\kappa})$}\\
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s\wedge\big(t^{2}\hat{\omega}_{S}-\mathop{}\!\textup{d}(\chi_{t}\cdot(t\tau_{S})^{*}\sigma_{S}^{t})\big)+\textup{Im}\thinspace t^{3}\hat{\Omega}_{S}&\textup{ over each $\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}$}\end{cases}

where
\chi_{t}\coloneqq\chi\circ(t\tau)
for a smooth non-decreasing function
\chi\colon\thinspace[0,\kappa]\to[0,1]
with

\displaystyle\chi(s)=\begin{cases}0\textup{ for $s\leq\kappa/4$}\\
1\textup{ for $s\geq\kappa/2$.}\end{cases}

Proposition 2.7

(
[
Joy96b
, Proof of Theorem 2.2.1]
)
.

Let
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
be a flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold satisfying
Section 2.1
and let
\mathcal{R}
be a set of resolution data. Denote by
(\hat{Y}_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t})
be the smooth manifold and
3
-form constructed above. Then there exists a positive constant
T_{pK}=T_{pK}(Y_{0},\phi_{0},\mathcal{R})
such that
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
defines a closed
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure on
\hat{Y}_{t}
for
t<T_{pK}
.

Joyce then perturbs the
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
to a torsion-free
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure
\phi_{t}
. In order to state a (slightly enhanced) version of his result we need to introduce the following norms:

First, let
r_{t}\colon\thinspace\hat{Y}_{t}\to[0,\kappa]
be the function defined by

\displaystyle r_{t}(y)\coloneqq\begin{cases}|t\tau(z)|&\textup{ if $y=[s,z]\in\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}^{t}$}\\
\kappa&\textup{ if $y\in Y_{0}\setminus\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{\kappa})$}\end{cases}

For
t\in(0,T_{pK})
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
, a weight exponent
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, and a domain
U\subset\hat{Y}_{t}
we define (as in
[
Wal13a
, Section 6]
) the following weighted Hölder norms:

\displaystyle[f]_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{(U)}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{x,y,\in U,\ d(x,y)\leq w_{t}(x,y)}w_{t}(x,y)^{\alpha-\beta}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|}{d(x,y)^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{{0}}_{{\beta},t}{(U)}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big\|w_{t}^{-\beta}f\big\|_{C^{{0}}{(U)}}

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{(U)}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left\|\nabla^{j}f\right\|_{C^{{0}}_{{\beta-j},t}{(U)}}+\left[\nabla^{j}f\right]_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta-j},t}{(U)}}

where

w_{t}(y)\coloneqq t+r_{t}(y)\quad\text{and}\quad w_{t}(x,y)\coloneqq\min\{w_{t}(x),w_{t}(y)\}.

Whenever
f
is a function, then all derivatives, inner products, and distances in the definition of the above norms are taken with respect to
\tilde{g}_{t}
(induced from the
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
) and its associated Levi–Civita connection. If
f
is a tensor field (or, more generally, a section of a vector bundle equipped with an inner-product and a metric connection), then we use parallel transport to compare two values of
f
lying in different fibers. If we do not specify a domain, then we consider
U=\hat{Y}_{t}
.

These norms satisfy (cf.
[
Wal13a
, Equation (6.5) and (6.6)]

\|f\cdot g\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{(U)}}\leq\|f\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta_{1}},t}{(U)}}\cdot\|g\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta_{2}},t}{(U)}}

(2.4)

for
\beta=\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}
and

(T_{pK}+\kappa)^{\beta_{1}-\beta_{3}}\|f\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta_{1}},t}{(U)}}\leq\|f\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta_{3}},t}{(U)}}\leq t^{\beta_{2}-\beta_{3}}\|f\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta_{2}},t}{(U)}}

(2.5)

for
\beta_{1}\leq\beta_{2}\leq\beta_{3}
.

The following existence theorem was first proven by Joyce in
[
Joy96a
]
. Its enhancement to
C^{k,\alpha}_{0,t}
-norms comes from
[
Wal13a
, Proposition 4.20 and Remark 4.26]
.
4
4

4

[
Wal13a
, Proposition 4.20 and Remark 4.26]
are formulated for
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifolds whose singular strata are of codimension
4
. However, the same proof also holds for orbifolds satisfying
Section 2.1
.

Theorem 2.8

(
[
Joy96b
]
[Theorem 2.2.1],
[
Joy96a
, Theorem A]
, and
[
Wal13a
, Proposition 4.20 and Remark 4.26]
)
.

Let
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
be a compact and flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold satisfying
Section 2.1
and let
\mathcal{R}
be a set of resolution data. Furthermore, let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then there are positive constants
T_{K}=T_{K}(Y_{0},\phi_{0},\mathcal{R},\alpha,k)
with
T_{K}\leq T_{pK}
and
c_{K}=c_{K}(Y_{0},\phi_{0},\mathcal{R},\alpha,k)
such that for any
t\in(0,T_{K})
there exists a torsion-free
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure
\phi_{t}
on
\hat{Y}_{t}
with

\|\phi_{t}-\tilde{\phi}_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{0},t}{}}<c_{K}t^{1/2}.

Remark 2.9
.

Going through the proofs of
[
Joy96b
, Theorem 2.2.1]
and
[
Joy96a
, Theorem A]
one can check that the factor of
t^{1/2}
in the estimate of the previous theorem can be improved to
t^{5/2}
. This is due to the observation that whenever
Y_{0}
has only codimension
6
singularities, then the exponents inside condition i) of
[
Joy96a
, Theorem A]
can be improved to
t^{6}
(cf.
[
Joy00
, Section 11.5.5]
).

Remark 2.10

(Equivariant generalised Kummer construction (cf.
[
DPW23
, Remark 2.20]
)
.

Let
H
be a finite group and
\lambda_{0}\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(Y_{0},g_{\phi_{0}})
be a group action that fixes
\psi_{0}\coloneqq*\phi_{0}
. An
H
-equivariant resolution data is a resolution data with the additional property that for every
h\in H
and
S\in\mathcal{S}
:

1.

We have

\Gamma_{hS}=\Gamma_{S},\quad\Lambda_{hS}=\Lambda_{S},\quad\text{and}\quad\rho_{hS}=\rho_{S}.

Furthermore, we demand that for every
[S]\in\mathcal{S}/H
there is a homomorphism
\lambda_{[S]}\colon\thinspace H\to\big(N_{(\textup{O}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\mathbb{R})\times(N_{\textup{O}(\mathbb{C}^{3})}(\Gamma_{S})/\Gamma_{S})}(\Lambda_{S})\big)/\Lambda_{S}
that satisfies

\lambda_{0}(h)\circ\mathtt{J}_{S}=\mathtt{J}_{hS}\circ[\lambda_{[S]}(h)].

Here
[\lambda_{[S]}(h)]
denotes the induced map on
\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa}=\mathcal{V}_{hS,\kappa}
.

2.

The resolving ALE spaces also satisfy
(\hat{Z}_{S},\tau_{S},\hat{\omega}_{S},\hat{\Omega}_{S})=(\hat{Z}_{hS},\tau_{hS},\hat{\omega}_{hS},\hat{\Omega}_{hS})
.

3.

We have a lift
\hat{\lambda}_{[S]}\colon\thinspace H\to\big(N_{(\textup{O}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\mathbb{R)\times}\textup{Isom}(\hat{Z}_{[S]},\hat{g}_{[S]})}(\Lambda_{[S]})\big)/\Lambda_{[S]}
of
\lambda_{[S]}
along
\tau_{[S]}
such that for every
h\in H

[\hat{\lambda}_{[S]}(h)]^{*}\psi_{[S]}=\psi_{[S]}

(where
\psi_{[S]}
is the coassociative 4-form associated to the Calabi–Yau structure
(\hat{\omega}_{[S]},\hat{\Omega}_{[S]})
as in
Section 2.1
).

In this case we obtain an induced action
\hat{\lambda}\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Diff}(\hat{Y}_{t})
and can choose
\sigma_{[S]}^{t}
in the pre-gluing construction to be
H
-invariant. This implies that
\hat{\lambda}(h)^{*}\tilde{\psi}_{t}=\tilde{\psi}_{t}
and therefore
\hat{\lambda}(h)^{*}\tilde{g}_{t}=\tilde{g}_{t}
(cf. the following remark) for every
h\in H
. The Kummer construction can then be performed
H
-equivariantly (see for example
[
Joy96b
, Section 4.2]
) and
\psi_{t}=*_{\phi_{t}}\phi_{t}
associated to the torsion-free
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure of
Section 2.1
is also invariant under
H
.

Remark 2.11
.

Let
\phi_{0}\in\Lambda^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{7})^{*}
be the model
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure over
\mathbb{R}^{7}
(obtained by choosing
(Z,\omega,\Omega)=(\mathbb{C}^{3},\omega_{0},\Omega_{0})
in
Section 2.1
) and
\psi_{0}=*\phi_{0}\in\Lambda^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{7})^{*}
its coassociative 4-form. It is well known that the stabiliser of
\psi_{0}
in
\textup{GL}(\mathbb{R}^{7})
equals

\textup{Stab}_{\textup{GL}(\mathbb{R}^{7})}(\psi_{0})=\textup{G}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}<\textup{O}(\mathbb{R}^{7})

where
(R,\pm 1)\in\textup{G}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
corresponds to the isomorphism
\pm R\in\textup{GL}(\mathbb{R}^{7})
(see for example
[
Gut24a
, Proposition 2.1.3]
for an elementary proof of this fact). Thus, demanding that the group action
\lambda_{0}\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(Y_{0},g_{\phi_{0}})
in the previous remark fixes
\psi_{0}
is slightly weaker than demanding it to preserve
\phi_{0}
.

2.2
ALE Calabi–Yau structures on crepant resolutions

As discussed in the previous section, the generalised Kummer construction requires ALE Calabi–Yau 3-folds resolving orbifolds of the form
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma
where
\Gamma<\textup{SU}(3)
is a finite group acting freely on
\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
(see
Point 2
of
Section 2.1
). This section summarises the construction of such resolutions of
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma
via Kähler reduction as carried out in
[
Inf96
]
and the existence of ALE Calabi–Yau structures on them as proven in
[
DW16
, Section 4]
via Joyce’s proof of the ALE Calabi conjecture
[
Joy00
, Theorem 8.2.3]
. We then give a condition under which certain group actions on
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma
lift to the resolving ALE space (as required in
Point 3
of
Section 2.1
). All the material below except
Point 6
(which is analogous to the case of ALE hyperkähler 4-manifolds) is contained in
[
DW16
, Sections 2,3,4]
and its presentation is similar to the one given for ALE hyperkähler
4
-manifolds in
[
DPW23
, Remark 2.15]
and
[
Gut24b
, Section 2.2.2]
.

1.

Let
\Gamma<\textup{SU}(3)
be a finite group acting freely on
\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
and denote by
\mathbb{C}[\Gamma]\coloneqq\textup{Maps}(\Gamma,\mathbb{C})
the regular representation of
\Gamma
equipped with its standard Hermitian inner product. Furthermore, define
G\coloneqq\mathbb{P}\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])^{\Gamma}
and

N\coloneqq\left\{B\in\big(\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\textup{End}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])\big)^{\Gamma}\mid[B\wedge B]=0\in\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes\textup{End}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])\right\}

where
[\cdot\wedge\cdot]
is the simultaneous wedge-product
\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{3}\to\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{C}^{3}
and commutator
\textup{End}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])\otimes\textup{End}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])\to\textup{End}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])
. Equip
(\mathbb{C}\otimes\textup{End}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma]))^{\Gamma}
with the canonical (flat) Kähler structure and
N
with the induced Kähler structure as a subvariety.

2.

The adjoint action of
G
on
(\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes\textup{End}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma]))^{\Gamma}
is Hamiltonian and has a distinguished moment map (written down in
[
DW16
, Proposition 3.1]
)

\mu\colon\thinspace(\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes\textup{End}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma]))^{\Gamma}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}.

[
Inf96
, Section 4.2]
proves that the Kähler quotient
(N\cap\mu^{-1}(0))/G
is isometric to
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma
.

3.

Let
\mathfrak{z}^{*}
be the annihilator of
[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}]
in
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, i.e. all elements in
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
fixed by the coadjoint action of
G
. An element
\zeta\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}
is called

\textit{generic}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\textup{$\zeta(i\pi_{R})\neq 0$ for every non-trivial proper subrepresentation $R\subset\mathbb{C}[\Gamma]$}

where
\pi_{R}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{C}[\Gamma]\to R
is the orthogonal projection onto
R
.
5
5

5

Note that the center of
\mathfrak{u}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])^{\Gamma}
is spanned by
\{i\pi_{R}\mid\textup{for $R\subset\mathbb{C}[\Gamma]$ irreducible subrepresentation}\}
(since by
Section 2.1

\Gamma
is abelian, all irreducible representations are 1-dimensional). This implies that set of generic elements in
\mathfrak{z}^{*}
is given by the complement of a finite union of linear codimension 1 subspaces (cf.
[
DW16
, Section 2,3]
). The set of generic elements is therefore open and dense.
Furthermore, we define
\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}
to be the set of linear maps
\zeta\colon\thinspace\mathfrak{g}\to\mathbb{R}
with
\zeta(i\pi_{R})\in\mathbb{Q}
for every subrepresentation
R\subset\mathbb{C}[\Gamma]
.

4.

If
\zeta\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}
is generic, then the Kähler quotient

\hat{Z}_{\zeta}\coloneqq\big(N\cap\mu^{-1}(\zeta)\big)/G

is a smooth Kähler manifold underlying a crepant resolution
\tau_{\zeta}\colon\thinspace\hat{Z}_{\zeta}\to\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma
(see
[
BKR01
, Theorem 1.1]
,
[
CI04
, Theorem 2.5]
, and
[
DW16
, Proposition 3.6]
).
6
6

6

Moreover,
[
Yam25
, Section 2.1]
argues that this result also holds true for generic
\zeta\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}
that do not lie in
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
.
Conversely,
[
CI04
, Theorem 1.1]
proves that every projective crepant resolution is biholomorphic to
\hat{Z}_{\zeta}
for some generic
\zeta\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
[
Inf96
, Theorem 5.1]
shows that the induced Kähler structure
\tilde{\omega}_{\zeta}
on
\hat{Z}_{\zeta}
is ALE of rate (at least)
4
(i.e. it satisfies the conditions of
Point 2
of
Section 2.1
with exponents
r^{-4-k}
).

5.

Using Joyce’s proof of the ALE Calabi conjecture
[
Joy00
, Theorem 8.2.3]
,
[
DW16
, Theorem 4.1]
shows that there exists a unique Ricci-flat ALE Kähler structure
\hat{\omega}_{\zeta}
on
\hat{Z}_{\zeta}
in the Kähler class of
\tilde{\omega}_{\zeta}
. This
\hat{\omega}_{\zeta}
is ALE of order
6
.

6.

Let
U
be an element in the normalizer
N_{\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}^{3})}(\Gamma)
. Then
U
acts on
\Gamma
by conjugation and we extend this action to a complex linear map
\textup{conj}_{U}\in\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])
. The standard representation of
U
on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
tensored with the adjoint action of
\textup{conj}_{U}
on
\textup{End}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])
induces an action on
(\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes\textup{End}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma]))^{\Gamma}
which restricts to
N
. The moment map satisfies

\mu\circ(U\otimes\textup{Ad}_{\textup{conj}_{U}})=\textup{Ad}_{\textup{conj}_{U}^{-1}}^{*}\circ\mu.

Thus, if
\textup{Ad}^{*}_{\textup{conj}_{U}^{-1}}\zeta=\zeta
, we obtain a holomorphic isometry
\hat{U}\in\textup{Isom}(\hat{Z}_{\zeta},\tilde{\omega}_{\zeta})
satisfying
\tau_{\zeta}\circ\hat{U}=U\circ\tau_{\zeta}
. By the uniqueness of the Ricci-flat ALE metric inside the same Kähler class as
\tilde{\omega}_{\zeta}
, we also obtain
\hat{U}^{*}\hat{\omega}_{\zeta}=\hat{\omega}_{\zeta}
.

2.3
HYM connections over ALE Calabi–Yau 3-folds

In
Section 4
we construct approximate
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons over resolutions of
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifolds satisfying
Section 2.1
. One ingredient of this construction are asymptotically flat Hermitian Yang–Mills connections over ALE
3
-folds whose theory we review in this section.

Let
(\hat{Z},\tau,\hat{\omega},\hat{\Omega})
be an ALE Calabi–Yau
3
-fold asymptotic to
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma
as defined in
Point 2
of
Section 2.1
. Furthermore, let
G
be a Lie group whose Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
has been equipped with an
Ad
-invariant inner product and
\pi\colon\thinspace\hat{P}\to\hat{Z}
be a principal
G
-bundle. We denote the space of connections on
\hat{P}
by
\mathcal{A}(\hat{P})
and for any
A\in\mathcal{A}(P)
its associated connection
1
-form by
\theta_{A}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{P},\mathfrak{g})^{G}
. For the following discussion we fix

•

a local framing at infinity, i.e. a principal
G
-bundle
\pi_{\infty}\colon\thinspace P_{\infty}\to(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})/\Gamma
together with a bundle isomorphism
\tilde{\tau}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{|\hat{Z}\setminus\tau^{-1}(0)}\to P_{\infty}
covering
\tau
, and

•

a flat connection
A_{\infty}
on
P_{\infty}
.

Definition 2.12
.

For
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
we denote by
\mathcal{A}_{\beta}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
the space of connections
A\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{P})
that satisfy

|\nabla^{k}(\tilde{\tau}_{*}\theta_{A}-\theta_{A_{\infty}})|=\mathcal{O}(r^{\beta-k})\textup{ as $r\to\infty$.}

The covariant derivative is hereby induced by
A_{\infty}
and the Levi–Civita connection associated to the flat metric on
(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})/\Gamma
. Similarly, the norm is induced by the flat metric and the
Ad
-invariant inner product on
\mathfrak{g}
.

Similarly, for any associated vector bundle
\hat{P}\times_{G}V
(where
V
is a linear
G
-module) we define
\Omega^{k}_{\beta}(\hat{Z},\hat{P}\times_{G}V)
as the space of
\omega\in\Omega^{k}(\hat{Z},\hat{P}\times_{G}V)
with

|\nabla^{k}\tilde{\tau}_{*}\omega|=\mathcal{O}(r^{\beta-k})\textup{ as $r\to\infty$.}

Definition 2.13
.

A connection
A\in\mathcal{A}_{\beta}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
is called
Hermitian Yang–Mills asymptotic to
A_{\infty}
of order
\beta
if

\Lambda_{\hat{\omega}}F_{A}=0\qquad\textup{and}\qquad F_{A}^{0,2}=0

where
\Lambda_{\hat{\omega}}
is the dual Lefschetz operator associated to
\hat{\omega}
. The set of hermitian Yang–Mills connections asymptotic to
A_{\infty}
of order
\beta
is denoted by
\mathcal{A}^{\textup{HYM}}_{\beta}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
.

Remark 2.14
.

If no order is specified we will always assume
\beta=-5
.

Remark 2.15
.

In the following we tacitly assume that
G
is a
real
Lie group. In the above definition we therefore complexify the adjoint bundle in order to project to
F_{A}^{0,2}
. An equivalent condition in this case (with the benefit of not requiring the extra step of complexification) is (cf.
[
Huy05
, Proposition 1.2.30]
)

\hat{\omega}\wedge\hat{\omega}\wedge F_{A}=0\qquad\textup{and}\qquad\textup{Re}\thinspace\hat{\Omega}\wedge F_{A}=0.

In the following, we denote by
\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}^{\mathbb{C}}\coloneqq\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}
the complexified adjoint bundle.

Proposition 2.16
.

Assume that
A\in\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\textup{HYM}}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
for some
\beta\in(-5,-1)
. Then there exists a

u\in\mathcal{G}_{0,\beta+1}\coloneqq\{u\in\mathcal{G}\mid|\nabla^{k}(\tilde{\tau}_{*}u-\textup{Id})|=\mathcal{O}(r^{\beta+1-k}))\}

such that
u^{*}A\in\mathcal{A}_{-5}^{\textup{HYM}}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
. Here,
\mathcal{G}
denotes the group of bundle automorphisms of
\hat{P}
that cover the identity. Furthermore, we assumed (for simplicity) that
G
is a subgroup of
\textup{GL}(V)
for a vector space
V
(and
\mathcal{G}
can therefore be regarded as a subset of the linear bundle
\textup{End}(\hat{P}\times_{G}V)
) in order to make sense of the difference
\tilde{\tau}_{*}u-\textup{id}
.

Remark 2.17
.

We made the assumption
\beta<-1
in order to ensure that the based gauge group
\mathcal{G}_{0,\beta+1}
acts on
\mathcal{A}_{\beta}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
. This is used in the proof when the connection is put in Coulomb gauge.

A similar result was stated without proof in
[
DW16
, Remark 4.8]
. We outline a proof for the convenience of the reader.

Proof.

Denote by
\mathcal{A}_{\textup{cpt}}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
the set of connections which are equal to
A_{\infty}
outside a compact set. There exists a
B\in\mathcal{A}_{\textup{cpt}}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
which can be put in Coulomb gauge relative to
A
.
7
7

7

This can be proven as
[
FU84
, Theorem 3.2]
by using the results of
[
Bar86
, Section 1 and 2]
; cf.
[
Nak90
, Proposition 2.3]
. When working with weighted Sobolev norms, one has to slightly enlarge
\beta<\beta^{\prime}<-1
. A similar argument as in the current proof then improves the decay of the gauge transformation
u\in\mathcal{G}_{0,\beta^{\prime}+1}
to
\beta+1
That is, there exists a
u\in\mathcal{G}_{0,\beta+1}
such that
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}^{*}((u^{-1})^{*}\theta_{B}-\theta_{A})=0
, or, equivalently,
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{B}^{*}(u^{*}\theta_{A}-\theta_{B})=0
.

Next, we write
u^{*}\theta_{A}-\theta_{B}\eqqcolon\alpha+\bar{\alpha}
with
\alpha\in\Omega^{0,1}_{\beta}(\hat{Z},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}^{\mathbb{C}})
. Together with the gauge-fixing, the Hermitian Yang–Mills equation on
A=B+(\alpha+\bar{\alpha})
becomes

\displaystyle 2\bar{\partial}_{B}\alpha

\displaystyle=-2F_{B}^{0,2}-[\alpha\wedge\alpha]

\displaystyle 2\bar{\partial}_{B}^{*}\alpha

\displaystyle=2\textup{Re}\thinspace(\bar{\partial}_{B}^{*}\alpha)+2i\textup{Im}\thinspace(\bar{\partial}^{*}_{B}\alpha)+i\Lambda_{\hat{\omega}}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{B}(\alpha+\overline{\alpha})-i\Lambda_{\hat{\omega}}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{B}(\alpha+\bar{\alpha})

\displaystyle=2i\textup{Im}\thinspace(\bar{\partial}^{*}_{B}\alpha)+i\Lambda_{\hat{\omega}}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{B}(\alpha+\overline{\alpha})+i\Lambda_{\hat{\omega}}F_{B}+i\Lambda_{\hat{\omega}}[\alpha\wedge\bar{\alpha}].

Outside of a compact subset of
\hat{Z}
the connection
B
agrees with
A_{\infty}
and the twisted Kähler identites
[
Huy05
, Lemma 5.2.3]
imply therefore that

\Lambda_{\hat{\omega}}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{B}(\alpha+\bar{\alpha})=\Lambda_{\hat{\omega}}\partial_{B}\alpha+\Lambda\bar{\partial}_{B}\bar{\alpha}=-2\textup{Im}\thinspace(\bar{\partial}^{*}_{B}\alpha)

holds outside of this subset. This shows that we can write the equations above schematically as
D\alpha=f+Q(\alpha)
where
D=2(\bar{\partial}_{B}+\bar{\partial}_{B}^{*})
,
f
has compact support, and
|\nabla^{k}Q(\alpha)|=\mathcal{O}(r^{2\beta-k})
. Thus,
D\alpha
has more decay than expected (this again uses
\beta<-1
). Up to a constant,
D
is a twisted Dirac operator and its critical rates (which correspond to the eigenvalues of the Dirac operator on
S^{5}/\Gamma
shifted by
-\frac{5}{2}
; cf.
[
DW16
, Proof of Theorem 6.1]
) do not intersect the interval
(-5,0)
(cf.
[
Bar86
, Proposition 6.1]
). This implies that the decay of
\alpha
can iteratively be improved until
\beta=-5
(cf.
[
Bar86
, Theorem 1.14]
,
[
Bar86
, Theorem 1.17]
, and
[
LM85
, Section I.5]
).
∎

Definition 2.18
.

For
A\in\mathcal{A}^{\textup{HYM}}_{\beta}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
we define the space of infinitesimal deformations as

\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A,\beta}\coloneqq\left\{\alpha\in\Omega^{0,1}_{\beta}(Z,\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}^{\mathbb{C}})\mid(\bar{\partial}_{A}+\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*})\alpha=0\right\}.

The connection is called
infinitesimally rigid
if
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A,\beta}=0
.

The following proposition can be proven as
Section 2.3
:

Proposition 2.19

(
[
DW16
, Proposition 5.5]
)
.

Let
A\in\mathcal{A}^{\textup{HYM}}_{\beta}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
for
\beta<0
. Then every
\alpha\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A,\beta}
lies in
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A,-5}
. (In fact, any
\alpha\in\ker(\bar{\partial}_{A}+\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*})\cap\Omega^{0,1}(\hat{Z},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}^{\mathbb{C}})
that satisfies
\lim_{r\to\infty}|\alpha|=0
lies in
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A,-5}
.)

Lemma 2.20
.

Let
\beta<0
and
A\in\mathcal{A}^{\textup{HYM}}_{\beta}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
and assume that either

\eta\in\Omega^{0,0}_{\beta}(\hat{Z},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}^{\mathbb{C}})\quad\textup{satisfies}\quad\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\bar{\partial}_{A}\eta=0

or

\eta\in\Omega^{0,3}_{\beta}(\hat{Z},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}^{\mathbb{C}})\quad\textup{satisfies}\quad\bar{\partial}_{A}\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\eta=0.

Then
\eta=0
.

This result is for example contained in
[
DW16
, Proof of Proposition 6.4]
. As the argument is short, we have included a (slightly simplified) version of the proof.

Proof.

Since
g_{\hat{\omega}}
is Ricci-flat and
A
is Hermitian Yang–Mills, the Weitzenböck formula (see for example
[
Oli14
, Corollary 1.1.4]
) implies that

\nabla_{A}^{*}\nabla_{A}\eta=2\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\bar{\partial}_{A}\eta=0\quad\textup{if $\eta\in\Omega^{0,0}_{\beta}(\hat{Z},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}^{\mathbb{C}})$}

or

\nabla_{A}^{*}\nabla_{A}\eta=2\bar{\partial}_{A}\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\eta=0\quad\textup{if $\eta\in\Omega^{0,3}_{\beta}(\hat{Z},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}^{\mathbb{C}})$}.

Together with the Bochner formula this leads to

\Delta|\eta|^{2}=2\langle\nabla_{A}^{*}\nabla_{A}\eta,\eta\rangle-2|\nabla_{A}\eta|^{2}\leq 0.

The function
|\eta|^{2}
is therefore subharmonic and decays to zero at infinity. Thus, by the maximum principle,
|\eta|^{2}=0
.
∎

Let now
\Gamma<\textup{SU}(3)
be a fixed finite subgroup acting freely on
\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
and let
\zeta\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}\cap(\mathfrak{pu}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])^{\Gamma})^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}
be generic as defined in
Section 2.2
. Let
(\hat{Z}_{\zeta},\tau_{\zeta},\hat{\omega}_{\zeta},\hat{\Omega}_{\zeta})
be the corresponding ALE Calabi–Yau manifold asymptotic to
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma
as described in
Section 2.2
. The following proposition follows from the work
in
[
DW16
]
and
Section 2.3
(or
[
DW16
, Remark 4.8]
). The last statement about the monodromy of the asymptotic connection can be proven as
[
KN90
, Proposition 2.2 (ii)]
.

Proposition 2.21

(
[
DW16
, Theorem 1.1]
and
[
DW16
, Proof of Lemma 5.1]
)
.

For every unitary representation
\nu\colon\thinspace\Gamma\to\textup{U}(n)
there exists a locally framed
\textup{U}(n)
-bundle
\pi_{\nu}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{\nu}\to\hat{Z}_{\zeta}
together with an infinitesimally rigid HYM connection
A_{\nu}\in\mathcal{A}_{-5}^{\textup{HYM}}(P_{\nu},A_{\infty})
asymptotic to the flat connection
A_{\infty}
over
(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})/\Gamma
whose monodromy corresponds to
\nu
.

Remark 2.22
.

Since
\Gamma<\textup{SU}(3)
is abelian by
Section 2.1
, the locally framed
\textup{U}(n)
-bundles and connections in the previous proposition decompose into a direct sum of
\textup{U}(1)
-bundles and connections.

Recall from
Section 2.2

Point 6
that for every
U\in N_{\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}^{3})}(\Gamma)
with
\textup{Ad}_{\textup{conj}_{U}^{-1}}^{*}\zeta=\zeta
there exists a holomorphic lift
\hat{U}
to
\hat{Z}_{\zeta}
preserving the Calabi–Yau metric. This implies, in particular, that for every element
U\in C_{\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}^{3})}(\Gamma)
in the centraliser of
\Gamma
we obtain such a lift
\hat{U}
(because conjugation by
U
induces the trivial action on
\mathbb{C}[\Gamma]
; i.e.
\textup{conj}_{U}=1
in the notation of the aforementioned paragraph). The following can be deduced from
[
DW16
, Sections 3 and 4]
and
Section 2.3
(note for this that the gauge transformation in
Section 2.3
can be chosen invariantly):

Lemma 2.23
.

Let
\nu\colon\thinspace\Gamma\to\textup{U}(n)
be a representation and
U\in C_{\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}^{3})}(\Gamma)
be an element of the centraliser of
\Gamma
. Denote by
\hat{U}
the induced isometry on
\hat{Z}_{\zeta}
. There exists a lift of
\hat{U}
to a bundle isomorphism of
\hat{P}_{\nu}
which preserves the HYM connection
A_{\nu}
.

Remark 2.24
.

This remark explains the bundle isomorphism of the previous lemma and, in particular, the condition that
U\in C_{\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}^{3})}(\Gamma)
in more detail. Recall from
Section 2.2
that
\hat{Z}_{\zeta}
is constructed via Kähler reduction

\hat{Z}_{\zeta}=(N\cap\mu^{-1}(\zeta))/\mathbb{P}\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])^{\Gamma}.

Similarly, the
\textup{U}(n)
-bundle of
Section 2.3
is constructed in
[
DW16
, Equation (3.4)]
as the associated bundle

\hat{P}_{\nu}\coloneqq(N\cap\mu^{-1}(\zeta))\times_{\mathbb{P}\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])^{\Gamma}}\textup{U}(n)

(where the homomorphism
\mathbb{P}\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])^{\Gamma}\to\textup{U}(n)
is in a certain way induced by the representation
\nu
). If
U\in C_{\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}^{3})}(\Gamma)
, then the induced map
\textup{conj}_{U}\in\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])
is trivial. In this case, the action of
U
on
N
described in
Point 6
of
Section 2.2
commutes with the action of
\mathbb{P}\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}[\Gamma])^{\Gamma}
(and restricts to
N\cap\mu^{-1}(\zeta)
). This induces the action on
\hat{P}_{\nu}
.

In
Section 6
we use the Hermitian Yang–Mills connections of
Section 2.3
to construct
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons on a certain
\textup{SO}(14)
-bundle. For this, we consider the connections of
Section 2.3
as
\textup{SO}(2n)
-connections (induced by the inclusion
\textup{U}(n)<\textup{SO}(2n)
). The following proposition ensures that these connections are still infinitesimally rigid (and therefore satisfy
Section 5.2
) when considered as
\textup{SO}(2n)
-connections.

Proposition 2.25
.

For a unitary representation
\nu\colon\thinspace\Gamma\to\textup{U}(n)
let
(\pi_{\nu}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{\nu}\to\hat{Z}_{\zeta},A_{\nu})
be as in
Section 2.3
. Furthermore, denote by
\hat{E}_{\nu}
the unitary vector bundle associated to
\hat{P}_{\nu}
. For any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we define
\pi\colon\thinspace\textup{SO}(\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{k}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu})\to\hat{Z}
to be the
\textup{SO}(2n+k)
-bundle associated to the Euclidean vector bundle
\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{k}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu}
. This comes equipped with a canonical local framing and an Hermitian Yang–Mills connection
A
(induced by
A_{\nu}
and the trivial connection). This connection is infinitesimally rigid and asymptotic to the flat connection associated to the (real) representation
\nu_{\textup{triv}}^{\oplus k}\oplus\nu
.

A similar statement and proof for ALE hyperkähler 4-manifolds appeared in
[
GPTW23
, Proof of Proposition 7.8]
and
[
Wal13a
, Proposition 5.22]
.

Proof.

We only prove the infinitesimal rigidity of
A
. For this note that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{so}\big(\underline{\mathbb{R}^{k}}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu}\big)^{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\cong\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{C}}\big(\underline{\mathbb{C}^{k}}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu}^{\mathbb{C}}\big)\cong\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{C}}\big(\underline{\mathbb{C}^{k}}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu}^{*}\big)\cong\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{C}}\big(\underline{\mathbb{C}^{k}}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu^{*}}\big)

and that
A
is mapped to the connection on the right-hand bundle that is induced by the trivial connection on
\underline{\mathbb{C}^{k}}
and
A_{\nu}\oplus A_{\nu^{*}}
on
\hat{E}_{\nu}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu^{*}}
. Furthermore, this bundle is a parallel subbundle of
\textup{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(\underline{\mathbb{C}^{k}}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu^{*}}\big)=\mathfrak{u}\big(\underline{\mathbb{C}^{k}}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu}\oplus\hat{E}_{\nu^{*}}\big)^{\mathbb{C}}
. By
Section 2.3
,
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A_{\textup{triv}}\oplus A_{\nu}\oplus A_{\nu^{*}},-5}=0
and therefore also
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A,-5}=0
.
∎

3
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons over generalised Kummer constructions

The first part of this section reviews the theory of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons over generalised Kummer constructions relevant for the rest of this article. In particular, we discuss the (non-linear) instanton map and prove an estimate on its quadratic part. We then give in
Section 3.2
sufficient conditions under which a family of connections over a generalised Kummer construction may be perturbed to a family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons. Furthermore, we prove estimates on the derivatives of this family and show under which hypothesis the instantons in the family are infinitesimally irreducible.

3.1
Gauge theory on generalised Kummer constructions

Let
(Y,\phi)
be a
7
-manifold together with a coclosed
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure and
\pi\colon\thinspace P\to Y
a principal
G
-bundle where
G
is a Lie group whose Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
has been equipped with an
Ad
-invariant inner product.

Definition 3.1

(cf.
[
Wal13a
, Proposition 3.7]
)
.

A connection
A\in\mathcal{A}(P)
is called a
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton if
F_{A}\wedge\psi=0
where
\psi=*\phi
.

The following two examples are well-known.

Example 3.2
.

Any flat connection over
Y
is a
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton.

Example 3.3
.

Let
(Z,\omega,\Omega)
be a Calabi–Yau
3
-fold and
\pi\colon\thinspace P\to Z
be a principal bundle over
Z
. Furthermore, let
A\in\mathcal{A}(P)
be a Hermitian Yang–Mills connection as defined in
Section 2.3
. Its pullback to
\mathbb{R}\times Z
is a
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton when
\mathbb{R}\times Z
is equipped with the
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure described in
Section 2.1
. This follows immediately from
Section 2.3
.

Proposition 3.4

(
[
Wal13a
, Proposition 3.7]
)
.

If
Y
is compact, then a connection
A\in\mathcal{A}(P)
is a
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton if and only if there exists a
\xi\in\Omega^{7}(Y,\mathfrak{g}_{P})
such that

F_{A}\wedge\psi-\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}^{*}\xi=0.

(3.1)

Since the proof is rather short, we have included it here in order to be self-contained.

Proof.

If
A\in\mathcal{A}(P)
is a
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton, then it satisfies (
3.1
) with
\xi=0
. Conversely, assume that
A
and
\xi
satisfy (
3.1
). The Bianchi-identity and closedness of
\psi
imply

-\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}^{*}\xi=\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}\big(F_{A}\wedge\psi-\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}^{*}\xi)\big)=0.

Thus,
\xi
is harmonic and therefore
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}^{*}\xi=0
because
Y
is closed.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

The advantage of the augmented equation (
3.1
) is that its linearisation (together with the Coulomb gauge condition) is elliptic.

Throughout the rest of this section we denote by
(\hat{Y}_{t},\phi_{t})
for
t\in(0,T_{K})
a
1
-parameter family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifolds obtained from Joyce’s generalised Kummer construction (as specified in
Section 2.1
and
Section 2.1
). Furthermore, let
\pi\colon\thinspace P\to\hat{Y}_{t}
be a principal
G
-bundle.
8
8

8

Since the diffeomorphism type of
\hat{Y}_{t}
does not change, we can assume that
\pi\colon\thinspace P\to\hat{Y}_{t}
is (up to isomorphism) defined for all
t\in(0,T_{K})
.
For any
A\in\mathcal{A}(P)
we define

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{A}\colon\thinspace\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{7}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})

\displaystyle\to\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})

\displaystyle(a,\xi)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(F_{A+a}\wedge\psi_{t}-\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A+a}^{\tilde{*}_{t}}\xi,\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}^{\tilde{*}_{t}}a\big)

where we have written
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}^{\tilde{*}_{t}}
to emphasise that the formal adjoints are taken with respect to the metric and orientation induced by
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
(the closed but not torsion-free
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure on
\hat{Y}_{t}
as described below
Section 2.1
). In the following, we will omit this explicit dependence on
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
from our notation.
\Upsilon_{A}
can be written as

\Upsilon_{A}(a,\xi)=\Upsilon_{A}(0)+L_{A}(a,\xi)+Q_{A}(a,\xi)

for

\displaystyle L_{A}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{t}\wedge\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}&-\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}^{*}\\
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}^{*}&0\end{pmatrix}\quad\textup{and}\quad Q_{A}(a,\xi)\coloneqq\left(\tfrac{1}{2}[a\wedge a]\wedge\psi_{t}+[i_{a^{\sharp}},\xi],0\right)

where
[i_{a^{\sharp}},\xi]
denotes insertion on the level of forms and the Lie bracket on
\mathfrak{g}_{P}
. Whenever
\Upsilon_{A}(a,\xi)=0
, then
A+a
is a
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton (in Coulomb gauge relative to
A
) by
Section 3.1
.

Remark 3.6
.

In the definition of
\Upsilon_{A}
we used the formal adjoints with respect to
\tilde{g}_{t}
because the Hodge star
*_{\tilde{\phi}_{t}}
is a parallel isometry with respect to
\tilde{g}_{t}
. This makes estimates in norms taken with respect to this metric slightly simpler. However, since the Hodge star is determined by a (universal) smooth function of the
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure, we have by
Section 2.1

\|*_{\phi_{t}}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{0},t}{}}\leq\tilde{c}\|\phi_{t}-\tilde{\phi}_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{0},t}{}}+\|*_{\tilde{\phi}_{t}}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{0},t}{}}\leq c

for all
t<T_{K}
and a
t
-independent constant
c>0
(where we consider the Hodge star as a section of the bundle
\textup{End}(\Lambda^{*}T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t})
with its induced inner product). This implies that all statements below also hold if we take the adjoints in the definition of
\Upsilon_{A}
with respect to
g_{t}
(cf.
[
Wal13a
, Sections 6,7,8]
).

Proposition 3.7

(cf.
[
Wal13a
, Section 8]
)
.

The linearisation
L_{A}
is an elliptic operator whose formal adjoint satisfies
L_{A}^{\tilde{*}_{t}}=\tilde{*}_{t}L_{A}\tilde{*}_{t}
(in the following we will again drop the dependence on
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
from our notation). Furthermore, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
, there exists a
c_{Q}>0
such that for any
t\in(0,T_{K})
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\underline{a}_{1},\underline{a}_{2}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{7}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
we have

\|Q_{A}(\underline{a}_{1})-Q_{A}(\underline{a}_{2})\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{2\beta},t}{}}\leq c_{Q}\big(\|\underline{a}_{1}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}+\|\underline{a}_{2}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}\big)\|\underline{a}_{1}-\underline{a}_{2}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}.

Proof.

The first part follows from
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}^{*}_{A|\Omega^{k}}=(-1)^{7(k-1)+1}*\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}*
,
*^{2}=1
,
i_{\psi_{t}^{\sharp}}=*(\psi_{t}\wedge(*\cdot))
, and the closedness of
\psi_{t}
. In order to prove the quadratic estimate, we write
\underline{a}_{i}\coloneqq(a_{i},\xi_{i})
and note that
[i_{{a_{i}}^{\sharp}},\xi_{i}]=\tilde{*}_{t}[a_{i}\wedge\tilde{*}_{t}\xi_{i}]
. Since
\psi_{t}=*_{\phi_{t}}\phi_{t}
is determined by a (universal) smooth function on the
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure we have by
Section 2.1

\|\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{0},t}{}}\leq\|\psi_{t}-\tilde{\psi}_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{0},t}{}}+\|\tilde{\psi}_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{0},t}{}}\leq c

for sufficiently small
t
and a
t
-independent
c>0
. We therefore obtain by (
2.4
)

\displaystyle\|Q_{A}(\underline{a}_{1})-Q_{A}(\underline{a}_{2})\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{2\beta},t}{}}

\displaystyle\leq c\big(\|[a_{1}\wedge a_{1}]-[a_{2}\wedge a_{2}]\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{2\beta},t}{}}+\|[a_{1}\wedge*\xi_{1}]-[a_{2}\wedge*\xi_{2}]\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{2\beta},t}{}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq c\big(\big(\|[(a_{1}-a_{2})\wedge(a_{1}+a_{2})]\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{2\beta},t}{}}+\|[a_{1}\wedge*(\xi_{1}-\xi_{2})]\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{2\beta},t}{}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|[a_{1}-a_{2}\wedge*\xi_{2}]\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{2\beta},t}{}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq c\big(\|\underline{a}_{1}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}+\|\underline{a}_{2}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}\big)\|\underline{a}_{1}-\underline{a}_{2}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}.\qed

Remark 3.8
.

Note that
Q_{A}
and therefore also the constant
c_{Q}
in the previous proposition are independent of the connection
A\in\mathcal{A}(P)
.

Let
\lambda\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(\hat{Y}_{t},\tilde{g}_{t})
now be an action by a (possibly trivial) group
H
preserving
\psi_{t}
.

Definition 3.9
.

A lift of
\lambda\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(\hat{Y}_{t},\tilde{g}_{t})
to
\pi\colon\thinspace P\to\hat{Y}_{t}
is a homomorphism
\tilde{\lambda}\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(P)
such that for every
h\in H
the bundle isomorphism
\tilde{\lambda}(h)
covers
\lambda(h)
(i.e.
\lambda(h)\circ\pi=\pi\circ\tilde{\lambda}(h)
).

Lemma 3.10
.

The lift
\tilde{\lambda}\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(P)
induces an
H
-action on
\Omega^{k}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
defined by

\big(\tilde{\lambda}(h)^{*}\eta\big)(v_{1},\dots,v_{k})\coloneqq\tilde{\lambda}(h)^{-1}\big(\eta(D\lambda(h)(v_{1}),\dots,D\lambda(h)(v_{k}))\big)

for
\eta\in\Omega^{k}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
and for all
v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\in T_{y}\hat{Y}_{t}
and
y\in\hat{Y}_{t}
. If
A\in\mathcal{A}(P)
satisfies
\tilde{\lambda}(h)^{*}\theta_{A}=\theta_{A}
for every
h\in H
(where
\theta_{A}
is the connection 1-form associated to
A
), then
\Upsilon_{A}
is an
H
-equivariant map.

Remark 3.11
.

The assumption that
\hat{\lambda}(h)
preserves both
\tilde{g}_{t}
and
\psi_{t}
(and therefore also
g_{t}
by
Section 2.1
) was made so that the previous lemma holds. All group actions which come via the equivariant generalised Kummer construction (as explained in
Section 2.1
) satisfy this assumption. Note, however, that if one takes the adjoints in the definition of
\Upsilon_{A}
with respect to
g_{t}
(cf.
Section 3.1
), then it suffices that
H
only fixes
\psi_{t}
.

Proof.

For any (not necessarily
H
-invariant) connection
B\in\mathcal{A}(P)
and any
\eta\in\Omega^{k}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
we have
\tilde{\lambda}(h)^{*}(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{B}\eta)=\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{\lambda}(h)^{*}B}\tilde{\lambda}(h)^{*}\eta
. Since
\lambda(h)
preserves
\tilde{g}_{t}
, the same holds true with respect to
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{B}^{*}
and, similarly,

\tilde{\lambda}(h)^{*}(F_{B}\wedge\psi_{t})=F_{\tilde{\lambda}(h)^{*}B}\wedge\psi_{t}.\qed

3.2
Perturbing families of invariant almost-instantons

In the following we will prove in
Section 3.2
the main result of this section. This gives sufficient conditions under which a family of
H
-invariant connections may be perturbed to a nearby family of (
H
-invariant)
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons. Furthermore, we prove that the perturbed family of instantons has the same regularity (seen as a map into
\mathcal{A}(P)
) as the initial family of connections and give in
Section 3.2
estimates on its derivatives (which are needed later in the proof of
Section 6.5
). The proof of these results requires the following extension of Banach’s fixed-point Theorem to a family of contractions, which is well known.

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
(B,\|\cdot\|)
be a Banach space and
U\coloneqq\overline{B_{R}(0)}\subset B
be a closed ball of radius
R\in(0,\infty]
. Assume further that
\mathfrak{F}
is a manifold (possibly non-compact and/or with boundary), and
E\colon\thinspace\mathfrak{F}\times U\to U
is a family of contractions (i.e.
E_{\mathfrak{f}}\coloneqq E(\mathfrak{f},\cdot)
is a contraction for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
) that satisfies
\|E_{\mathfrak{f}}(b_{1})-E_{\mathfrak{f}}(b_{2})\|\leq C\|b_{1}-b_{2}\|
for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
and
b_{1},b_{2}\in U
for an
\mathfrak{f}
-independent constant
C<1
. For any
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
we denote by
a_{\mathfrak{f}}\in U
the unique fixed-point of
E_{\mathfrak{f}}
and by
\textup{Fix}\colon\thinspace\mathfrak{F}\to U
the map
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto a_{\mathfrak{f}}
. The following hold:

1.

If
E
is continuous (as a map
\mathfrak{F}\times U\to U
), then so is
Fix
.

2.

If
E
is
C^{k}
(as a map
\mathfrak{F}\times U\to U
), then so is
Fix
. Furthermore, its derivative
D\textup{Fix}\colon\thinspace T\mathfrak{F}\to U\times B
satisfies for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
and
v\in T_{\mathfrak{f}}\mathfrak{F}

\displaystyle\|(D\textup{Fix})_{\mathfrak{f}}(v)\|

\displaystyle\leq(1-\|(\partial_{B}E)_{(\mathfrak{f},a_{\mathfrak{f}})}\|_{\textup{op}})^{-1}\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}E)_{(\mathfrak{f},a_{\mathfrak{f}})}(v)\|

\displaystyle\leq(1-c_{\mathfrak{f}})^{-1}\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}E)_{(\mathfrak{f},a_{\mathfrak{f}})}(v)\|.

Here
\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}E
and
\partial_{B}E
denote the respective partial derivatives of
E
in
\mathfrak{F}
and
B
direction and
c_{\mathfrak{f}}<C
denotes the optimal constant for which
\|E_{\mathfrak{f}}(b_{1})-E_{\mathfrak{f}}(b_{2})\|\leq c_{\mathfrak{f}}\|b_{1}-b_{2}\|
for every
b_{1},b_{2}\in U
.

Proof.

In order to prove the first point, let
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2}\in\mathfrak{F}
. Then

\displaystyle\|a_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}}-a_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|E_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}}(a_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}})-E_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}}(a_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}})\|+\|E_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}}(a_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}})-E_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}}(a_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|E_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}}(a_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}})-E_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}}(a_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}})\|+C\|a_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}}-a_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}}\|

where
C<1
is
\mathfrak{f}_{2}
-independent by the assumption. Absorbing the second term on the right-hand side gives

\|a_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}}-a_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}}\|\leq C^{\prime}\|E_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}}(a_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}})-E_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}}(a_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}})\|.

(3.2)

When
E
is continuous, then
\mathfrak{f}_{2}\to\mathfrak{f}_{1}
implies therefore
a_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}}\to a_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}}
which proves the first assertion.

For the second point, we work locally and assume that
\mathfrak{F}
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
or
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{x_{1}\geq 0}
and prove differentiability of
Fix
at
\mathfrak{f}_{0}=0
. By the differentiability of
E
we have

\displaystyle a_{\mathfrak{f}}-a_{0}

\displaystyle=E_{\mathfrak{f}}(a_{\mathfrak{f}})-E_{0}(a_{0})=(DE)_{(0,a_{0})}(\mathfrak{f},a_{\mathfrak{f}}-a_{0})+o(|\mathfrak{f}|+\|a_{\mathfrak{f}}-a_{0}\|)

\displaystyle=(\partial_{B}E)_{(0,a_{0})}(a_{\mathfrak{f}}-a_{0})+(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}E)_{(0,a_{0})}\cdot\mathfrak{f}+o(|\mathfrak{f}|+\|a_{\mathfrak{f}}-a_{0}\|)

where
DE
denotes the (total) differential of
E
and
\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}E
and
\partial_{B}E
the partial derivatives in
\mathfrak{F}
and
B
direction. Since
\|E_{0}(b)-E_{0}(a_{0})\|\leq c_{0}\|b-a_{0}\|
for every
b\in U
with
c_{0}<1
, we obtain
\|(\partial_{B}E)_{(0,a_{0})}\|_{\textup{op}}=\|(DE_{0})_{a_{0}}\|_{\textup{op}}\leq c_{0}<1
. The operator
(\textup{Id}-(\partial_{B}E)_{(0,a_{0})})
is therefore invertible via the Neumann series of
(\partial_{B}E)_{(0,a_{0})}
. This gives

a_{\mathfrak{f}}-a_{0}=(\textup{Id}-(\partial_{B}E)_{(0,a_{0})})^{-1}\big((\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}E)_{(0,a_{0})}\cdot\mathfrak{f}+o(|\mathfrak{f}|+\|a_{\mathfrak{f}}-a_{0}\|)\big).

The differentiability of
E
together with (
3.2
) imply that
o(|\mathfrak{f}|+\|a_{\mathfrak{f}}-a_{0}\|)=o(|\mathfrak{f}|)
. The previous formula for
a_{\mathfrak{f}}-a_{0}
shows therefore that
Fix
is differentiable at
\mathfrak{f}=0
with derivative

(D\textup{Fix})_{\mathfrak{f}}=(\textup{Id}-(\partial_{B}E)_{(\mathfrak{f},a_{\mathfrak{f}})})^{-1}\big((\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}E)_{(\mathfrak{f},a_{\mathfrak{f}})}(\ \cdot\ )\big).

Since inversion and concatenation are smooth operations,
E\in C^{k}
implies via bootstrapping that
\textup{Fix}\in C^{k}
.
∎

In the following let
(\hat{Y}_{t},\phi_{t})
for
t\in(0,T_{K})
again be a fixed 1-parameter family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-manifolds obtained from Joyce’s generalised Kummer construction together with an
H
-action
\lambda\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(\hat{Y}_{t},\tilde{g}_{t})
by a (possibly trivial) group
H
preserving
\psi_{t}
.

Theorem 3.13
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
be a fixed Hölder exponent and
\gamma,C>0
and
\varepsilon\geq 0
be constants with
\varepsilon<\gamma
. Then there exist constants
0<T_{I}<T_{K}
and
c_{I}>0
depending only
\alpha,\gamma,C,\varepsilon
with the following significance: Let
t\in(0,T_{I})
and
\pi\colon\thinspace P\to\hat{Y}_{t}
be a principal
G
-bundle together with a lift of the
H
-action. Assume further that
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}
is a connected subset (i.e. a point or an interval – possibly non-compact and/or with boundary) and that
\mathfrak{F}\ni\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}\in\mathcal{A}(P)
is a family of
H
-invariant connections that satisfies

1.

\|F_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq Ct^{\gamma}
for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}

2.

\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq C\|L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}
for every
H
-invariant
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
and every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}

3.

\|L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq C\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{-\varepsilon},t}{}}
for every
H
-invariant
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
and every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
.

All norms in the expressions above are taken with respect to the metric induced by
\tilde{g}_{t}
and the
Ad
-invariant inner product on
\mathfrak{g}
. Similarly, covariant derivatives are taken with respect to the Levi–Civita connection and a fixed
B\in\mathcal{A}(P)
. Under these assumptions there exists an
H
-invariant family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons
\mathfrak{F}\ni\mathfrak{f}\mapsto A_{\mathfrak{f}}\in\mathcal{A}(P)
with
\|\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}-A_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq c_{I}t^{\gamma}
for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
. Furthermore, if the map
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
lies in
C^{L}(\mathfrak{F},\mathcal{A}(P))
for
L\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\cup\{\infty\}
, then
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto A_{\mathfrak{f}}
also lies in
C^{L}(\mathfrak{F},\mathcal{A}(P))
.

Remark 3.14
.

Throughout this article we consider
\mathcal{A}(P)
as an affine Fréchet space and a map
\textup{Fam}\colon\thinspace\mathfrak{F}\to\mathcal{A}(P)
(for an interval
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}
) to be of regularity
C^{1}
if the map

\displaystyle D\textup{Fam}\colon\thinspace\mathfrak{F}\times\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\to\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{f},v)

\displaystyle\mapsto(D\textup{Fam})_{\mathfrak{f}}(v)\coloneqq\lim_{s\to 0}\tfrac{\textup{Fam}(\mathfrak{f}+sv)-\textup{Fam}(\mathfrak{f})}{s}

exists and is (jointly) continuous. Higher regularity is defined in a similar manner.

Remark 3.15
.

Regarding weakening the assumptions of the previous theorem:

1.

Denote by
\textup{Fam}\colon\thinspace\mathfrak{F}\to\mathcal{A}(P)
the family of connections of the previous theorem, i.e.
\textup{Fam}(\mathfrak{f})\coloneqq\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathfrak{f}}
. We have made the assumption that
\mathfrak{F}
is an interval because this leads to the somewhat simpler notation
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\textup{Fam})_{\mathfrak{f}}\coloneqq(D\textup{Fam})_{\mathfrak{f}}(1)
for the derivative of
Fam
. Since the connections in
Section 6
to which we apply
Section 3.2
are parametrised by an interval, this assumption does not restrict us in this article. Note however, that one can easily generalise the theorems of this section to apply to any manifold
\mathfrak{F}
(possibly non-compact and/or with boundary).

2.

Similarly, one can weaken the condition that the
H
-action lifts to
P
. Indeed, it suffices that the action lifts to the adjoint bundle
\mathfrak{g}_{P}
and
\Upsilon_{A}
is equivariant with respect to this lift.

Remark 3.16
.

If
H
and
\mathfrak{F}
are both trivial and
\varepsilon=0
, then the above theorem becomes analogous to the construction used in
[
Wal13a
]
to produce
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons over generalised Kummer constructions resolving orbifolds with codimension
4
singularities. Note, however, that for non-trivial
H
-actions our second assumption (listed under
Point 2
) differs from the analogous condition in
[
Wal13a
, Section 8]
. This is because the
H
-invariant kernel and cokernel of
L_{A}
are in general not isomorphic (as can be see in the examples of
Section 6
). Note also that establishing
Point 2
of
Section 3.2
(which is done in
Section 5.2
) becomes simpler when
\varepsilon>0
and we will therefore make use of this freedom.

Remark 3.17
.

If
\mathfrak{F}
is an interval, then each
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton
A_{\mathfrak{f}}
in the family
(A_{\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}
constructed in the previous theorem is necessarily obstructed. Note, however, that under mild assumptions (e.g. the analogue of
Point 4
in
Section 3.2
for the operator
L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}
) the instanton
A_{\mathfrak{f}}
becomes
H
-unobstructed (i.e. there are no
H
-invariant elements in the cokernel of
L_{A_{\mathfrak{f}}}
) once
t
is sufficiently small. This can be proven as
Section 3.2
.

Proof of
Section 3.2
.

For
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
define the closed (and therefore complete) subspaces

\displaystyle V^{k,\alpha}_{\beta,t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\underline{a}\in C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta,t}(T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P}\oplus\Lambda^{7}T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P})\mid\textup{$\underline{a}$ is $H$-invariant}\right\}

\displaystyle W^{k,\alpha}_{\beta,t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\underline{b}\in C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta,t}(\Lambda^{6}T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{P})\mid\textup{$\underline{b}$ is $H$-invariant}\right\}.

Since
L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}
and
L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}
are
H
-equivariant, the second condition implies that
L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\colon\thinspace W^{2,\alpha}_{-\varepsilon,t}\to W^{0,\alpha}_{-\varepsilon-2,t}
is injective. Formal self-adjointness,
H
-equivariance, and elliptic regularity imply that
L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\colon\thinspace W^{2,\alpha}_{-\varepsilon,t}\to W^{0,\alpha}_{-\varepsilon-2,t}
is also surjective and we can therefore define

R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\coloneqq L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}(L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*})^{-1}\colon\thinspace W^{0,\alpha}_{-2-\varepsilon,t}\to V^{1,\alpha}_{-1-\varepsilon,t}.

By construction, this is a right-inverse to
L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}
and
Point 2
and
Point 3
in the above statement and
Section 2.1
imply

\displaystyle\|R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}

\displaystyle\leq c\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}

(3.3)

for a
t
and
\mathfrak{f}
-independent constant
c>0
(which we will assume to be larger than 2 in the following).

Since
F_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\wedge\psi_{t}\in W^{0,\alpha}_{-2-\varepsilon,t}
and
Q_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\colon\thinspace V^{1,\alpha}_{-1-\varepsilon,t}\to W^{0,\alpha}_{-2-\varepsilon,t}
, we can define the map

\displaystyle E_{\mathfrak{f}}\colon\thinspace W^{0,\alpha}_{-2-\varepsilon,t}

\displaystyle\to W^{0,\alpha}_{-2-\varepsilon,t}

\displaystyle\underline{b}

\displaystyle\mapsto E_{\mathfrak{f}}(\underline{b})\coloneqq-\big(Q_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}(R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b})+F_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\wedge\psi_{t}\big).

Our assumptions,
Section 3.1
, its following remark, and (
2.5
) imply

\displaystyle\|E_{\mathfrak{f}}(0)\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}

\displaystyle\leq ct^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\|E_{\mathfrak{f}}(\underline{b}_{1})-E_{\mathfrak{f}}(\underline{b}_{2})\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}

\displaystyle\leq c\big(\|R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}_{1}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon/2},t}{}}+\|R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}_{2}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon/2},t}{}}\big)\|R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}(\underline{b}_{1}-\underline{b}_{2})\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon/2},t}{}}

\displaystyle\leq ct^{-\varepsilon}\big(\|R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}_{1}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}+\|R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}_{2}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\big)\|R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}(\underline{b}_{1}-\underline{b}_{2})\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}

\displaystyle\leq ct^{-\varepsilon}\big(\|\underline{b}_{1}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}+\|\underline{b}_{2}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}\big)\|\underline{b}_{1}-\underline{b}_{2}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}

where
c=c(\alpha,C)
is independent of
t
and of
\mathfrak{f}
.
Therefore, the map
E_{\mathfrak{f}}
restricts to a contraction on
\overline{B_{R}(0)}\subset W^{0,\alpha}_{-2-\varepsilon,t}
if

2ct^{-\varepsilon}R<1\quad\textup{and}\quad ct^{\gamma}+ct^{-\varepsilon}R^{2}<R.

This holds, for example, whenever
t^{\gamma}<(2c)^{-2}t^{\varepsilon}
(which can be achieved as
0<\varepsilon<\gamma
) and
R\coloneqq 2ct^{\gamma}
. Let
\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}\in\overline{B_{R}(0)}
be the unique fixpoint of
E_{\mathfrak{f}}
and
\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}}\coloneqq R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}
. By construction, this solves

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{A_{\mathfrak{f}}}(\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}})

\displaystyle=L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}}+Q_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}(\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}})+F_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\wedge\psi_{t}=\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}+Q_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}(R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}})+F_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\wedge\psi_{t}=0

and is therefore smooth by elliptic regularity. Furthermore,
Section 3.1
implies that
A_{\mathfrak{f}}\coloneqq\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}+a_{\mathfrak{f}}\in\mathcal{A}(P)
is a
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton.

If
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
lies in
C^{L}(\mathfrak{F},\mathcal{A}(P))
, where we assume from now on that
\mathfrak{F}
is an interval, then
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}
lies in
C^{L}(\mathfrak{F},\textup{Lin}(C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta,t},C^{k-1,\alpha}_{\beta-1,t}))
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
(where
\textup{Lin}(C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta,t},C^{k-1,\alpha}_{\beta-1,t})
denotes the Banach space of bounded linear maps
C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta,t}\to C^{k-1,\alpha}_{\beta-1,t}
