). Since concatenation and inversion are smooth operations, this implies that

\mathfrak{F}\times W^{0,\alpha}_{-2-\varepsilon,t}\ni(\mathfrak{f},\underline{b})\mapsto E_{\mathfrak{f}}(\underline{b})\in W^{0,\alpha}_{-2-\varepsilon,t}

is a
C^{L}
-map.
Section 3.2
implies therefore that
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto a_{\mathfrak{f}}
lies in
C^{L}(\mathfrak{F},V^{1,\alpha}_{-1-\varepsilon,t})
(where
V^{1,\alpha}_{-1-\varepsilon,t}
is equipped with its Banach space topology).

Next, we will prove that
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto a_{\mathfrak{f}}
is indeed of regularity
C^{L}
when regarded as a map into the space
C^{\infty}(T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P}\oplus\Lambda^{7}T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P})
equipped with its Fréchet topology. For this we will denote in the following by
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}}\in V^{1,\alpha}_{-1-\varepsilon,t}
and
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}L_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}\in\textup{Lin}(V^{1,\alpha}_{\beta,t},W^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1,t})
the respective derivatives of
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}}
and
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}
taken with respect to the indicated Banach space topologies. Similarly, we will denote by
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}F_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}\in\Omega^{2}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
the derivative of
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto F_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}
taken with respect to the Fréchet topology.

Taking the derivative of
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\Upsilon_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}(\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}})
(as a map into the Banach space
W^{0,\alpha}_{-2-\varepsilon,t}
) at
\mathfrak{f}_{0}\in\mathfrak{F}
(and noting that
Q_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\equiv Q
is independent of the connection) yields

L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}}(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}+(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}L_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}+(D_{\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}}Q)((\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}})+(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}F_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}\wedge\psi_{t}=0.

Elliptic regularity of the operator
L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}}
gives via bootstrapping
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}\in C^{\infty}(T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P}\oplus\Lambda T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P})
.

Next, we prove that
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}
is also the derivative of
\underline{a}
at
{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}
when regarded as a map
\mathfrak{F}\to C^{\infty}(T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P}\oplus\Lambda^{7}T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P})
. For this we assume that we have already proven that
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}
is the derivative of
\underline{a}
at
{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}
seen as a map
\mathfrak{F}\to C^{k-1,\alpha}(T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P}\oplus\Lambda^{7}T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P})
for some fixed
k\in\mathbb{N}
(the case
k=2
holds by construction and
Section 3.2
) and show the same statement holds when regarded as a map
\mathfrak{F}\to C^{k,\alpha}(T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P}\oplus\Lambda^{7}T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P})
. Since sequences converge in the
C^{\infty}
-topology if and only if they converge in all
C^{k,\alpha}
-norms, this implies the claim.

The Schauder estimate
9
9

9

The constant
c
in the following estimates will in general depend on
t
. This does not affect the argument.
for
L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}}
gives for any
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}
with
|\mathfrak{f}|
sufficiently small

\displaystyle\|\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}+\mathfrak{f}}-\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}-(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}\cdot\mathfrak{f}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}{}}

\displaystyle\leq c\big(\|L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}}(\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}+\mathfrak{f}}-\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}-(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}\cdot\mathfrak{f})\|_{C^{{k-1},\alpha}{}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}+\mathfrak{f}}-\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}-(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}\cdot\mathfrak{f}\|_{C^{{0}}{}}\big).

The second term on the right-hand side is by assumption
o(|\mathfrak{f}|)
. In order to bound the first term we rewrite

L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}}\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}+\mathfrak{f}}=\big(L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}+\mathfrak{f}}}-(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}L_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}\cdot\mathfrak{f}-\mathfrak{R}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}(\mathfrak{f})\big)\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}+\mathfrak{f}}

where by the differentiability of
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
and
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}}
we have
\|\mathfrak{R}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}(\mathfrak{f})\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}+\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{k-1},\alpha}{}}=o(|\mathfrak{f}|)
. Inserting the equations for
L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}+\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}+\mathfrak{f}}
,
L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}}\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}
, and
L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}}(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}
, noting that
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}L_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}
is an algebraic operator, and using the assumption that
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}
is the derivative of the map
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}}\in C^{k-1,\alpha}
, one can prove that the first term on the right-hand side is also
o(|\mathfrak{f}|)
. This shows that
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}_{0}}
is also the derivative of the map
\mathfrak{F}\to C^{k,\alpha}(T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P}\oplus\Lambda^{7}T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P})
and by iteration also the derivative of
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}}\in C^{\infty}(T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P}\oplus\Lambda^{7}T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P})
.

Finally, the continuity of the derivative
\mathfrak{F}\ni\mathfrak{f}\mapsto(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}}\in C^{\infty}(T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P}\oplus\Lambda^{7}T^{*}\hat{Y}_{t}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P})
can be proven analogously which implies that
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto A_{\mathfrak{f}}
lies in
C^{1}(\mathfrak{F},\mathcal{A}(P))
.

For the higher derivatives, inductively take the
\ell
-th derivative of
\Upsilon_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}(\underline{a}_{\mathfrak{f}})
for
\ell\in\{1,\dots,L\}
and proceed in the same manner.
∎

Proposition 3.18
.

Assume the situation of
Section 3.2
in which the family
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
lies in
C^{L}(\mathfrak{F},\mathcal{A}(P))
for some fixed
L\in\mathbb{N}
and assume that we additionally have

4.

\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}F_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq Ct^{\gamma}
for every
\ell\in\{0,\dots,L\}
and every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
,

5.

\|(\partial^{\ell}_{\mathfrak{F}}L_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}\underline{a}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq C\|\underline{a}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}
for every
H
-invariant
\underline{a}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{7}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
, every
\ell\in\{0,\dots,L\}
, and every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
,

6.

\|(\partial^{\ell}_{\mathfrak{F}}L_{\tilde{A}}^{*})_{\mathfrak{f}}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq C\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{-\varepsilon},t}{}}
for every
H
-invariant
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
, every
\ell\in\{0,\dots,L\}
, and every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
.

Here and below
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}L_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}
and
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}F_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}
denote the derivatives of
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\in\textup{Lin}(C^{1,\alpha}_{-1-\varepsilon,t},C^{0,\alpha}_{-2-\varepsilon,t})
and
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto F_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\in\Omega^{2}(\hat{Y},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
, respectively. Under these assumptions
c_{I}
in
Section 3.2
can be enlarged so that additionally

\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}\tilde{A})_{\mathfrak{f}}-(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}A)_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq c_{I}t^{\gamma}

holds for every
\ell\in\{0,\dots,L\}
.

Proof.

In order to estimate the difference of the first derivatives of
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
and
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto A_{\mathfrak{f}}
note that by construction

\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\tilde{A})_{\mathfrak{f}}-(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}A)_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}=\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{a})_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}=\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}R_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}+R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{b})_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}

where
\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}
and
R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}
are as in the proof of
Section 3.2
. In the following we will prove that there exists a constant so that

\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{b})_{f}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq ct^{\gamma}\qquad\textup{and}\qquad\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}R_{\tilde{A}})_{f}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq c\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}

(3.4)

for every
{\mathfrak{f}}\in\mathfrak{F}
and
H
-invariant
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
. Since by (
3.3
) and
Section 3.2
we additionally have

\|R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq c\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}\qquad\textup{and}\qquad\|\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq ct^{\gamma}

for every
{\mathfrak{f}}\in\mathfrak{F}
and
H
-invariant
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
, this implies the estimate on the first derivative.

Note that the contraction constant of the contraction
E_{\mathfrak{f}}
in the proof of
Section 3.2
can be bounded
\mathfrak{f}
-independently by
4c^{2}t^{\gamma-\varepsilon}<1
.
Section 3.2
implies therefore for the derivative of
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}
:

\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}\underline{b})_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq c\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}E)_{\mathfrak{f}}(\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}})\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}=c\|(D_{R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}}Q)((\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}R_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}})-\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}(F_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}.

Furthermore, the derivative of the quadratic map
Q
takes the form

(D_{R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}}Q)((\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}R_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}})=\{R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}},(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}R_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}\}

where
\{\cdot,\cdot\}
is a bilinear algebraic map. This observation together with
Point 4
in the assumptions,
\|\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq ct^{\gamma}
, and
\|R_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{b}_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq ct^{\gamma}
, implies that the first estimate in (
3.4
) follows from the second estimate.

We are therefore left to prove

\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}R_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq c\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}

for every
{\mathfrak{f}}\in\mathfrak{F}
and
H
-invariant
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
. For this note that taking the derivative of
(L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*})(L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*})^{-1}=\textup{id}
implies that

\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}(L_{\tilde{A}}L_{\tilde{A}}^{*})_{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}=-(L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*})^{-1}(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}(L_{\tilde{A}}L_{\tilde{A}}^{*})_{\mathfrak{f}})(L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*})^{-1}.

The assumption in
Point 2
of
Section 3.2
and
Point 5
and
Point 6
in this proposition yield the
t
-independent bound

\|\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}(L_{\tilde{A}}L_{\tilde{A}}^{*})_{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq c\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}

for every
H
-invariant
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
. Similarly, we obtain

\|\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}(R_{\tilde{A}})_{\mathfrak{f}}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-\varepsilon},t}{}}=\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}L_{\tilde{A}}^{*})_{\mathfrak{f}}(L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*})^{-1}\underline{b}+L_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}(L_{\tilde{A}}L_{\tilde{A}}^{*})_{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1})\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq c\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}

for every
H
-invariant
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
which is exactly the second estimate in (
3.4
). This completes the proof for
L=1
. The estimates for the difference of the higher derivatives of
f\mapsto\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
and
f\mapsto A_{f}
are proven in a similar way by inductively showing that (
3.4
) also holds for higher
\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}
-derivatives.
∎

Denote by
\mathfrak{z}\subset\mathfrak{g}
the center of
\mathfrak{g}
, i.e. all elements of
\mathfrak{g}
that are fixed under the Adjoint-action. There exists a canonical inclusion
\mathfrak{z}\subset\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
which associates to each
\zeta\in\mathfrak{z}
the section

\hat{Y}_{t}\ni y\mapsto[p_{y},\zeta]\in\mathfrak{g}_{P}

where
p_{y}\in P
is any element in the fiber over
y
.

Definition 3.19
.

A connection
A\in\mathcal{A}(P)
is called infinitesimally irreducible if the kernel of
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}\colon\thinspace\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\to\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
equals
\mathfrak{z}
.

Proposition 3.20
.

Assume the set-up of
Section 3.2
. There exists a
0<T_{iI}<T_{I}
depending only on
\alpha,\gamma,C,
and
\varepsilon
with the following significance: If
t<T_{iI}
and
\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
for
{\mathfrak{f}}\in\mathfrak{F}
satisfies the additional hypothesis

4.

\|\xi\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{-\varepsilon},t}{}}\leq C\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}
for every
\xi\in\mathfrak{z}^{\perp,L^{2}}\subset\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
,

5.

\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{}}\leq C\|\xi\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}
for any
\xi\in\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
and
\beta\in\{0,-1-\varepsilon\}
,

then the
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton
A_{\mathfrak{f}}
constructed in
Section 3.2
is infinitesimally irreducible.

Proof.

By integration by parts, the connections
A_{\mathfrak{f}}
and
\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
are infinitesimally irreducible if and only if the associated Laplacians
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{\mathfrak{f}}}
and
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}
mapping
C^{2,\alpha}_{-\varepsilon,t}(\mathfrak{g}_{P})\to C^{0,\alpha}_{-2-\varepsilon,t}(\mathfrak{g}_{P})
have kernels equal to
\mathfrak{z}
. For
\xi\in C^{2,\alpha}_{-\varepsilon,t}(\mathfrak{g}_{P})
we have by (
2.4
), (
2.5
),
Section 3.2
, and
Point 5

\displaystyle\|(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}-\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{\mathfrak{f}}})\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}-\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{\mathfrak{f}}})\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}+\|(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}-\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{\mathfrak{f}}}^{*})\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{\mathfrak{f}}}\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2-\varepsilon},t}{}}

\displaystyle\leq c\|(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}-\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{\mathfrak{f}}})\xi\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}+\|\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}-A_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{\mathfrak{f}}}\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-1},t}{}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}-A_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\big(c\|\xi\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{0},t}{}}+\|A_{\mathfrak{f}}-\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-1-\varepsilon},t}{}}\|\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\varepsilon},t}{}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-1},t}{}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq ct^{\gamma-\varepsilon}\big(\|\xi\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-\varepsilon},t}{}}+t^{\gamma-\varepsilon}\|\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-\varepsilon},t}{}}+\|\xi\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-\varepsilon},t}{}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq ct^{\gamma-\varepsilon}(2+t^{\gamma-\varepsilon})\|\xi\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{-\varepsilon},t}{}},

where all constants are independent of
t
and
\mathfrak{f}
. This together with
Point 4
implies that
A_{\mathfrak{f}}
is infinitesimally irreducible once
ct^{\gamma-\varepsilon}(2+t^{\gamma-\varepsilon})<\frac{1}{C}
.
∎

4
Approximate
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons from gluing data

Let
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
be a flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold satisfying
Section 2.1
. That is, for any connected component of the singular set
S\in\mathcal{S}
there is a finite subgroup
\Gamma_{S}<\textup{SU}(3)
, a lattice
\Lambda_{S}<\mathbb{R}
, and an open embedding
\mathtt{J}_{S}\colon\thinspace\mathcal{V}_{S}\to Y_{0}
for
\mathcal{V}_{S}\coloneqq(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0)/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
that satisfies
\mathtt{J}_{S}^{*}\phi_{0}=\phi_{S}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
(\pi_{0}\colon\thinspace P_{0}\to Y_{0},A_{0})
be a flat orbifold principal
G
-bundle. The pullback
\mathtt{J}_{S}^{*}\pi_{0}\colon\thinspace\mathtt{J}_{S}^{*}P_{0}\to\mathcal{V}_{S}
is isomorphic to

\big(\mathbb{R}\times(B_{\kappa}(0)\times_{\Gamma_{S}}G)\big)/\Lambda_{S}

where
\Gamma_{S}
and
\Lambda_{S}
act on
G
via the monodromy representation associated to
\mathtt{J}_{S}^{*}A_{0}
. This isomorphism identifies
\mathtt{J}^{*}_{S}A_{0}
with the flat connection induced from the canonical connection on
(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa})\times G
.

Proof.

This follows from the general theory of flat bundles (cf.
[
DK90
, Proposition 2.2.3]
and
[
ALR07
, Theorem 2.36]
) because
\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0)
is the universal orbifold covering space of
\mathcal{V}_{S}
(cf.
[
ALR07
, Example 2.20]
) and the orbifold fundamental group fits into the short exact sequence

In the following we will specify data from which we will subsequently construct the connections over
\hat{Y}_{t}
which are our candidates for applying
Section 3.2
.

Definition 4.2

(cf.
[
Wal13a
, Definition 6.3]
)
.

Let
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
be a flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold satisfying
Section 2.1
together with a set
\mathfrak{R}
of resolution data. A set of gluing data compatible with
\mathfrak{R}
consists of

•

a flat orbifold principal
G
-bundle
(\pi_{0}\colon\thinspace P_{0}\to Y_{0},A_{0})
,

and for every
S\in\mathcal{S}
of

•

a choice of isomorphism
\tilde{\mathtt{J}}_{S}\colon\thinspace\big(\mathbb{R}\times(B_{\kappa}(0)\times_{\Gamma_{S}}G)\big)/\Lambda_{S}\to\mathtt{J}^{*}_{S}P_{0}
as in the previous lemma,

•

a
G
-bundle
\pi_{S}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{S}\to\hat{Z}_{S}
together with a locally framed HYM connection
\hat{A}_{S}
asymptotic to the flat connection over the bundle
(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})\times_{\Gamma_{S}}G
(with rate
-5
), and

•

a lift
\hat{\tilde{\rho}}_{S}\colon\thinspace\Lambda_{S}\to\textup{Isom}(\hat{P}_{S})
of the action
\hat{\rho}_{S}\colon\thinspace\Lambda_{S}\to\textup{Isom}(\hat{Z}_{S})
that

1.

satisfies
\tilde{\tau}_{S}^{-1}\circ\tilde{\rho}_{S}\circ\tilde{\tau}_{S}=\hat{\tilde{\rho}}_{S}
where
\tilde{\rho}\colon\thinspace\Lambda_{S}\to\textup{Isom}((\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})\times_{\Gamma_{S}}G)
is induced by the monodromy representation of
A_{0}
.

2.

preserves
\hat{A}_{S}
.

Remark 4.3
.

The last point in the previous definition means that for every
g\in\Lambda_{S}
the following diagram commutes (where the dashed arrows signify that
\tau_{S}
and
\tilde{\tau}_{S}
are only defined on (or restricted to) the complement of the exceptional divisor
\tau_{S}^{-1}(0)
and map
\hat{A}_{S}
only asymptotically to
A_{0}
):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big((\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})\times_{\Gamma_{S}}G,A_{0}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big((\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})\times_{\Gamma_{S}}G,A_{0}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\hat{P}_{S},\hat{A}_{S}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\hat{P}_{S},\hat{A}_{S}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})/\Gamma_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})/\Gamma_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{Z}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{Z}_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\rho}_{S}(g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\tilde{\rho}}_{S}(g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\tau}_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\tau}_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{S}(g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\rho}_{S}(g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{S}}

In
Section 2.1
we have seen that some generalised Kummer constructions carry a non-trivial group action preserving the coassociative 4-form. The following extension of
Section 4
helps us to lift this action to the principal bundle constructed out of the data specified in
Section 4
and to perform the pre-gluing construction of the connection equivariantly.

Remark 4.4

(Equivariant gluing data)
.

Let
\lambda_{0}\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(Y_{0},g_{\phi_{0}})
be a group action as in
Section 2.1
and let the chosen resolution data be
H
-equivariant. An
H
-equivariant gluing data is a set of gluing data together with a lift
\tilde{\lambda}_{0}\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(P_{0})
preserving
A_{0}
and, moreover:

1.

\tilde{\mathrm{J}}_{S}=\tilde{\mathrm{J}}_{hS}
,
\hat{P}_{S}=\hat{P}_{hS}
,
\tilde{\tau}_{S}=\tilde{\tau}_{hS}
,
\hat{A}_{S}=\hat{A}_{hS}
, and
\hat{\tilde{\rho}}_{S}=\hat{\tilde{\rho}}_{hS}
for every
h\in H
and
S\in\mathcal{S}
.

2.

For every
[S]\in\mathcal{S}/H
there exists
\tilde{\lambda}_{[S]}\colon\thinspace H\to N_{\textup{Isom}((\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3})\times_{\Gamma_{S}}G)}(\Lambda_{[S]})/\Lambda_{[S]}
covering
\lambda_{[S]}
that satisfies

\tilde{\mathtt{J}}_{[S]}\circ[\tilde{\lambda}_{[S]}(h)]=\tilde{\lambda}(h)\circ\tilde{\mathtt{J}}_{[S]}

for every
h\in H
(where
[\tilde{\lambda}_{[S]}(h)]
denotes the induced map on
((\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3})\times_{\Gamma_{[S]}}G)/\Lambda_{[S]}
).

3.

For every
[S]\in\mathcal{S}/H
we have a lift
\hat{\tilde{\lambda}}_{[S]}\colon\thinspace H\to N_{\textup{Isom}(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{P}_{[S]})}(\Lambda_{[S]})/\Lambda_{[S]}
of
\hat{\lambda}_{[S]}
that preserves
\hat{A}_{[S]}
and satisfies

\tilde{\tau}_{[S]}\circ[\hat{\tilde{\lambda}}_{[S]}(h)]=[\tilde{\lambda}_{[S]}(h)]\circ\tilde{\tau}_{[S]}

for every
h\in H
.

Proposition 4.5

(cf.
[
Wal13a
, Proposition 6.7]
)
.

Let
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
be a flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold satisfying
Section 2.1
together with a chosen set of resolution data
\mathfrak{R}
. Furthermore, assume that
\mathfrak{G}
is a set of gluing data compatible with
\mathfrak{R}
. Then there exists for each
t\in(0,T_{K})
a bundle
\pi_{t}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{t}\to\hat{Y}_{t}
together with a connection
\tilde{A}_{t}
. Moreover, for each
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and
\vartheta\geq 0
there exists a constant
c_{pI}
(independent of
t<T_{K}\equiv T_{K}(k,\alpha)
) with

\|F_{\tilde{A}_{t}}\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6+\vartheta},t}{}}\leq c_{pI}t^{\textup{min}\{9/2-\vartheta,4\}}.

In addition: if
\lambda_{0}\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(Y_{0})
is a group action and the resolution and gluing data are chosen
H
-equivariantly (in the sense of
Section 2.1
and
Section 4
), then there exists a lift
\hat{\tilde{\lambda}}\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(\hat{P}_{t})
(covering the action
\hat{\lambda}
of
Section 2.1
) that preserves
\tilde{A}_{t}
.

The proof is almost identical to
[
Wal13a
, Proof of Proposition 6.7]
, where Kummer constructions resolving orbifolds with codimension 4 singularities and trivial group actions were considered. We have included a proof, nevertheless, for the sake of completeness and to verify the
H
-equivariance in the construction.

Proof.

For the construction of
\hat{P}_{t}
we first set
P_{0}^{\circ}\coloneqq{P_{0}}_{|Y_{0}\setminus\mathrm{J}(\mathcal{V}_{\kappa/8})}
and for each
S\in\mathcal{S}
we define
\hat{P}^{\circ}_{S}\coloneqq(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{P}_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
. Note that the latter naturally carries the structure of a
G
-bundle
\pi^{\circ}_{S}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}^{\circ}_{S}\to(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
. Furthermore, let in the following

\widetilde{t\tau}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{S}^{\circ}\to((\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0))\times_{\Gamma_{S}}G)/\Lambda_{S}

denote the concatenation of first applying
[\textup{Id}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\tilde{\tau}]
and then parallel translation (with respect to the flat connection) over the (shortest) straight line connecting any
[s,z]\in(\mathbb{R}\times B_{t^{-1}\kappa}(0)/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
with
[s,tz]\in\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa}
. As a manifold

\hat{P}_{t}\coloneqq P_{0}^{\circ}\cup\big(\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{P}^{\circ}_{S|\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}}\big)/\sim

where
\hat{P}^{\circ}_{S|\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}\setminus\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{t}_{S,\kappa/8}}\ni p\sim\tilde{\mathtt{J}}_{S}(\widetilde{t\tau}(p))\in P^{\circ}_{0|\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathcal{V}_{\kappa/8})}
. Since
\tilde{\mathtt{J}}_{S}\circ\widetilde{t\tau_{S}}
is a bundle map,
\hat{P}_{t}
carries a canonical
G
-bundle structure
\pi_{t}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{t}\to\hat{Y}_{t}
.

For the construction of
\tilde{A}_{t}
we first note that the connections
(\hat{A}_{S})_{S\in\mathcal{S}}
over
(\hat{P}_{S})_{S\in\mathcal{S}}
induce connections over
(\hat{P}^{\circ}_{S})_{S\in\mathcal{S}}
which we again denote by
\hat{A}_{S}
. Furthermore, over
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}
these satisfy:

\hat{A}_{S}=\widetilde{t\tau}^{*}\hat{\mathtt{J}}_{S}^{*}A_{0}+a_{S}

where
a_{S}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{t}_{S,\kappa},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}^{\circ}_{S}})
satisfies
|\nabla^{k}a_{S}|_{g_{\mathbb{R}}\oplus t^{2}g_{\hat{Z}_{S}}}=t^{4}\mathcal{O}(r_{t}^{-5-k}).

The connection over
\hat{P}_{t}
is now defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{t}\coloneqq\begin{cases}A_{0}&\textup{ over $(P_{0}^{\circ})_{|r_{t}^{-1}[\kappa/4,\kappa]}$}\\
(\widetilde{t\tau})^{*}\tilde{\mathtt{J}}_{S}^{*}A_{0}+\widetilde{\chi}_{t}a_{S}&\textup{ over each $\hat{P}^{\circ}_{S}$}\end{cases}

where
\widetilde{\chi}_{t}\coloneqq\widetilde{\chi}\circ r_{t}
for a smooth non-increasing function
\widetilde{\chi}\colon\thinspace[0,\kappa]\to[0,1]
with

\displaystyle\widetilde{\chi}(s)=\begin{cases}0\textup{ for $s\geq\kappa/4$}\\
1\textup{ for $s\leq\kappa/8$.}\end{cases}

To estimate the error we first observe that the connection
\tilde{A}_{t}
agrees over
(P_{0}^{\circ})_{|r_{t}^{-1}[\kappa/4,\kappa]}
with
A_{0}
and is therefore flat in this area. Over each
\hat{P}_{S}^{\circ}
we have

F_{\tilde{A}_{t}}=\widetilde{\chi}_{t}F_{\hat{A}_{S}}+\mathop{}\!\textup{d}\widetilde{\chi}_{t}\wedge a_{S}+\tfrac{\widetilde{\chi}_{t}^{2}-\widetilde{\chi}_{t}}{2}[a_{S}\wedge a_{S}].

The curvature of
\hat{A}_{S}
can be estimated by
|\nabla^{k}F_{\hat{A}_{S}}|_{g_{\mathbb{R}}\oplus t^{2}g_{\hat{Z}_{S}}}=t^{4}\mathcal{O}(r_{t}^{-6-k})
which together with (
2.4
),
Section 3.1
, and
Section 2.1
leads to

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\chi}_{t}F_{\hat{A}_{S}}\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{t}_{S,\kappa})}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{\chi}_{t}F_{\hat{A}_{S}}\wedge(\psi_{t}-\tilde{\psi}_{t})\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{t}_{S,\kappa})}}\leq ct^{1/2}\|F_{\hat{A}_{S}}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{t}_{S,\kappa})}}\leq ct^{9/2}.

Finally, (
2.5
) implies
\|F_{\hat{A}_{t}}\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6+\vartheta},t}{}}\leq ct^{9/2-\vartheta}
.

The rest in the expression of
F_{\tilde{A}_{t}}
is supported in the region
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}\setminus\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa/8}^{t}
where the
C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta,t}
-norms for all values of
\beta
are equivalent (uniformly in
t
). This leads to

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}\widetilde{\chi}_{t}\wedge a_{S}+\tfrac{\widetilde{\chi}_{t}^{2}-\widetilde{\chi}_{t}}{2}[a_{S}\wedge a_{S}]\right)\wedge\psi_{t}\right\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6+\vartheta},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{t}_{S,\kappa})}}

\displaystyle\leq c\big(\|a_{S}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-5},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{t}_{S,\kappa})}}+\|a_{S}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-5},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{t}_{S,\kappa})}}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq c\big(t^{4}+t^{8}\big)

and in total

\|F_{\tilde{A}_{t}}\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6+\vartheta},t}{}}\leq ct^{\textup{min}\{9/2-\vartheta,4\}}.

Assume now that
\mathfrak{R}
and
\mathfrak{G}
are
H
-equivariant for some
H
-action as defined in
Section 2.1
and
Section 4
. We then obtain induced
H
-actions on
P_{0}^{\circ}
and
\hat{P}_{S}^{\circ}
(via
\tilde{\lambda}
and
\hat{\tilde{\lambda}}_{S}
). Since
\tilde{\tau}_{S}
and parallel transport are equivariant, these combine to
\hat{\tilde{\lambda}}\colon\thinspace H\to\textup{Isom}(\hat{P}_{t})
. Since
A_{0}
,
A_{S}
, and
\tilde{\chi}_{t}
are invariant under
H
, the same holds true for
\tilde{A}_{t}
.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

An improved estimate on the
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure
\phi_{t}
(cf.
Section 2.1
) of the form

\|\phi_{t}-\tilde{\phi}_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{0},t}{}}\leq ct^{\gamma}

would improve the error estimate in the previous proposition by

\|F_{\tilde{A}_{t}}\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6+\vartheta},t}{}}\leq ct^{\textup{min}\{4+\gamma-\vartheta,4\}}.

Proposition 4.7

(Pregluing for a simple family of gluing data)
.

Assume that we have a set of gluing data
\mathfrak{G}\coloneqq\{(\pi\colon\thinspace P_{0}\to Y_{0},A_{0}),(\tilde{\mathtt{J}}_{S},\pi_{S}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{S}\to\hat{Z}_{S},\tilde{\tau}_{S},\hat{A}_{S},\hat{\tilde{\rho}}_{S})_{S\in\mathcal{S}}\}
compatible with a chosen set of resolution data. Assume further that
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}
is a compact interval and that we have

•

a smooth family
(A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}\subset\mathcal{A}(P_{0})
of flat connections on
P_{0}
,

•

for every
S\in\mathcal{S}
a smooth family
(\hat{A}_{S}+\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}\subset\mathcal{A}((\mathbb{R}\times\hat{P}_{S})/\Lambda_{S})
of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons,

and that these families of connections satisfy for every
S\in\mathcal{S}
and
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
:

•

\tilde{\mathtt{J}}_{S}^{*}a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}=(\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
over
\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa}\setminus\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa/8}
,

•

the restriction of
(\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
to
(\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus B_{\kappa/8}(0))/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
satisfies

\delta_{\lambda}^{*}((\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}})=(\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}

for every
\lambda\geq 1
, where
\delta_{\lambda}\colon\thinspace(\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus B_{\kappa/8}(0))/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}\to(\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus B_{\lambda\kappa/8}(0))/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
denotes the dilation by
\lambda
in
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S}
-direction (i.e.
\delta_{\lambda}([s,z])\coloneqq[s,\lambda z]
).

Then there exists for each
t\in(0,T_{K})
a bundle
\pi_{t}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{t}\to\hat{Y}_{t}
together with a smooth family of connections
(\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\hat{P}_{t})
such that for each
L,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and
\vartheta\geq 0
there exists a (
t
-independent) constant
c_{pI}>0
with

\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}F_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}})\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6+\vartheta},t}{}}\leq c_{pI}t^{\min\{9/2-\vartheta,4\}}

and

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}})\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{}}\leq c_{pI}\|\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k+1},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}

for any
\underline{a}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{7}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
and
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}L^{*}_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}})\underline{b}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{}}\leq c_{pI}\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{k+1},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}

for any
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
and
\beta\in\mathbb{R}

for any
\ell\in\{0,\dots,L\}
. Here we again denote by
\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}F_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\in\Omega^{2}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
and
\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{f,t}}\in\textup{Lin}(C^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta,t},C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1,t})
the
\ell
-th derivative of the family
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto F_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\in\Omega^{2}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
and
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{f,t}}\in\textup{Lin}(C^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta,t},C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1,t})
. Furthermore, the covariant derivatives in the
\|\cdot\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}
-norms in the estimates above are taken with respect to
\tilde{A}_{t}
and the Levi-Civita connection with respect to
\tilde{g}_{t}
.

In addition: if
\mathfrak{G}
is a set of
H
-equivariant gluing data and both
a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
and
\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
are
H
-invariant for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
, then so is
\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
.

Remark 4.8
.

The first condition on the families of connections in the previous proposition ensures that the respective connections match over the gluing region. The second condition says that on the complement of
\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa/8}\subset(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
, the (asymptotic) 1-form
(\tau_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
is dilation-invariant. This is in particular satisfies whenever the 1-form
(\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
is on the complement of
\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa/8}
pulled back from
(\mathbb{R}\times S^{5}/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}\subset(\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
.

Remark 4.9
.

As in the formulation of
Section 3.2
we restricted ourself in the previous proposition to families parametrised by an interval
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}
, because this simplifies the notation and is the relevant case for our examples in
Section 6
. However,
Section 4
can be generalised to any
compact
manifold
\mathfrak{F}
(possibly with boundary). Similarly, one can weaken the condition that
(\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
is dilation-invariant on the complement of
\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa/8}
and agrees with
\tilde{J}_{S}^{*}a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
in the gluing region to respective asymptotic conditions.

Remark 4.10
.

If
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
is of the form
b=(0,\xi)
for some
\xi\in\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
, then
L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}
acts as
L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{f,t}}^{*}\underline{b}=\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\xi
. The previous proposition implies therefore the estimate

\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\xi\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{}}\leq c_{pI}\|\xi\|_{C^{{k+1},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}\textup{ for any $\xi\in\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})$ and $\beta\in\mathbb{R}$.}

Proof of
Section 4
.

First, we construct
\hat{\pi}_{t}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{t}\to\hat{Y}_{t}
and
\tilde{A}_{t}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{P}_{t})
from
\mathfrak{G}
as in
Section 4
. Next, we define
\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}\coloneqq\begin{cases}a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}\textup{ over $Y_{0}\setminus\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{\kappa/8})$,}\\
\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}\textup{ over any $\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}$.}\end{cases}

Our conditions ensure that
\tilde{\mathtt{J}}^{*}_{S}a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}=(\widetilde{t\tau_{S}})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
over
\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa}\setminus\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa/8}
for any
t>0
. Thus,
\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
is well-defined and smooth. Furthermore, the
H
-invariance (in the presence of an
H
-action) of
\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
follows immediately from the
H
-invariance of
a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
and
\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
.

We will now prove all pregluing-estimates for
L=0
. The estimates for higher
L
can be proven analogously.

For the error estimate we note as in the proof of
Section 4
that over
P_{0}^{\circ}
, the connection
\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
agrees with the flat connection
A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
. Over each
\hat{P}_{S}^{\circ}
we have

F_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}=\widetilde{\chi}_{t}F_{\hat{A}_{S}+\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}+\mathop{}\!\textup{d}\widetilde{\chi}_{t}\wedge a_{S}+\tfrac{\widetilde{\chi}_{t}^{2}-\widetilde{\chi}_{t}}{2}[a_{S}\wedge a_{S}]+(1-\widetilde{\chi}_{t})(\widetilde{t\tau_{S}})^{*}\tilde{\mathtt{J}}_{S}^{*}\big(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}}a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}+\tfrac{1}{2}[a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}\wedge a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}]\big)

(where
a_{S}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Z}_{S},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{S}})
is as in the previous proof defined by
\hat{A}_{S}-(\widetilde{t\tau_{S}})^{*}\tilde{\mathtt{J}}_{S}^{*}A_{0}
). Note that the last term vanishes because
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}}a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}+\tfrac{1}{2}[a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}\wedge a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}]=F_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}=0
and that the second and the third term can be estimated as in the proof of
Section 4
. Furthermore, over the complement of
\tau_{S}^{-1}(\mathcal{V}_{S,\kappa/8})\subset(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
we have

\displaystyle F_{\hat{A}_{S}+\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}

\displaystyle=F_{\hat{A}_{S}}+(\widetilde{t\tau_{S}})^{*}\tilde{\mathtt{J}}_{S}^{*}\big(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}}a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}+\tfrac{1}{2}[a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}\wedge a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}]\big)+[a_{S},\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}]

\displaystyle=F_{\hat{A}_{S}}+[a_{S},\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}].

(4.1)

Since
\delta_{\lambda}^{*}(\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}(\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}})=(\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}(\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}})
for any
\lambda\geq 1
over

(\mathbb{R}_{s}\times(\mathbb{R}_{r>\kappa/8}\times S^{5}/\Gamma_{S}))/\Lambda_{S}=(\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus B_{\kappa/8}(0))/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S},

we may write
(\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
over this domain as

(\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}=\textup{pr}_{S^{5}/\Gamma_{S}}^{*}(a_{r,\mathfrak{f}})\tfrac{\mathop{}\!\textup{d}r}{r}+\textup{pr}_{S^{5}/\Gamma_{S}}^{*}(a_{S^{5}/\Gamma_{S},\mathfrak{f}})+\textup{pr}_{S^{5}/\Gamma_{S}}^{*}(a_{s,\mathfrak{f}})\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s

where
a_{r,\mathfrak{f}},a_{s,\mathfrak{f}}\in\Omega^{0}((\mathbb{R}\times S^{5}/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
and
a_{S^{5}/\Gamma_{S},\mathfrak{f}}\in\Omega^{1}((\mathbb{R}\times S^{5}/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
satisfies
i_{\partial_{s}}(a_{S^{5}/\Gamma_{S},\mathfrak{f}})=0
and where
\textup{pr}_{S^{5}/\Gamma_{S}}\colon\thinspace(\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}\to(\mathbb{R}\times S^{5}/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
denotes the radial projection. In the following we will drop the pullback by
\textup{pr}_{S^{5}/\Gamma_{S}}
from our notation.

Since
\mathfrak{F}
and
(\mathbb{R}\times S^{5}/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
are compact, the explicit form of
(\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
over the region
(\mathbb{R}\times B_{t^{-1}\kappa}(0)/\Gamma_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
implies that

|\nabla^{k}(a_{r,\mathfrak{f}}\tfrac{\mathop{}\!\textup{d}r}{r}+a_{S^{5}/\Gamma_{S},\mathfrak{f}})|_{g_{\mathbb{R}}\oplus t^{2}g_{\mathbb{C}^{3}}}\leq cw_{t}^{-k-1}

and

|\nabla^{k}(a_{s,\mathfrak{f}}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s)|_{g_{\mathbb{R}}\oplus t^{2}g_{\mathbb{C}^{3}}}\leq cw_{t}^{-k}

and by (
2.5
)

\|\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-1},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}\leq\|a_{r,\mathfrak{f}}\tfrac{\mathop{}\!\textup{d}r}{r}+a_{S^{5}/\Gamma_{S},\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-1},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}+\|a_{s,\mathfrak{f}}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{0},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}\leq c.

(4.2)

Using (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) we can bound as in the proof of
Section 4

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\chi}_{t}F_{\hat{A}_{S}+\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}

\displaystyle\leq ct^{1/2}\|F_{\hat{A}_{S}+\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}

\displaystyle\leq ct^{1/2}\big(\|F_{\hat{A}_{S}}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-6},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}+\|a_{S}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-5},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}\cdot\|\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-1},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq ct^{9/2}

which together with (
2.5
) implies the pregluing estimate.

In order to prove
\|L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{}}\leq c\|\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k+1},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}
for every
\underline{a}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})\oplus\Omega^{7}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
we first note that

L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\underline{a}=L_{\tilde{A}_{t}}\underline{a}+\{\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}},\underline{a}\}

where
\{\cdot,\cdot\}
denotes a bilinear algebraic operation. Since we consider
C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta,t}
-norms on
\hat{Y}_{t}
in which the derivative is taken with respect to
\tilde{A}_{t}
, it suffices to estimate

\|\{\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}},\underline{a}\}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{}}\leq c\|\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-1},t}{}}\cdot\|\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}.

Since
\mathfrak{F}
is compact and the restriction of the weighted norm
\|\cdot\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}
to
\hat{Y}_{t}\setminus\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}^{t}
is uniformly (in
t\in(0,T)
) equivalent to the ordinary Hölder norm on
Y_{0}\setminus\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{\kappa})
, we have
\|\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-1},t}{(\hat{Y}_{t}\setminus\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}^{t})}}\leq c
. Together with (
4.2
) this implies
\|\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-1},t}{}}\leq c
and finishes the estimate on
L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}
. Since
L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}=*L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}*
and
\|*\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{0},t}{}}<c
, this also implies the estimate on
L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}
.
∎

5
Linear analysis

This section establishes
Point 2
of
Section 3.2
and
Point 4
of
Section 3.2
for the approximate instantons constructed in
Section 4
and
Section 4
.

5.1
A model operator on
\mathbb{R}\times\text{ALE}

Let
(\hat{Z},\tau,\hat{\omega},\hat{\Omega})
be a Calabi–Yau ALE
3
-fold,
\pi\colon\thinspace\hat{P}\to\hat{Z}
be a locally framed principal
G
-bundle together with a Hermitian Yang–Mills connection
A\in\mathcal{A}_{-5}^{\textup{HYM}}(\hat{P},A_{\infty})
.

Definition 5.1
.

For any
q\in\{0,1,2,3\}
define
*_{\hat{\Omega}}\colon\thinspace\Lambda^{0,q}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}\to\Lambda^{0,3-q}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}
by demanding that

\eta_{1}\wedge*_{\hat{\Omega}}\eta_{2}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{8}}\langle\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{\bar{\Omega}}

for any
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in\Lambda^{0,q}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}
.

Remark 5.2
.

The map
*_{\hat{\Omega}}
is an anticomplex version of the Hodge star operator and appeared, for example, in
[
DT98
, Section 2]
.

The following identities can be proven either by wedging with a
(0,q)
-form and inserting
\textup{vol}=\frac{i}{8}\hat{\Omega}\wedge\hat{\bar{\Omega}}
or as for the ordinary Hodge star operator.

Lemma 5.3
.

This operator satisfies the following identities:

\displaystyle*\bar{\eta}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{(-1)^{(3-q)+1}i}{\sqrt{8}}\hat{\Omega}\wedge*_{\hat{\Omega}}\eta

\displaystyle*_{\hat{\Omega}}(i\eta)

\displaystyle=-i*_{\hat{\Omega}}\eta

\displaystyle*_{\hat{\Omega}}^{2}\eta

\displaystyle=(-1)^{q(3-q)}\eta

\displaystyle\langle*_{\hat{\Omega}}\eta_{1},*_{\hat{\Omega}}\eta_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\overline{\langle\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\rangle}

\displaystyle({*_{\hat{\Omega}}\bar{\partial}*_{\hat{\Omega}}})\eta

\displaystyle=(-1)^{q}\bar{\partial}^{*}\eta

where
\eta,\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in\Omega^{0,q}(\hat{Z},\mathbb{C})
.

We now consider the product
\hat{Y}\coloneqq\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}
equipped with its
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure (
2.1
) and with the pullback bundle (again denoted by)
\pi\colon\thinspace\hat{P}\to\hat{Y}
. By
Section 3.1
, the pullback of
A
to this bundle is a
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton which we will again denote by
A
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Consider the isomorphisms of vector bundles over
\hat{Y}
(where we suppress any pullbacks in our notation) given by

\displaystyle T^{*}\mathbb{R}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus T^{*}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus\Lambda^{7}T^{*}\hat{Y}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}

\displaystyle\to\Lambda^{0,1}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus\Lambda^{0,3}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}

\displaystyle\big(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s\otimes\xi_{1},a,\textup{vol}_{\hat{Y}}\otimes\xi_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(\sqrt{2}a^{0,1},*_{\hat{\Omega}}(\xi_{1}+i\xi_{2})\big)

and

\displaystyle T^{*}\mathbb{R}\otimes\Lambda^{5}T^{*}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus\Lambda^{6}T^{*}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}

\displaystyle\to\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\Lambda^{0,2}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}

\displaystyle\big(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s\otimes b,\textup{vol}_{\hat{Z}}\otimes\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(\xi_{2}-i\xi_{1},\tfrac{1}{2}i_{\hat{\Omega}^{\sharp}}b\big),

where
i_{\hat{\Omega}^{\sharp}}
denotes the insertion of
\hat{\Omega}^{\sharp}\in\Lambda^{3,0}T_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}
.

Under the induced isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})\oplus\Omega^{7}(\hat{Y},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})

\displaystyle\cong\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y},\Lambda^{0,\textup{odd}}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})

\displaystyle\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})

\displaystyle\cong\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y},\Lambda^{0,\textup{even}}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})

the linearised instanton operator and its adjoint become

\displaystyle L_{A}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&-*_{\hat{\Omega}}\partial_{s}\\
*_{\hat{\Omega}}\partial_{s}&0\end{pmatrix}+\sqrt{2}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}&0\\
\bar{\partial}_{A}&\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle L_{A}^{*}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&-*_{\hat{\Omega}}\partial_{s}\\
*_{\hat{\Omega}}\partial_{s}&0\end{pmatrix}+\sqrt{2}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\partial}_{A}&\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\\
0&\bar{\partial}_{A}\end{pmatrix}.

These satisfy

L_{A}L_{A}^{*}=-\partial_{s}^{2}+2{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{A}}}_{|\Omega^{0,\textup{even}}}.

Proof.

As a first step, we identifiy

\displaystyle T^{*}\mathbb{R}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus T^{*}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus\Lambda^{7}T^{*}\hat{Y}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}

\displaystyle\to\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus T^{*}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}

\displaystyle\big(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s\otimes\xi_{1},a,\textup{vol}_{\hat{Y}}\otimes\xi_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},a\big)

and

\displaystyle T^{*}\mathbb{R}\otimes\Lambda^{5}T^{*}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus\Lambda^{6}T^{*}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}

\displaystyle\to\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}\oplus\Lambda^{5}T^{*}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}}

\displaystyle\big(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s\otimes b,\textup{vol}_{\hat{Z}}\otimes\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},b\big).

The operator then becomes

\displaystyle L_{A}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\partial_{s}&\Lambda_{\hat{\omega}}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}\\
-\partial_{s}&0&\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}^{*}\\
-*_{\hat{Z}}\hat{I}^{*}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}&-*_{\hat{Z}}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}&*_{\hat{Z}}\hat{I}^{*}\partial_{s}+\textup{Re}\thinspace\hat{\Omega}\wedge\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}\end{pmatrix}

where both
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}
and
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A}^{*}
are taken over
\hat{Z}
. This makes use of the identities
\frac{1}{2}*_{\hat{Z}}(\hat{\omega}\wedge\hat{\omega}\wedge b)=\Lambda_{\hat{\omega}}b
for
b\in\Omega^{2}(\hat{Z})

and
\frac{1}{2}*_{\hat{Z}}(\hat{\omega}\wedge\hat{\omega}\wedge a)=-I^{*}a
for
a\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Z})
(cf.
[
Huy05
, Chapter 1.2]
). Going through the other identifications is straight forward and uses the identities given in the previous lemma.
∎

The following lemma immediately follows from
[
Wal13a
, Lemma 7.10]
:

Lemma 5.5
.

Identify

\displaystyle\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})

\displaystyle\cong\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y},\Lambda^{0,\textup{even}}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})

as in the previous lemma and assume that
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y},\Lambda^{0,\textup{even}}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})
satisfies
L_{A}L_{A}^{*}\underline{b}=0
and
\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0}}{}}<\infty
. Then
\underline{b}
is constant in the
\mathbb{R}
direction.

Next, we introduce the following weight functions on
\hat{Y}=\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}
:

w(s,z)\coloneqq 1+r(z)\quad\text{and}\quad w((s_{1},z_{1}),(s_{2},z_{2}))\coloneqq\min\{w(s_{1},z_{1}),w(s_{2},z_{2})\},

and for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
the following weighted norms:

\displaystyle[f]_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{(U)}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{d(x,y)\leq w(x,y)}w(x,y)^{\alpha-\beta}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|}{d(x,y)^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{{0}}_{{\beta}}{(U)}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big\|w^{-\beta}f\big\|_{C^{{0}}{(U)}}

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{(U)}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left\|\nabla^{j}f\right\|_{C^{{0}}_{{\beta-j}}{(U)}}+\left[\nabla^{j}f\right]_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta-j}}{(U)}}.

Proposition 5.6
.

Identify

\displaystyle\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})

\displaystyle\cong\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y},\Lambda^{0,\textup{even}}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})

as in the previous lemmas and assume that
\underline{b}\coloneqq(\xi,\eta)\in C^{k,\alpha}_{\ell oc}(\Lambda^{0,\textup{even}}T^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}\hat{Z}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})
satisfies
L_{A}L_{A}^{*}\underline{b}=0
and
\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0}}_{{\beta}}{}}<\infty
for any
\beta<0
. Then
\xi=0
and
\eta
is the pullback of an element in
*_{\hat{\Omega}}\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A,-5}
(as in
Section 2.3
).

Proof.

First note that
\underline{b}
is smooth by elliptic regularity. The previous lemma implies that
\underline{b}
is constant in
\mathbb{R}
direction and therefore
\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{A}}\underline{b}=0
. This means

\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{A}}\xi=0\quad\textup{and}\quad\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{A}}\eta=0

and therefore
\xi=0
by
Section 2.3
.

One can now argue as in the proof of
Section 2.3
: Since there are no indicial roots of
\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{A}}
contained in the interval
(-4,0)
, we have
|\nabla^{k}\eta|=\mathcal{O}(r^{-4-k})
(cf.
[
Bar86
, Proposition 1.14]

or
[
LM85
]
). Integration by parts implies
(\bar{\partial}_{A}+\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*})\eta=0
and by the last identity in
Section 5.1

(\bar{\partial}_{A}+\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*})(*_{\hat{\Omega}}\eta)=*_{\hat{\Omega}}\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\eta-*_{\hat{\Omega}}\bar{\partial}_{A}\eta=0.

By
Section 2.3
,
*_{\hat{\Omega}}\eta\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A,-5}
or, equivalently,
\eta\in*_{\hat{\Omega}}\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A,-5}
.

Somewhat alternatively, if one already has
\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1}}_{{\beta}}{}}<\infty
(which holds in the proof of
Section 5.2
where we apply this proposition), one can also argue as follows: First note that by
\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{A}}\eta=0
,
\bar{\partial}_{A}^{2}=0
and
Section 2.3
we have
\bar{\partial}_{A}\eta=0
. This implies that
\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\eta
satisfies
(\bar{\partial}_{A}+\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*})\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\eta=0
and therefore (by
Section 2.3
)
\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\eta\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A,-5}
. In particular,
|\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\eta|
decays like
r^{-5}
and since
|\eta|
decays like
r^{\beta}
with
\beta<0
, we have

0=\int_{\hat{Z}}\langle\bar{\partial}_{A}\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\eta,\eta\rangle=\int_{\hat{Z}}|\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*}\eta|^{2}.

Thus,
(\bar{\partial}_{A}+\bar{\partial}_{A}^{*})\eta=0
and as above one shows that
\eta\in*_{\hat{\Omega}}\mathcal{H}^{1}_{A,-5}
.
∎

Assume now that the ALE
3
-fold
\hat{Z}\equiv\hat{Z}_{S}
, the bundle
{\hat{P}}\equiv\hat{P}_{S}
, and the HYM connection
A\equiv\hat{A}_{S}
are part of resolution and gluing data for a flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold. As before we denote by
\hat{Y}_{t}
the manifold defined in
Section 2.1
and by
(\hat{P}_{t},\tilde{A}_{t})
the bundle and connection constructed in
Section 4
.

Next we want to compare the linearised instanton operator
L_{\tilde{A}_{t}}
over
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}
with the model operator
L_{\hat{A}_{S}}
. For this we define for
t\in(0,T_{K})
and
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
the following dilation map

\displaystyle\delta_{t,s_{0}}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S}

\displaystyle\to(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})/\Lambda_{S}

\displaystyle(s,x)

\displaystyle\mapsto[ts+s_{0},x]

and for
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and any
a\in\Omega^{\ell}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
the following rescaling:

\displaystyle\mathrm{s}_{\beta,t,s_{0}}(a)\coloneqq t^{-\beta-\ell}\delta_{t,s_{0}}^{*}a_{|\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}}.

(5.1)

We will denote the induced map on any sum of the form
\Omega^{k}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})\oplus\Omega^{\ell}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
by
\mathrm{s}_{\beta,t,s_{0}}
as well.

Lemma 5.7

(cf.
[
Wal13a
, Proposition 7.7]
)
.

For fixed
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and
\alpha\in(0,1)
there exists a constant
C
such that for every
t\in(0,T_{K})
,
a\in\Omega^{\ell}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
,
\underline{a}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})\oplus\Omega^{7}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
, and
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
the following holds:

C^{-1}\|a\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}\leq\|\mathrm{s}_{\beta,t,s_{0}}(a)\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{(\mathbb{R}\times(t\tau_{S})^{-1}(B_{\kappa}(0)/\Gamma_{S}))}}\leq C\|a\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}

\|(L_{\tilde{A}_{t}}-\mathrm{s}_{\beta-1,t,s_{0}}^{-1}L_{\hat{A}_{S}}\mathrm{s}_{\beta,t,s_{0}})(\underline{a})\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}\leq Ct^{1/2}\|\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k+1},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}

and

\|(L_{\tilde{A}_{t}}^{*}-\mathrm{s}_{\beta-1,t,s_{0}}^{-1}L_{\hat{A}_{S}}^{*}\mathrm{s}_{\beta,t,s_{0}})(\underline{b})\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}\leq Ct^{1/2}\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{k+1},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}.

Proof.

The proof is the same as
[
Wal13a
, Proposition 7.7]
:
\delta_{t,s_{0}}
pulls back the metric
g_{\mathbb{R}}\oplus t^{2}g_{\hat{Z}_{S}}
to
t^{2}(g_{\mathbb{R}}\oplus g_{\hat{Z}_{S}})
. Thus, if we would calculate the norm on
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}
with respect to the product metric
g_{\mathbb{R}}\oplus t^{2}g_{\hat{Z}_{S}}
, then the first chain of inequalities would actually hold as an equality. However, the norm on
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}
is calculated with respect to the metric
\tilde{g}_{t}
which only agrees with
g_{\mathbb{R}}\oplus t^{2}g_{\hat{Z}_{S}}
up to
\mathcal{O}(t^{6})
; thus the inequality.

For the second, we calculate

\displaystyle L_{\hat{A}_{S}}s_{\beta,t,s_{0}}(a,\xi)=t^{-\beta-7}\begin{pmatrix}t^{6}\hat{\psi}_{S}\wedge\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\hat{A}_{S}}\delta_{t,s_{0}}^{*}a&-\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\hat{A}_{S}}^{*}\delta_{t,s_{0}}^{*}\xi\\
t^{6}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\hat{A}_{S}}^{*}\delta_{t,s_{0}}^{*}a&0\end{pmatrix}

(where
\hat{\psi}_{S}
denotes the product
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure on
\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S}
and
*_{S}
its associated Hodge-star operator) and

\displaystyle s_{\beta-1,t,s_{0}}L_{\tilde{A}_{t}}(a,\xi)=t^{-\beta-5}\begin{pmatrix}\delta_{t,s_{0}}^{*}\big(\psi_{t}\wedge\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{t}}a\big)&-\delta_{t,s_{0}}^{*}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{t}}^{*}\xi\\
t^{6}\delta_{t,s_{0}}^{*}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{t}}^{*}a&0\end{pmatrix}.

Again, if everything in the definition of
L_{\tilde{A}_{t}}
came from a product structure (i.e. on
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t}
:
\tilde{A}_{t}=\hat{A}_{S}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{t}=\hat{\psi}_{S,t}
where
\hat{\psi}_{S,t}
is associated to
g_{\mathbb{R}}\oplus t^{2}g_{\hat{Z}_{S}}
), then both operators would agree. As in the previous situation one needs to estimate how far all entries in
L_{\tilde{A}_{t}}
are from those defined from product structures. This uses
Section 2.1
which explains the factor
t^{1/2}
in the estimate. The same holds true for
L_{\tilde{A}_{t}}^{*}
.
∎

For the rest of this section we make the following assumption:

Assumption 5.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}
be a compact interval and
(\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}
be the family of connections over
\hat{P}_{t}
that arises from the construction in
Section 4
. Assume that over
S\in\mathcal{S}
the family of 1-forms
\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}\in\Omega^{1}((\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})/\Lambda_{S},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{S}})
in the construction of
Section 4
is of the form
\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}=\hat{\xi}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s
where both
\hat{\xi}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}\in\Omega^{0}((\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})/\Lambda_{S},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{S}})
and
(\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}\hat{\xi}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
are constant.

Remark 5.9
.

As in
Section 3.2
and
Section 4
, these assumptions can be weakened at the cost of a heavier notation.

For
t>0
and
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
we define
L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}
to be the linearised instanton operator associated to the connection
\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{P}_{S})
over
\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S}
.

Proposition 5.10
.

For fixed
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and
\alpha\in(0,1)
there exists a constant
C
such that for every
t\in(0,T_{K})
,
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
,
\underline{a}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})\oplus\Omega^{7}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
, and
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\|(L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}-\mathrm{s}_{\beta-1,t,s_{0}}^{-1}L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}\mathrm{s}_{\beta,t,s_{0}})(\underline{a})\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{1/2}\|\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k+1},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}

\displaystyle\|(L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}-\mathrm{s}_{\beta-1,t,s_{0}}^{-1}L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\mathrm{s}_{\beta,t,s_{0}})(\underline{b})\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{1/2}\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{k+1},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{(\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{S,\kappa}^{t})}}.

Proof.

This is proven as in
Section 5.1
. The
t
-dependence of the model connection
\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
over
\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S}
is a consequence of
\delta_{t,s_{0}}^{*}(\hat{\xi}_{S,f}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s)=t\hat{\xi}_{S,f}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s
.
∎

Proposition 5.11
.

For fixed
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and
\alpha\in(0,1)
, there exists a
c_{SI}>0
and
0<T_{SI}<T_{K}
such that for every
t\in(0,T_{SI})
,
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
, and
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{S}})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{S}})
the estimate

\displaystyle\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{k+2},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{(\mathbb{R}\times\tau^{-1}(B_{t^{-1}\kappa/2}(0)))}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{SI}\big(\|L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-2}}{(\mathbb{R}\times\tau^{-1}(B_{t^{-1}\kappa}(0)))}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0}}_{{\beta}}{(\mathbb{R}\times\tau^{-1}(B_{t^{-1}\kappa}(0)))}}\big)

holds over
\mathbb{R}\times\tau_{S}^{-1}(B_{t^{-1}\kappa}(0))\subset\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S}
.

Proof sketch.

The Schauder estimate for the fixed translation- and asymptotically dilation-invariant operator
L_{\hat{A}_{S}}L_{\hat{A}_{S}}^{*}
is standard and proven in
[
Wal13a
, Proposition 7.11]
(see also
[
NW73
, Theorem 3.1]
and
[
Bar86
, Proposition 1.6]
). The proof for
L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}
(which is not asymptotically dilation-invariant anymore due to the
t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
-term) and the observation that
c_{SI}
can be chosen independently of
\mathfrak{f}
is similar and we only sketch the argument:

When restricted to
\mathbb{R}\times K
for a fixed compact set
K\subset\hat{Z}_{S}
, the operator
L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\in\textup{Lin}(C^{k+2,\alpha}_{\beta},C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-2})
is for small
t<T_{SI}
an arbitrarily small perturbation of
L_{\hat{A}_{S}}L_{\hat{A}_{S}}^{*}
. The estimate for
L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}
over
\mathbb{R}\times K
follows therefore from the
\mathfrak{f}
-independent Schauder estimate of
L_{\hat{A}_{S}}L^{*}_{\hat{A}_{S}}
.

Choosing
K\subset\hat{Z}_{S}
in the previous step sufficiently large, we may assume that over
(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})\setminus(\mathbb{R}\times K)
the connections
\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
and
(\tilde{\tau}_{S})^{*}A_{\infty}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}
are as close as we wish. For any
p\coloneqq(s,z)\in(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})\setminus(\mathbb{R}\times K)
and
R\coloneqq\tfrac{1}{8}(1+r(z))
we can now prove a local estimate

\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{k+2},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{(B_{R}(p))}}\leq c\big(\|L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-2}}{(B_{2R}(p))}}+\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0}}_{{\beta}}{(B_{2R}(p))}}\big)

over the ball
B_{R}(p)
as follows: After rescaling (and identifying
\hat{Z}_{S}\setminus K\cong(\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S})\setminus\tau_{S}(K)
) this estimate becomes equivalent to

\displaystyle\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{k+2},\alpha}{(B_{1}(0))}}\leq c\big(\|L_{A_{\infty}+tR(\tau_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{A_{\infty}+tR(\tau_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}{(B_{2}(0)}}+\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0}}{(B_{2}(0))}}\big)

(5.2)

over the fixed balls
B_{1}(0),B_{2}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}
(see also
Section 5.1
below). If now
p\in(\mathbb{R}\times\tau_{S}^{-1}(B_{t^{-1}\kappa}(0)))
, then
tR\leq T_{SI}+\kappa
and
L_{A_{\infty}+tR(\tau_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{A_{\infty}+tR(\tau_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}
differs from
L_{A_{\infty}}L_{A_{\infty}}^{*}
only by lower order terms which are uniformly bounded. The estimate (
5.2
) follows therefore from the standard interior Schauder estimate.

The estimate over
\mathbb{R}\times K
and over each
B_{R}(p)
for
p\in(\mathbb{R}\times\tau_{S}^{-1}(B_{t^{-1}\kappa}(0)))\setminus(\mathbb{R}\times K)
imply the estimate over
(\mathbb{R}\times\tau_{S}^{-1}(B_{t^{-1}\kappa}(0)))
.
∎

We end this section by comparing the family of operators
L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}
to its asymptotic limit
L_{A_{\infty}+t(\tau_{S})_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}
on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S}
. For this recall that
\hat{Z}_{S}
comes equipped with a resolution map
\tau_{S}\colon\thinspace\hat{Z}_{S}\to\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S}
which is covered by the framing
\tilde{\tau}_{S}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{|\hat{Z}_{S}\setminus\tau_{S}^{-1}(0)}\to(P_{\infty})_{|\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\}/\Gamma_{S}}
such that
|\nabla^{k}((\tilde{\tau}_{S})_{*}A-A_{\infty})|=\mathcal{O}(r^{-5-k})
.

For
R>0
let

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{R}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma

\displaystyle(s,z)

\displaystyle\mapsto(Rs,Rz)

and with this dilation at hand we define the following rescaling:

Definition 5.12
.

Let
\beta,R_{1},R_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
be constants with
R_{1},R_{2}>0
. For
a\in\Omega^{\ell}(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})
we define

\tilde{\mathrm{s}}_{\beta,R_{1},R_{2}}(a)\in\Omega^{\ell}\big(\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus B_{R_{1}/R_{2}}(0))/\Gamma),\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\infty}}\big)

as

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{s}}_{\beta,R_{1},R_{2}}(a)\coloneqq R_{2}^{-\beta-\ell}\tilde{\delta}_{R_{2}}^{*}\tilde{\tau}(a_{|\hat{Z}\setminus\tau^{-1}(B_{R_{1}})})

where we use parallel transport with respect to
A_{\infty}
in radial direction to identify different fibers of
\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\infty}}
. As above, we will denote the induced map on any sum of the form
\Omega^{k}(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})\oplus\Omega^{\ell}(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}})
by
\tilde{\mathrm{s}}_{\beta,R_{1},R_{2}}
as well.

Lemma 5.13
.

For fixed
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and
\alpha\in(0,1)
there exists a constant
C
such that for every
R_{1},R_{2}\geq 1
:

C^{-1}\|\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{(\mathbb{R}\times(\hat{Z}\setminus B_{R_{1}}))}}\leq\|\tilde{\mathrm{s}}_{\beta,R_{1},R_{2}}\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{(\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus B_{R_{1}/R_{2}})/\Gamma)}}\leq C\|\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{(\mathbb{R}\times(\hat{Z}\setminus B_{R_{1}}))}}

\|(L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}-\tilde{\mathrm{s}}_{\beta-1,R_{1},R_{2}}^{-1}L_{A_{\infty}+tR_{2}\tau_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}\tilde{\mathrm{s}}_{\beta,R_{1},R_{2}})\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-1}}{}}\leq CR_{1}^{-4}\|\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k+1},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{}}

\|(L_{\hat{A}_{S}+t\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}-\tilde{\mathrm{s}}_{\beta-1,R_{1},R_{2}}^{-1}L_{A_{\infty}+tR_{2}\tau_{*}\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\tilde{\mathrm{s}}_{\beta,R_{1},R_{2}})\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta-1}}{}}\leq CR_{1}^{-4}\|\underline{a}\|_{C^{{k+1},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{}}

where the family of connection
(\hat{A}_{S}+\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}
is as in
Section 5.1
.
Furthermore, the weighted
C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta}
-norm on
\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus B_{R_{1}}(0))/\Gamma_{S}
is defined as on
\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}
but with the weight function
w(s,z)\coloneqq|z|
.

This is proven as
Section 5.1
and
Section 5.1
. The factor of
R_{1}^{-4}
on the right-hand side of the second and third estimate comes from
\|A-A_{\infty}\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{-1}}{(\mathbb{R}\times(\hat{Z}\setminus B_{R_{1}}))}}\leq cR_{1}^{-4}
.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
\beta<0
and
\underline{b}\in C^{k,\alpha}_{\ell oc}(\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\infty}}\oplus\Lambda^{6}T^{*}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma)\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\infty}})
satisfy
\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0}}_{{\beta}}{}}<\infty
and
L_{A_{\infty}}L_{A_{\infty}}^{*}\underline{b}=0
. Then
\underline{b}=0.

Proof.

As in the proof of
Section 5.1
we obtain that (under an analogous identification)
\underline{b}
is constant in
\mathbb{R}
-direction and satisfies
\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{A_{\infty}}}\underline{b}=0
over
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma
. By the maximum principle
\underline{b}=0
.
∎

The linear estimate

and

in

for a simple case of connections constructed in

Assumption 5.15
.

Assume that we are in the set-up of
Section 4
where for every
S\in\mathcal{S}
and
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
, the 1-form
\hat{a}_{S,\mathfrak{f}}\in\Omega^{1}((\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})/\Lambda_{S},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{S}})
is as in

. Furthermore, assume that

1.

for every

the only
H
-invariant element in

is
0
,

2.

for each Hermitian Yang–Mills connection

one of the following holds:

•

either

is

, where

was defined in

and

in

,

•

alternatively, let

consist of all elements

with

and assume that the action

(where

. We assume that the only

-invariant element in

is
0
.

Proposition 5.16
.

For
t\in(0,T_{K})
let
\pi_{t}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{t}\to\hat{Y}_{t}
and

The proof of this proposition is very similar to
[

, Proposition 8.1]
:

Proposition 5.17
.

For
t\in(0,T_{K})
let
\pi_{t}\colon\thinspace\hat{P}_{t}\to\hat{Y}_{t}
and
(\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\hat{P}_{t})

. Assume further, that the 1-forms

in the construction of

. Then there exists for any

,

,

we have

Proof.

it suffices to prove this over the two regions
r_{t}^{-1}([0,\kappa/2])
and

separately. Over

from

,
Section 5.1
, and
Section 5.1
.

The metric
\tilde{g}_{t}
and the family of connections

converges in

for any fixed

to

and
(A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}
. Since

is compact, the coefficients in
