of a fixed size.
∎

The previous proposition reduces the proof of
Section 5.2
to the following:

Proposition 5.18
.

Assume that we are in the setup of
Section 5.2
. For any
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\beta\in(-4,0)
there are constants
0<T_{le}^{\prime}<T_{K}
and
c_{le}^{\prime}>0
such that for all
t\in(0,T^{\prime}_{le})
,
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
and
H
-invariant
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
we have

\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0}}_{{\beta},t}{}}\leq c\|L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta-2},t}{}}

Proof.

In various places throughout the proof it will be necessary to take a subsequence of a certain sequence. By abuse of notation we will tacitly denote this subsequence by the same letter as the original sequence.

Assume that such a constant does not exist to produce a contradiction. Then there exist sequences
(\underline{b}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of
H
-invariant sections,
(t_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,T_{K})
, and
(\mathfrak{f}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathfrak{F}
such that

t_{n}\to 0,\quad\|\underline{b}_{n}\|_{C^{{0}}_{{\beta},t}{}}=1,\quad\textup{and}\quad\|L_{\tilde{A}_{t_{n}}+\tilde{a}_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{t_{n}}+\tilde{a}_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}}}^{*}\underline{b}_{n}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta-2},t}{}}\to 0.

First, note that because
\mathfrak{F}
is compact,
\mathfrak{f}_{n}\to\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
. Furthermore, the previous proposition implies

\|\underline{b}_{n}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}\leq 2c.

(5.3)

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
pick a
y_{n}\in\hat{Y}_{t_{n}}
such that
w_{t_{n}}(y_{n})^{-\beta}|\underline{b}_{n}(y_{n})|=1
.

Case 1:
There exists an
\varepsilon>0
such that
\limsup r_{t_{n}}(y_{n})=\varepsilon
.

For each
t\in(0,T_{K})
we use
t\tau\colon\thinspace\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}^{t}\to\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}
(as defined prior to
Section 2.1
) to identify
\hat{Y}_{t}\setminus(t\tau)^{-1}(\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S)
with
Y_{0}\setminus\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S
and analogously

\hat{P}_{t|\hat{Y}_{t}\setminus(t\tau)^{-1}(\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S)}\cong{P_{0}}_{|Y_{0}\setminus\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S}.

Note that both identifications are
H
-equivariant.

We can therefore regard the restrictions
{\underline{b}_{n}}_{|Y_{t}\setminus(t\tau)^{-1}(\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S)}
as a sequence on
Y_{0}\setminus\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S
. Pick an exhaustion

\overline{Y_{0}\setminus U}\coloneqq K_{1}\subset K_{2}\subset K_{3}\subset\dots\subset Y_{0}\setminus\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S

by
H
-invariant compact subsets. Since on every
K_{i}
the metric
\tilde{g}_{t}
converges in
C^{\infty}
to
g_{0}
(by the construction of
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
), (
5.3
) implies that
\|{\underline{b}_{n}}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}{(K_{i})}}
is uniformly bounded. The Arzelà–Ascoli Theorem gives therefore rise to a converging subsequence on
K_{i}
. Using a diagonal sequence, we obtain an
H
-invariant
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(Y_{0}\setminus\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S,\mathfrak{g}_{P_{0}})\oplus\Omega^{0}(Y_{0}\setminus\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S,\mathfrak{g}_{P_{0}})
such that
\underline{b}_{n}\to\underline{b}
in
C^{2}_{\textup{loc}}
. This limit satisfies

|\underline{b}|\leq c\cdot d(\cdot,S)^{\beta}\quad\textup{and}\quad|\nabla\underline{b}|\leq c\cdot d(\cdot,S)^{\beta-1}

(5.4)

and since
(\tilde{A}_{t_{n}}+\tilde{a}_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}})|_{K_{i}}\to({A_{0}}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}})|_{K_{i}}
,

L_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\underline{b}=0.

The assumption
\beta>-4
and (
5.4
) imply that
L_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\underline{b}=0
in the sense of distributions over the entire
Y_{0}
. By elliptic regularity
\underline{b}
is smooth. Furthermore,
Section 5.2
and integration by parts implies that the only
H
-invariant element in the kernel of
L_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}
is the trivial section, therefore
\underline{b}=0
. However, since
\limsup r_{t_{n}}(y_{n})=\varepsilon
, there exists a converging subsequence
y_{n}\to y\in Y_{0}\setminus\cup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}S
with
\textup{d}(x,S)=\varepsilon
. At this point

|\underline{b}|(x)=\varepsilon^{\beta}>0

which is a contradiction.

Case 2:

\limsup r_{t_{n}}(y_{n})=0
.

We can assume that
(y_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is only contained inside one
\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{t}_{S,\kappa}
. We write each
y_{n}
as
[s_{n},z_{n}]\in(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})/\Lambda_{S}
and distinguish the following two scenarios:

Case 2a:

\liminf r(z_{n})<\infty
.

First, pick a subsequence
(y_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
z_{n}\to z\in\hat{Z}_{S}
. For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
we define
\tilde{\underline{b}}_{n}\coloneqq\mathrm{s}_{n}(\underline{b}_{n})
where
\mathrm{s}_{n}\coloneqq\mathrm{s}_{\beta,t_{n},s_{n}}
is the rescaling map defined in (
5.1
). By (
5.3
) and
Section 5.1
these satisfy

\|\tilde{\underline{b}}_{n}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{(\mathbb{R}\times(t\tau_{S})^{-1}(B_{\kappa}(0)/\Gamma_{S}))}}\leq 2c^{2}\quad\textup{and}\quad|w^{-\beta}\tilde{\underline{b}}_{n}|(0,z_{n})\geq c^{-1}.

As in the first case we find an element
\underline{b}\in C^{2}(\Lambda^{6}T^{*}(\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}_{S})\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{S}}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{S}})
with
\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{}}<\infty
such that
\tilde{\underline{b}}_{n}\to\underline{b}
in
C^{2}_{\textup{loc}}
. Because
t_{n}\to 0
,
Section 5.1
implies that
L_{\hat{A}_{S}}L_{\hat{A}_{S}}^{*}\underline{b}=0
and by
Section 5.1
,
\underline{b}
is pulled back from an element in
*_{\hat{\Omega}_{S}}\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\hat{A}_{S}}
.

By
Section 5.2
, either
*_{\hat{\Omega}_{S}}\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\hat{A}_{S}}=0
, or
H_{S}
acts on
\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}
via
\textup{Id}\times\hat{\vartheta}_{S}
and the maps
\delta_{t,s_{n}}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{R}\times\hat{Z}\to\hat{U}^{t}_{S}
in the definition of the rescaling map (
5.1
) are all
H_{S}
-equivariant. Each
\tilde{\underline{b}}_{n}
is therefore
H_{S}
-invariant and the same holds therefore true for
\underline{b}
. In either case,
Section 5.2
implies that
\underline{b}=0
which again contradicts
|\underline{b}|(0,z)\geq c^{-1}w^{\beta}(z)>0
.

Case 2b:

\liminf r(z_{n})=\infty
.

We define
\tilde{\underline{b}}_{n}
as in the previous case and rescale a second time:

\tilde{\underline{b}}^{\prime}_{n}\coloneqq\tilde{\mathrm{s}}_{\beta,\sqrt{r_{n}},r_{n}}(\tilde{\underline{b}}_{n})

where
r_{n}\coloneqq r(z_{n})
and
\tilde{\mathrm{s}}_{\beta,\sqrt{r_{n}},r_{n}}
is the map defined in
Section 5.1
. By
Section 5.1
, this sequence satisfies

\|\tilde{\underline{b}}^{\prime}_{n}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{}}\leq 2c^{2}\quad\textup{and}\quad|\tilde{\underline{b}}_{n}^{\prime}|(0,z^{\prime}_{n})\geq c^{-1}

where
z^{\prime}_{n}\coloneqq\frac{\tau(z_{n})}{|\tau(z_{n})|}
. As before, we obtain a subsequence
(\tilde{\underline{b}}^{\prime}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\underline{b}\in C^{2}(\Lambda^{6}T^{*}(\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})/\Gamma_{S})\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\infty}}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\infty}})
with
\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{\beta}}{}}<\infty
such that
\tilde{\underline{b}}^{\prime}_{n}\to\underline{b}
in
C^{2}_{\textup{loc}}
. Since
r_{n}t_{n}=r_{t_{n}}(y_{n})\to 0
,
Section 5.1
implies that this limit satisfies
L_{A_{\infty}}L_{A_{\infty}}^{*}\underline{b}=0
(strongly) over
\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{0\})/\Gamma_{S}
. Because of the assumption
\beta>-4
we have, in fact,
L_{A_{\infty}}L_{A_{\infty}}^{*}\underline{b}=0
over the entire
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma_{S}
in the sense of distributions. Elliptic regularity implies that
\underline{b}
is smooth and
Section 5.1
that it vanishes everywhere. However, the sequence
z_{n}^{\prime}
has an accumulation point
z
over which
|\underline{b}|(0,z)\geq c^{-1}>0
which is a contradiction.
∎

The following establishes
Point 4
of
Section 3.2
:

Proposition 5.19
.

Assume that we are in the setup of
Section 4
and assume that
\ker(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}\colon\thinspace\Omega^{0}(Y_{0},\mathfrak{g}_{P_{0}})\to\Omega^{1}(Y_{0},\mathfrak{g}_{P_{0}}))=0
for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
. Then there exists for
\beta\in(-4,0)
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
two constants
0<T_{iL}<T_{K}
and
c_{iL}>0
such that for
t\in(0,T_{iL})
and
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
any
\xi\in\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
satisfies

\|\xi\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}\leq c_{iL}\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta-2},t}{}}.

Proof.

This can be proven directly as
Section 5.2
. Somewhat alternatively, note that
L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}
acts on
(0,\xi)\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
as
(\psi_{t}\wedge[F_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}},\xi],\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\xi)
. Going through the proof of
Section 5.2
using only (not necessarily
H
-invariant)
\underline{b}_{n}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\hat{P}_{t}})
of the form
(0,\xi_{n})
and the new condition, one can check that there exist
T,c>0
such that for every
\underline{b}=(0,\xi)
,
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
and
t\in(0,T)

\displaystyle\|\xi\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}

\displaystyle=\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}\leq c\|L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta-2},t}{}}

\displaystyle\leq c\big(\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}^{*}_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta-2},t}{}}+\|F_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2},t}{}}\|\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}\big).

Since
\|F_{\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-2},t}{}}\leq ct^{1/2}
by
Section 4
, the second term can be absorbed to the left-hand side once
t
is sufficiently small.
∎

6
Examples

This sections finds examples of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons over a generalised Kummer construction resolving the
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold discovered in
[
Joy96b
, Example 18]
. For this, consider the orbifold
Y_{0}\coloneqq\mathbb{R}^{7}/\Gamma
where
\Gamma<\textup{G}_{2}\ltimes\mathbb{R}^{7}
is the crystallographic group generated by

\displaystyle\alpha(y_{1},\dots,y_{7})

\displaystyle=(y_{2},y_{3},y_{7},-y_{6},-y_{4},y_{1},y_{5})

\displaystyle\beta(y_{1},\dots,y_{7})

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{1}{2}-y_{1},\tfrac{1}{2}-y_{2},-y_{3},-y_{4},\tfrac{1}{2}+y_{5},\tfrac{1}{2}+y_{6},y_{7})

\displaystyle\tau_{1}(y_{1},\dots,y_{7})

\displaystyle=(y_{1}+1,y_{2},y_{3},y_{4},y_{5},y_{6},y_{7}).

It is not difficult to verify that the flat
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure
\phi_{0}\in\Lambda^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{7})^{*}
(associated to
(\mathbb{C}^{3},\omega_{0},\Omega_{0})
via
Section 2.1
) is preserved by
\Gamma
and induces therefore a
\textup{G}_{2}
-structure on
Y_{0}
.

Proposition 6.1
.

\Gamma
is isomorphic to the finitely presented group
\langle\,a,b,t\,|\,R\,\rangle
with relations
R
listed below. This isomorphism is defined by mapping
a,b,t
to
\alpha,\beta,\tau_{1}
, respectively. In order to state the relations
R
, we define
b_{i}\coloneqq a^{i}ba^{-i}
and
t_{i}\coloneqq a^{i}ta^{-i}
(note that
t_{i}
does in general not correspond to the map on
\mathbb{R}^{7}
which translates by
e_{i}
). Then
R
is given by:

1.

a^{7}=1

2.

b^{2}=t_{1}t_{3}

3.

[b,b_{1}]=t_{6}t_{2}^{-1}t_{3}t_{1}
,
[b,b_{2}]=tt_{5}^{-1}t_{2}^{-1}t_{1}
,
[b,b_{3}]=t_{6}^{-1}t_{2}^{-1}t_{3}t_{1}
,
[b,b_{4}]=t^{-1}t_{6}t_{5}^{-1}t_{3}
,
[b,b_{5}]=tt_{6}^{-1}t_{5}^{-1}t_{3}
,
[b,b_{6}]=t^{-1}t_{5}^{-1}t_{2}^{-1}t_{1}

4.

b_{0}b_{1}=t_{1}t_{2}^{-1}b_{3}

5.

[b,t]=t^{-2}
,
[b,t_{1}]=1
,
[b,t_{2}]=t_{2}^{-2}
,
[b,t_{3}]=1
,
[b,t_{4}]=1
,
[b,t_{5}]=t_{5}^{-2}
,
[b,t_{6}]=t_{6}^{-2}

6.

[t_{i},t_{j}]=1
for all
i,j

Proof.

First, we define a map on the free group
F\colon\thinspace\langle\,a,b,t\,\rangle\to\Gamma
by mapping
a,b,t
to
\alpha,\beta
,
\tau_{1}
, respectively. Since
\alpha
,
\beta
and
\tau_{1}
generate
\Gamma
, this map is surjective. The relations are chosen such that
F
descends to the quotient
\langle\,a,b,t\,|\,R\,\rangle
and we only have to prove that this map is also injective. Due to the relations, each element in
\langle\,a,b,t\,|\,R\,\rangle
is equivalent to one of the form

t_{0}^{i^{t}_{0}}t_{1}^{i^{t}_{1}}t_{2}^{i^{t}_{2}}t_{3}^{i^{t}_{3}}t_{4}^{i^{t}_{4}}t_{5}^{i^{t}_{5}}t_{6}^{i^{t}_{6}}b_{0}^{i^{b}_{0}}b_{1}^{i^{b}_{1}}b_{2}^{i^{b}_{2}}a^{i^{a}}

with
i^{t}_{0},\dots,i^{t}_{6}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
i^{b}_{0},i^{b}_{1},i^{b}_{2}\in\{0,1\},
and
i^{a}\in\{0,\dots,6\}
. This is also in accordance with
[
Joy96b
, Example 18]
which states that the quotient of
\Gamma
by the lattice
\mathbb{Z}^{7}<\Gamma
is of order 56. Direct inspection of the maps involved in the definition of
\Gamma
reveals that such an element is only mapped to
1
under
F
if and only if all exponents equal zero. This is again in accordance with the quotient of
\Gamma
by
\mathbb{Z}^{7}
being of order 56 as stated in
[
Joy96b
, Example 18]
.
∎

6.1
A family of flat bundles

Proposition 6.2
.

For each
\theta\in S^{1}\subset\mathbb{C}
, define the representation

f_{\theta}\colon\thinspace\langle\,a,b,t\,\rangle\to\textup{SO}(14)\cong\textup{SO}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})

by

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(a)\colon\thinspace(z_{1},\dots,z_{7})

\displaystyle\mapsto(z_{2},z_{3},z_{7},\overline{z}_{6},\overline{z}_{4},z_{1},z_{5})

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(b)\colon\thinspace(z_{1},\dots,z_{7})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\theta\overline{z}_{1},\theta\overline{z}_{2},\overline{z}_{3},\overline{z}_{4},\theta z_{5},\theta z_{6},z_{7})

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(t)\colon\thinspace(z_{1},\dots,z_{7})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\theta^{2}z_{1},z_{2},\dots,z_{7}).

Then
f_{\theta}
descends to
\Gamma\cong\langle a,b,t\,|\,R\rangle
.

Proof.

The proof reduces to checking that
f_{\theta}(a)
,
f_{\theta}(b)
, and
f_{\theta}(t)
satisfy the relations in the previously given presentation of
\Gamma
. Relations
1
,
2
,
4
, and
6
are easily verified. Here is a heuristic as to why the Relations
3
and
5
hold: Note that
f_{\theta}(b)
and
f_{\theta}(t)
originate from
\beta
and
\tau_{1}
as follows: A reflection of a coordinate is replaced by complex conjugation of the same coordinate and translation by
\frac{k}{2}
is replaced by multiplication with
\theta^{k}
. The relations given in
3
and
5
all originate from commuting reflections with translations. Commuting complex conjugation and multiplication by an element in
S^{1}
behaves (multiplicatively) analogous.
∎

Proposition 6.3
.

If
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in S^{1}
are distinct, then there is no
R\in\textup{SO}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
such that
Rf_{\theta_{1}}(\gamma)R^{-1}=f_{\theta_{2}}(\gamma)
for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
. Moreover, if
\theta\in S^{1}\setminus\{\pm 1\}
, then the only elements
R\in\textup{SO}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
with
Rf_{\theta}(\gamma)R^{-1}=f_{\theta}(\gamma)
for every
\gamma\in\Gamma
are
R=\pm\textup{id}
.

Proof.

Note that under the inclusion
\textup{SO}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})\subset\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}^{7}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})
, all
f_{\theta_{i}}(\gamma)
for
\gamma\in\Gamma
are diagonalisable. Furthermore, if
f_{\theta_{2}}(\gamma)=Rf_{\theta_{1}}(\gamma)R^{-1}
for some
R\in\textup{SO}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
, then all eigenvalues and the dimensions of their respective eigenspaces must agree. One can see that the eigenvalues of
f_{\theta_{i}}(b)
are
1,-1,\theta_{i}
and
\overline{\theta_{i}}
and that the complex dimension of their respective eigenspaces are 6, 4, 2, 2. Thus, we can only have
f_{\theta_{2}}(b)=Rf_{\theta_{1}}(b)R^{-1}
if
\theta_{2}=\theta_{1}
or
\theta_{2}=\overline{\theta_{1}}
. This proves the claim for
\theta_{1}\in\{\pm 1\}
.

We therefore assume
\theta_{1},\theta_{1}\notin\{\pm 1\}
. Direct inspection of the involved (block-) matrices reveals that we can only have
Rf_{\theta_{1}}(t)=f_{\theta_{2}}(t)R
if

\displaystyle R=\begin{pmatrix}A_{1}&0\\
0&\tilde{A}\end{pmatrix}

where
A_{1}\in\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C})
and
\tilde{A}\in\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{6})
. Similarly,
R
can only satisfy
Rf_{\theta_{1}}(a^{i}ta^{-i})=f_{\theta_{2}}(a^{i}ta^{-i})R
for all
i=0,\dots,6
if it is of the form
R=\textup{diag}(A_{1},\dots,A_{7})
with
A_{i}\in\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C})
.

The condition
Rf_{\theta_{1}}(a)R^{-1}=f_{\theta_{2}}(a)
implies that all
A_{i}
have the same determinant. Furthermore, recall from the first step of the proof that either
\theta_{2}=\theta_{1}
or
\theta_{2}=\overline{\theta_{1}}
. If
\theta_{2}=\overline{\theta_{1}}
, then the sixth equation of
Rf_{\theta_{1}}(b)R^{-1}=f_{\overline{\theta_{1}}}(b)
implies
\det(A_{7})=-1
. However, this would lead to
\det(R)=\det(A_{7})^{7}=-1
which contradicts the fact that
R
lies in
\textup{SO}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
. This shows that
f_{\theta_{1}}
and
f_{\theta_{2}}
are for
\theta_{1}\neq\theta_{2}
not conjugated in
\textup{SO}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
.

In order to prove the second claim we note that for
\theta\notin\{\pm 1\}
,
Rf_{\theta}(a^{i}ta^{-i})R^{-1}=f_{\theta}(a^{i}ta^{-i})
for every
i=0,\dots,6
again implies that
R=\textup{diag}(A_{1},\dots,A_{7})
with
A_{i}\in\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
. The third equation in
Rf_{\theta}(b)R^{-1}=f_{\theta}(b)
implies that
A_{3}
is diagonal. Finally,
Rf_{\theta}(a)R^{-1}=f_{\theta}(a)
implies that either all
A_{i}=\textup{id}
or all
A_{i}=-\textup{id}
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

We will see later that
f_{\theta}
and
f_{\overline{\theta}}
are conjugated in
\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
(cf.
Section 6.1
). Again, since the eigenvalues of any two conjugated
f_{\theta_{1}}(b)
and
f_{\theta_{2}}(b)
must agree, these are the only two possibilities of conjugated homomorphisms.

Lemma 6.5
.

The representation
f_{\theta}\colon\thinspace\Gamma\to\textup{SO}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
given in the previous proposition induces an action on
\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
(via the adjoint representation
\textup{Ad}\circ f_{\theta}
) and on
\mathbb{R}^{7}\otimes\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
(via
\textup{pr}_{1}\otimes(\textup{Ad}\circ f_{\theta})
where
\textup{pr}_{1}\colon\thinspace\Gamma<\textup{G}_{2}\ltimes\mathbb{R}^{7}\to\textup{G}_{2}
is the projection to the first entry). For
\theta\notin\{1,-1\}
, the fixpoint-set of these actions are
\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})^{\Gamma}=0
and

(\mathbb{R}^{7}\otimes\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}))^{\Gamma}=\left\langle e_{1}\otimes e_{1}\wedge f_{1}+\dots+e_{7}\otimes e_{7}\wedge f_{7}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}

where we denote by
e_{1},\dots,e_{7}
and
f_{1},\dots,f_{7}
the canonical basis of
\mathbb{R}^{7}
and
i\mathbb{R}^{7}
, respectively, and write
\mathbb{C}^{7}=\mathbb{R}^{7}+i\mathbb{R}^{7}
.

Proof.

It is not difficult to verify that once
\theta\notin\{1,-1\}
, the only elements invariant under all
f_{\theta}(t_{i})
for
i=0,\dots,6
are

\left\langle e_{1}\wedge f_{1},\dots,e_{7}\wedge f_{7}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}\subset\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})

and

\mathbb{R}^{7}\otimes\left\langle e_{1}\wedge f_{1},\dots,e_{7}\wedge f_{7}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{7}\otimes\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}).

Of these elements, none in
\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
is additionally invariant under all
f_{\theta}(b_{i})
and the only ones in
\mathbb{R}^{7}\otimes\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
are

\left\langle e_{1}\otimes e_{1}\wedge f_{1},\dots,e_{7}\otimes e_{7}\wedge f_{7}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}.

Ultimately,
f_{\theta}(a)
reduces this further to

(\mathbb{R}^{7}\otimes\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}))^{\Gamma}=\left\langle e_{1}\otimes e_{1}\wedge f_{1}+\dots+e_{7}\otimes e_{7}\wedge f_{7}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}.\qed

Proposition 6.6
.

Denote by
a,b,t
the three generators of
\Gamma
as in
Section 6
. For every
\theta\in S^{1}\setminus\{\pm 1\}
there exists a
c_{\theta}>0
depending continuously on
\theta
, such that for every
\xi\in\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
there exists a
\gamma\in\{a,b,t\}\subset\Gamma
such that

|\xi|\leq c_{\theta}|(\textup{id}-\textup{Ad}_{f_{\theta}(\gamma)})(\xi)|.

Proof.

The previous proposition implies that the map

\displaystyle\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})

\displaystyle\to(\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}))^{3}

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle\mapsto((\textup{id}-\textup{Ad}_{f_{\theta}(a)})(\xi),(\textup{id}-\textup{Ad}_{f_{\theta}(b)})(\xi),(\textup{id}-\textup{Ad}_{f_{\theta}(t)})(\xi)))

is injective. Since
\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
is finite dimensional, this implies the claim.
∎

Definition 6.7
.

For each
\theta\in S^{1}
, let
E_{0,\theta}\coloneqq\mathbb{R}^{7}\times_{\Gamma,f_{\theta}}\mathbb{C}^{7}
be the (flat) oriented Euclidean vector bundle associated to
f_{\theta}
. Furthermore, let
\textup{SO}(E_{0,\theta})
and
\textup{O}(E_{0,\theta})
be the principal bundles of positively oriented and non-oriented frames, respectively.

Remark 6.8
.

The bundles
E_{0,\theta}
for
\theta\in S^{1}
are all isometric (because they lie in a smooth 1-parameter family of bundles). However,
Section 6.1
proves that such an isomorphism cannot identify the flat connections on
\textup{SO}(E_{\theta_{1},0})
and
\textup{SO}(E_{\theta_{2},0})
whenever
\theta_{1}\neq\theta_{2}
(cf.
[
DK90
, Proposition 2.2.3]
). We therefore obtain an
S^{1}
-family of flat
\textup{SO}(14)
-bundles.

The situation is slightly different when going to the structure group
\textup{O}(14)
: Here, the isomorphism

\displaystyle E_{0,\theta}

\displaystyle\to E_{\bar{\theta},0}

\displaystyle[y,w]

\displaystyle\mapsto[y,R_{0}w]

where
R_{0}\in\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
maps
(z_{1},\dots,z_{7})\mapsto(\overline{z}_{1},\dots,\overline{z}_{7})
identifies the flat connections on
\textup{O}(E_{0,\theta})
and
\textup{O}(E_{\bar{\theta},0})
.

Proposition 6.9
.

There exists a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
action
\lambda_{0}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{Z}_{2}\to\textup{Isom}(Y_{0},g_{0})
which is induced by the map
\mathbb{R}^{7}\to\mathbb{R}^{7},y\mapsto-y
. This map is orientation reversing and preserves
\psi_{0}
. This action lifts to
E_{0,\theta}
via
\tilde{\lambda}_{0}(-1)[y,w]\coloneqq[-y,R_{0}w]
where
R_{0}\in\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
is given by
R_{0}(z_{1},\dots,z_{7})\coloneqq(\overline{z}_{1},\dots,\overline{z}_{7})
. This action is isometric, orientation reversing and fixes the flat connection on
E_{0,\theta}
. We therefore obtain a connection preserving action (also denoted by
\tilde{\lambda}_{0}
) on
\textup{O}(E_{0,\theta})
.

Proof.

First note, that
(-1,0)\in N_{\textup{O}(7)\ltimes\mathbb{R}^{7}}(\Gamma)
because

(-1,0)\tau_{1}(-1,0)=\tau_{1}^{-1}\quad(-1,0)\alpha(-1,0)=\alpha\quad(-1,0)\beta(-1,0)=\tau_{1}^{-1}\tau_{2}^{-1}\tau_{5}^{-1}\tau_{6}^{-1}\beta

(where
\tau_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,7
denote the map that translates be
e_{i}
). This induces the action on
Y_{0}
. Next, we note that
R_{0},f_{\theta}(\alpha),f_{\theta}(\beta),
and
f_{\theta}(\tau_{1})
satisfy the analogous relations

R_{0}f_{\theta}(\tau_{1})R_{0}=f_{\theta}(\tau_{1}^{-1})\quad R_{0}f_{\theta}(\alpha)R_{0}=f_{\theta}(\alpha)\quad R_{0}f_{\theta}(\beta)R_{0}=f_{\theta}(\tau_{1}^{-1}\tau_{2}^{-1}\tau_{5}^{-1}\tau_{6}^{-1}\beta)

which implies that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
action lifts to
E_{0,\theta}
. The rest of the statement is easily verified.
∎

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
\theta\in S^{1}
and denote by
A_{0,\theta}
the canonical (flat)
\textup{G}_{2}
-instanton on
\textup{O}(E_{0,\theta})
. If
\theta\notin\{1,-1\}
, then
A_{0,\theta}
is infinitesimally irreducible and
\ker L_{A_{0,\theta}}^{*}
is 1-dimensional and consists of elements that are anti-invariant under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action described in
Section 6.1
.

Remark 6.11
.

In the following we work with the bundle
\textup{O}(E_{0,\theta})
because the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action is orientation reversing. The
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons that we will construct using
Section 3.2
, will therefore a-priori also lie on (a glued)
\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
-bundle
\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{\theta})
. However, this
\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
-bundle will admit an
\textup{SO}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
-reduction
\textup{SO}(E_{0,\theta})\subset\textup{O}(E_{0,\theta})
and since
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})=\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
, these
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons will restrict to
\textup{SO}(E_{0,\theta})
. Note that although
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
does not act on
\textup{SO}(E_{0,\theta})
, it still acts on the adjoint bundle
\mathfrak{so}(E_{0,\theta})
. In the light of
Point 2
of
Section 3.2
, one could therefore deform the
\textup{SO}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
-connection directly, without the diversion to
\textup{O}(E_{0,\theta})
.

Proof.

Since
Y_{0}
and
A_{0,\theta}
are flat, the Weitzenböck-formula implies that any element in the kernel of
L_{A_{0,\theta}}
is parallel and therefore corresponds to a fixed-point of the representation of
\Gamma
on
(\mathbb{R}^{7}\otimes\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})\oplus\Lambda^{7}\mathbb{R}^{7}\otimes\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}))
described in
Section 6.1
(cf.
[
Wal13a
, Proposition 9.2]
). Furthermore, the connection is infinitesimally irreducible if and only if the representation of
\Gamma
on
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})=\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
only fixes the center
\mathfrak{z}=0
of
\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
.
Section 6.1
implies therefore, that for
\theta\notin\{1,-1\}

A_{0,\theta}
is infinitesimally irreducible and, furthermore,
\ker(L_{A})=\mathbb{R}a
where
0\neq a\in\Omega^{1}(Y_{0},\mathfrak{so}({E_{0,\theta}}))
. The explicit form of
a
given in
Section 6.1
(or the fact that the entire
1
-parameter family of connections determined by
f_{\theta}
for
\theta\in S^{1}
is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant by
Section 6.1
) implies that
a
is invariant under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action. Since
L_{A_{0,\theta}}^{*}=*L_{A_{0,\theta}}*
we have
\ker(L_{A_{0,\theta}}^{*})=\mathbb{R}(*a)
and since the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action is orientation reversing, we obtain
\tilde{\lambda}_{0}(-1)^{*}(*a)=-a
.
∎

Proposition 6.12
.

For every
\theta\in S^{1}\setminus\{\pm 1\}
there exists a constant
c_{\theta}>0
, which depends continuously on
\theta
, with the following property: Let
U\subset Y_{0}
be an open set such that its preimage
\check{U}\subset\mathbb{R}^{7}
under the quotient map
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{7}/\Gamma=Y_{0}
is connected and for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\check{U}\subset\mathbb{R}^{7}
we have
\textup{dist}_{\check{U}}(y_{1},y_{2})<10(|y_{1}-y_{2}|+1)
(where
\textup{dist}_{\check{U}}(y_{1},y_{2})
measures the distance between
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
with respect to paths contained in
\check{U}
). Then every
\xi\in\Omega^{0}(U,\mathfrak{so}(E_{0,\theta}))
satisfies

\|\xi\|_{C^{{0}}{(U)}}\leq c_{\theta}\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0,\theta}}\xi\|_{C^{{0}}{(U)}}.

Proof.

As in the statement we denote by
\check{U}\subset\mathbb{R}^{7}
the preimage of
U
under the quotient map
\mathbb{R}^{7}\to\mathbb{R}^{7}/\Gamma=Y_{0}
. Furthermore, let
\check{\xi}\in\Omega^{0}(\check{U},\mathbb{R}^{7}\times\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}))^{\Gamma,f_{\theta}}
be the
\Gamma
-equivariant lift of
\xi
. We will prove in the following that

\|\check{\xi}\|_{C^{{0}}{(\check{U})}}\leq c_{\theta}\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}\check{\xi}\|_{C^{{0}}{(\check{U})}}

for a constant
c_{\theta}>0
that depends continuously on
\theta
. Since
A_{0,\theta}
is induced by the trivial connection on
\mathbb{R}^{7}\times\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
and the quotient map
\mathbb{R}^{7}\to Y_{0}
is isometric, this implies the statement.

For this let
\alpha,\beta,\tau_{1}\in\Gamma
be the set of generators described in the beginning of this section. For every
y\in\check{U}
there exists by
Section 6.1
a
\gamma\in\{\alpha,\beta,\tau_{1}\}
such that

\big|\check{\xi}(y)\big|\leq c_{\theta}\big|(\textup{id}-\textup{Ad}_{f_{\theta}(\gamma)})(\check{\xi}(y))\big|=c_{\theta}\big|\check{\xi}(y)-\check{\xi}(\gamma(y))\big|

where
c_{\theta}>0
only depends on
\theta
(and this dependence is continuous). Our assumptions on
\check{U}
and direct inspection of the maps
\alpha,\beta,\tau_{1}
show that whenever
y\in\check{U}\cap[0,1]^{7}
, then we can always find a path connecting
y
and
\gamma(y)
for
\gamma\in\{\alpha,\beta,\tau_{1}\}
of length less than
10\cdot(2\sqrt{7}+1)
. The Fundamental Theorem of Calculus implies therefore that whenever
y\in\check{U}\cap[0,1]^{7}

|\check{\xi}|(y)\leq c_{\theta}|\check{\xi}(y)-\check{\xi}(\gamma(y))|\leq 70c_{\theta}\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}\check{\xi}\|_{C^{{0}}{(\check{U})}}.

Note that a general
y\in\check{U}
can always be translated via elements in
\Gamma
to an element in
\check{U}\cap[0,1]^{7}
. Since
\check{\xi}
is
\Gamma
-equivariant and the action of
\Gamma
on
\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
preserves the norm, the previous inequality actually holds for any
y\in\check{U}
and the claim follows.
∎

6.2
A family of flat connections over a fixed bundle

Next, we fix the bundle
E_{0}\coloneqq E_{0,1}
and connection
A_{0}\coloneqq A_{0,1}
and express the family of flat bundles
(E_{0,\theta},A_{0,\theta})_{\theta\in S^{1}}
discovered in the previous section as a family of flat connections
(A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}}
on the fixed bundle
\textup{O}(E_{0})
(and the subbundle
\textup{SO}(E_{0})
), where
A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
,
A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}+2\pi}
, and
A_{0}+a_{0,-\mathfrak{f}}
will turn out to be gauge equivalent (and
A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
,
A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}+2\pi}
in the case of
\textup{SO}(E_{0})
):

Proposition 6.13
.

Fix
(E_{0},A_{0})\coloneqq(E_{0,1},A_{0,1})
and let
\theta\coloneqq\textup{e}^{i\mathfrak{f}}\in S^{1}
for
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}
. Define

\displaystyle F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{R}^{7}\times\mathbb{C}^{7}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{R}^{7}\times\mathbb{C}^{7}

\displaystyle(y,w)

\displaystyle\mapsto(y,\textup{diag}(\textup{e}^{iy_{1}\mathfrak{f}},\dots,\textup{e}^{iy_{7}\mathfrak{f}})\cdot w)

where
y=(y_{1},\dots,y_{7})\in\mathbb{R}^{7}
. Then
F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}
descends to an (isometric and orientation preserving)
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant isomorphism

\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}\colon\thinspace E_{0}\equiv E_{0,1}\to E_{0,\theta}

that pulls back the flat connection
A_{0,\theta}\in\mathcal{A}(\textup{SO}(E_{0,\theta}))=\mathcal{A}(\textup{O}(E_{0,\theta}))
to

(\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}})^{*}A_{0,\theta}-A_{0}=\textup{diag}(i\mathfrak{f}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{1},\dots,i\mathfrak{f}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{7})\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{7},\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}))^{\Gamma,f_{1}}\cong\Omega^{1}(Y_{0},\mathfrak{so}(E_{0,1})).

Proof.

For the first point, one chooses a set of generators
\{a,b,t\}
as in
Section 6
and checks directly that
F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}\circ(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma))=(\gamma,f_{\theta}(\gamma))\circ F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}
for every generator
\gamma\in\{a,b,t\}
. It is not difficult to see that the same equivariance holds true for the generator of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action. Furthermore, note that
F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}
pulls back the trivial connection
A_{\textup{trivial}}
on
\mathbb{R}^{7}\times\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
to

(F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime})^{*}A_{\textup{trivial}}-A_{\textup{trivial}}=\textup{diag}(i\mathfrak{f}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{1},\dots,i\mathfrak{f}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{7})\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{7},\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})).

Since

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathbb{R}^{7}\times\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}),A_{\textup{trivial}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathbb{R}^{7}\times\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}),A_{\textup{trivial}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\textup{O}(E_{0,1}),A_{0,1}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\textup{O}(E_{0,\theta}),A_{0,\theta}\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}}

commutes, this implies the second statement.
∎

Next, we will modify
\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}
so that the family of connections
((\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}})^{*}A_{0,\textup{e}^{i\mathfrak{f}}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}}
satisfies the assumptions in
Section 4
and
Section 5.1
. For this we first need the following local description of
Y_{0}
near its singular set:

Proposition 6.14

(
[
Joy96b
, Example 18]
)
.

The set
\mathcal{S}
of connected components of
\textup{sing}(Y_{0})
consist of one element
S\cong S^{1}
and (in the notation of
Section 5.2
) there exists an open embedding
\mathtt{J}\colon\thinspace(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})/\mathbb{Z}\to Y_{0}
with
\mathtt{J}^{*}\phi_{0}=\phi_{S}
. The group
\mathbb{Z}_{7}
acts hereby on
B_{\kappa}(0)\subset\mathbb{C}^{3}
via

\big(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\big)\mapsto\big(e^{2\pi i/7}z_{1},e^{4\pi i/7}z_{2},e^{8\pi i/7}z_{3}\big)

(6.1)

and
\mathbb{Z}
only acts on
\mathbb{R}
via translations by multiples of
\sqrt{7}
.

In fact,
\mathtt{J}
can be constructed as follows: There exists an orthonormal basis
\{b_{1},\dots,b_{7}\}
of
\mathbb{R}^{7}
with
b_{1}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{7}}(1,1,1,-1,1,1,1)
such that (under the identification
\mathbb{R}^{7}\cong\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{3}
via
(b_{1},b_{2}+ib_{3},b_{4}+ib_{5},b_{6}+ib_{7})
)
\alpha
attains the form described in (
6.1
) and
\phi_{0}\in\Lambda^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{7})^{*}
is given by (
2.1
) (cf.
[
SW17
, Theorem 8.1]
). Then
\mathtt{J}
is induced by the map

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0)

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{R}^{7}

\displaystyle(s,x_{1}+iy_{1},\dots,x_{3}+iy_{3})

\displaystyle\mapsto sb_{1}+x_{1}b_{2}+y_{1}b_{3}+\dots+y_{3}b_{7}.

By shrinking the positive constant
\kappa>0
in the previous proposition if necessary, we may assume that
\mathtt{J}
extends to an open embedding
\mathtt{J}\colon\thinspace(\mathbb{R}\times B_{2\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})/\mathbb{Z}\to Y_{0}
. The following gives a description of
\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}
in the neighbourhood
\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{2\kappa})
(where
\mathcal{V}_{2\kappa}
is defined analogously to
\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}
prior to
Section 2.1
).

Proposition 6.15
.

There exists
\mathbb{R}
-linear maps
\varphi_{i}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{C}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
for
i=1,\dots,7
with the following properties:

•

|\varphi_{i}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})|\leq|(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})|
and
(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{7})\circ\alpha=(\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{7},-\varphi_{6},-\varphi_{4},\varphi_{1},\varphi_{5})
(where
\alpha
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
via (
6.1
)).

•

Over
(\mathbb{R}\times B_{2\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})/\mathbb{Z}
the isomorphism
\mathtt{J}^{*}\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}\colon\thinspace\mathtt{J}^{*}E_{0}\to\mathtt{J}^{*}E_{0,\theta}
is given by

\big[(s,z),w\big]\mapsto\big[(s,z),M_{\mathfrak{f}}(s,z)\cdot w\big]

where

M_{\mathfrak{f}}(s,z)\coloneqq\textup{diag}\Big(\textup{e}^{i\mathfrak{f}\tfrac{s}{\sqrt{7}}}\cdot\textup{e}^{i\mathfrak{f}\varphi_{1}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})},\dots,\textup{e}^{i\mathfrak{f}\tfrac{s}{\sqrt{7}}}\cdot\textup{e}^{i\mathfrak{f}\varphi_{7}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})}\Big)

(where in the 4-th entry there is a minus in the exponent of
\textup{e}^{-i\mathfrak{f}\tfrac{s}{\sqrt{7}}}
).

Proof.

Let
\{b_{1},\dots,b_{7}\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{7}
be the orthogonal basis described in
Section 6.2
and define
\varphi_{i}
for
(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})\coloneqq(x_{1}+iy_{1},\dots,x_{3}+iy_{3})
as

\varphi_{i}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})\coloneqq\textup{pr}_{i}(x_{1}b_{2}+y_{1}b_{3}+x_{2}b_{4}+y_{2}b_{5}+x_{3}b_{6}+y_{3}b_{7})

where
\textup{pr}_{i}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{R}^{7}\to\mathbb{R}
denotes the i-th coordinate projection. The proposition then follows from the fact that
\{b_{2},\dots,b_{7}\}
are orthonormal, the construction of
\mathtt{J}
in
Section 6.2
, and the explicit form of
F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}
given in
Section 6.2
.
∎

We will now modify the isomorphism
\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}
over
\mathcal{V}_{2\kappa}/\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}
so that
(\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}})^{*}A_{0,\theta}
attains over the gluing region
\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}
the form required in
Section 4
and
Section 5.1
.

Definition 6.16
.

For
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}
let
\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}\colon\thinspace E_{0}\to E_{0,\textup{e}^{i\mathfrak{f}}}
be the isomorphism constructed in
Section 6.2
. We now define the following (orientation preserving and isometric) isomorphism
\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}}\colon\thinspace E_{0}\to E_{0,\textup{e}^{i\mathfrak{f}}}
:

\displaystyle\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}}([y,w])=\begin{cases}\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}([y,w])\textup{ for $[y]\in Y_{0}\setminus\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{2\kappa})$}\\
\big[y,M_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(s,z)w\big]\textup{ for $y=sb_{1}+\dots+\textup{Im}\thinspace(z_{3})b_{7}\in\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{2\kappa})$}\end{cases}

where
\{b_{1},\dots,b_{7}\}
is as in
Section 6.2
and

M_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(s,z)\coloneqq\textup{diag}\Big(\textup{e}^{i\xi\tfrac{s}{\sqrt{7}}}\cdot\textup{e}^{i\xi\chi(|(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})|)\varphi_{1}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})},\dots,\textup{e}^{i\xi\tfrac{s}{\sqrt{7}}}\cdot\textup{e}^{i\xi\chi(|(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})|)\varphi_{7}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})}\Big)

with
\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{7}
as in the previous proposition (and where in the 4-th entry there is a minus in the exponent of
\textup{e}^{-i\mathfrak{f}\tfrac{s}{\sqrt{7}}}
) and
\chi\colon\thinspace[0,2\kappa]\to[0,1]
a non-decreasing function satisfying

\displaystyle\chi(s)=\begin{cases}0\textup{ for $s\leq 5\kappa/4$}\\
1\textup{ for $s\geq 7\kappa/4$.}\end{cases}

Note that this isomorphism is well-defined and smooth by the previous proposition. With this we define the following family of flat connections on
\textup{O}(E_{0})
(and
\textup{SO}(E_{0})
):

(A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}}\quad\textup{where}\quad a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}\coloneqq A_{0}-(\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}})^{*}(A_{0,\textup{e}^{i\mathfrak{f}}}).

Remark 6.17
.

Since all of the
\varphi_{i}
in the previous definition are
\mathbb{R}
-linear, one can easily see that the corresponding isomorphisms
\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}}
are still
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant. Consequently, all
A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
are
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant connections.

We saw in
Section 6.1
that
A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{1}}
and
A_{0}+a_{0+\mathfrak{f}_{2}}
are gauge equivalent in
\textup{SO}(E_{0})
if and only if
\mathfrak{f}_{1}=\mathfrak{f}_{2}+2\pi m
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
(and are gauge equivalent in
\textup{O}(E_{0})
if and only if
\mathfrak{f}_{1}=\pm\mathfrak{f}_{2}+2\pi m
; cf.
Section 6.1
). The following proposition shows that the family
(A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}}
is not even tangent to the gauge orbits.

Proposition 6.18
.

Let
(A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}}
be the family of flat connections on
E_{0}
defined in
Section 6.2
. Then there does not exist an
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi\in\Omega^{0}(Y_{0},\mathfrak{so}(E_{0}))
such that

\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}\xi=(\partial_{\mathbb{R}}a_{0,\,\cdot\,})(\mathfrak{f}),

where
\partial_{\mathbb{R}}a_{0,\,\cdot\,}
denotes the derivative of the map
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
.

Proof.

Section 6.2
and the construction in
Section 6.2
imply that

a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}=(\overline{F_{\mathfrak{f}}})^{*}A_{0,\textup{e}^{i\mathfrak{f}}}-A_{0}=\textup{diag}(i\mathfrak{f}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{1},\dots,i\mathfrak{f}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{7})+\tilde{a}_{\mathfrak{f}}

where
\tilde{a}_{\mathfrak{f}}\in\Omega^{1}(Y_{0},\mathfrak{so}(E_{0}))
is supported in
\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{2\kappa})
and is given in the (local) coordinates
\mathtt{J}\colon\thinspace(\mathbb{R}\times B_{2\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})/\mathbb{Z}\to Y_{0}
by

\displaystyle\mathtt{J}^{*}\tilde{a}_{\mathfrak{f}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{f}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}}\Big(i\ \textup{diag}\big((-1+\chi(|z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}|)\varphi_{1}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}),\dots,(-1+\chi(|z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}|)\varphi_{7}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})\big)\Big).

The section

\displaystyle i\ \textup{diag}((-1+\chi\varphi_{1}),\dots,(-1+\chi\varphi_{7}))

\displaystyle\in\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R}\times B_{2\kappa}(0),\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}))^{\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{7}}

\displaystyle\equiv\Omega^{0}((\mathbb{R}\times B_{2\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})/\mathbb{Z},\mathfrak{so}(E_{0}))

can be extended by zero to a section over the entire orbifold which we schematically write as
\textup{diag}(h_{1},\dots,h_{7})\in\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{7},\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}))^{\Gamma}\equiv\Omega^{0}(Y_{0},\mathfrak{so}(E_{0}))
for functions
h_{1},\dots,h_{7}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{R}^{7}\to i\mathbb{\mathbb{R}}
.

We therefore have for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}
:

\displaystyle(\partial_{\mathbb{R}}a_{0,\,\cdot\,})(\mathfrak{f})

\displaystyle=\textup{diag}(i\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{1},\dots,i\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{7})+\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}}\textup{diag}(h_{1},\dots,h_{7})

\displaystyle=\textup{diag}(i\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{1},\dots,i\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{7})+\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}\textup{diag}(h_{1},\dots,h_{7})

where the last equality comes from the fact that both
a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
and
\textup{diag}(h_{1},\dots,h_{7})
are diagonal matrices which therefore commute. The first term of the right-hand side satisfies

\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}(\textup{diag}(i\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{1},\dots,i\mathop{}\!\textup{d}y^{7}))=0

and lies therefore
L^{2}
-perpendicular to the image of
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}
. This implies the claim.
∎

6.3
Augmenting the flat connections to a family of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant gluing data

Next, we augment the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant family of flat connection
(A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\textup{SO}(E_{0}))=\mathcal{A}(\textup{O}(E_{0}))
constructed in
Section 6.2
to a set of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant gluing data. We begin by finding a set of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant resolution data for the
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
.

Proposition 6.19
.

Define a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
action on
(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}_{7})/\mathbb{Z}
by
(-1)\cdot[s,z]\coloneqq[-s,-z]
. With respect to this action and
\lambda_{0}
, as defined in
Section 6.1
,
\mathtt{J}
as defined in
Section 6.2
becomes
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant.

Proof.

This follows immediately from the description of
\mathtt{J}
given in
Section 6.2
.
∎

Now, recall from
Section 2.2
that for each generic
\zeta\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}\cap(\mathfrak{pu}(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{7}])^{\mathbb{Z}_{7}})_{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
, there exists an ALE Calabi–Yau
3
-fold
(\hat{Z}_{\zeta},\tau_{\zeta},\hat{\omega}_{\zeta},\hat{\Omega}_{\zeta})
asymptotic to
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}_{7}
. Furthermore, if
\textup{Ad}_{\textup{conj}_{-1}}^{*}\zeta=\zeta
, then there exists a lift of the induced
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action on
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}_{7}
to
(\hat{Z}_{\zeta},\tau_{\zeta},\hat{\omega}_{\zeta},\hat{\Omega}_{\zeta})
.

Lemma 6.20
.

We have that
\textup{Ad}_{\textup{conj}_{-1}}^{*}=1
so that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action lifts to
\hat{Z}_{\zeta}
for any
\zeta\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}
. Every generic choice of
\zeta\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}\cap(\mathfrak{pu}(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{7}])^{\mathbb{Z}_{7}})_{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
leads therefore to a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant resolution data of
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
.

Proof.

Recall from
Point 6
in
Section 2.2
that
\textup{Ad}^{*}_{\textup{conj}_{-1}}
was the coadjoint action of the element
\textup{conj}_{-1}\in\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{7}])
defined as the complex linear extension of the conjugation map
\textup{conj}_{-1}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{Z}_{7}\to\mathbb{Z}_{7}
(where
\mathbb{Z}_{7}
is considered as a subgroup of
\textup{SU}(3)
via (
6.1
)). Since
-1
lies in the center of
\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
, conjugation leaves each element in
\mathbb{Z}_{7}\subset\textup{U}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
invariant. Thus
\textup{conj}_{-1}=1
and, consequently,
\textup{Ad}_{\textup{conj}_{-1}}^{*}=1
.
∎

Remark 6.21
.

In
[
Joy96b
, Example 18]
the
\textup{G}_{2}
-orbifold
(Y_{0},\phi_{0})
is resolved using resolution data consisting of a crepant resolution
\hat{Z}
of
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}_{7}
found in
[
MOP87
, Example 3]
and the ALE Calabi–Yau metric on
\hat{Z}

discovered in
[
TY91
]
. While
Point 4
of
Section 2.2
argues that
\hat{Z}
is biholomorphic to one of the manifolds constructed in
Section 2.2
, it is not clear to the author whether the Calabi–Yau metric
in
[
TY91
]
is isometric to any of the metrics in
Section 2.2
. The Kummer construction which we construct in the following is (for a suitable choice of
\zeta\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}\cap(\mathfrak{pu}(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{7}])^{\mathbb{Z}_{7}})_{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
) therefore diffeomorphic to the manifold in
[
Joy96b
, Example 18]
. However, the
\textup{G}_{2}
-structures may be different.

We now fix one choice of generic
\zeta\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}\cap(\mathfrak{pu}(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{7}])^{\mathbb{Z}_{7}})_{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
and consider the corresponding set of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant resolution data consisting of

defined in

, the ALE-space

, and the

\lambda_{0}
and the

described in

of

.

Proposition 6.22
.

Recall from
Section 6.2
that there exists an
R\in\textup{SO}(\mathbb{R}^{7})
such that
R^{-1}\alpha R
is of the form (
6.1
). Define the isomorphism

F_{1}\colon\thinspace(z_{1},\dots,z_{7})\mapsto(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},-\bar{z}_{4},z_{5},z_{6},z_{7})\in\textup{O}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})

and with this the map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\colon\thinspace\mathbb{C}\oplus(\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})\equiv(\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{3})\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}^{7}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\equiv\mathbb{C}^{7}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}^{7}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}}

Furthermore, define the flat
\textup{SO}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7})
-bundle

(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0))\times_{\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{7}}(\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})\to(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})/\mathbb{Z}

where
\mathbb{Z}
acts trivially on
\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{7}
acts via

\displaystyle f_{1}(n)\big(w,v_{1}\otimes

\displaystyle w_{1},v_{2}\otimes w_{2},v_{3}\otimes w_{3}\big)=\big(w,e^{2n\pi i/7}v_{1}\otimes w_{1},e^{4n\pi i/7}v_{2}\otimes w_{2},e^{8n\pi i/7}v_{3}\otimes w_{3}\big).

The map

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathtt{J}}\colon\thinspace(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0))\times_{\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{7}}(\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})

\displaystyle\to\mathtt{J}^{*}E_{0}

\displaystyle[(s,z),w]

\displaystyle\mapsto[y,F(w)]

where
y=sb_{1}+\textup{Re}\thinspace(z_{1})b_{2}+\dots+\textup{Im}\thinspace(z_{3})b_{7}
for the orthonormal basis
\{b_{1},\dots,b_{7}\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{7}
of
Section 6.2
is an isomorphism that identifies
\mathtt{J}^{*}A_{0}
with the canonical flat connection on
(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0))\times_{\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{7}}(\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})
. If we define a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action on
(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0))\times_{\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{7}}(\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})
via

(-1)\cdot[(s,z),(w,z_{1}\otimes w_{1},z_{2}\otimes w_{2},z_{3}\otimes w_{3})]=[(-s,-z),(\overline{w},z_{1}\otimes\overline{w}_{1},z_{2}\otimes\overline{w}_{2},z_{3}\otimes\overline{w}_{3})],

then
\tilde{\mathtt{J}}
becomes
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant.

Proof.

We will prove that
F
conjugates between the monodromy representations of
\mathtt{J}^{*}A_{0}
and the flat connection on
(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0))\times_{\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{7}}(\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})
. Since
\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0)
is the orbifold covering space of
(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})/\mathbb{Z}
this implies the first part of the proposition.

The singular set is precisely the image of the fixed-point set of
\alpha
in
Y_{0}
. Thus, the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{7}
is generated by
f_{1}(\alpha)
. Similarly, the action of
\mathbb{Z}
is generated by
f_{1}(\tau_{1}\tau_{2}\tau_{3}\tau_{4}^{-1}\tau_{5}\tau_{6}\tau_{7})=\textup{id}
. Conjugating these two actions by
F_{1}
and identifying
\mathbb{C}^{7}=\mathbb{R}^{7}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}
leads to

F_{1}^{-1}f_{\theta}(\alpha)F_{1}=\alpha\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}}\qquad\textup{and}\qquad F_{1}^{-1}f_{\theta}(\tau_{1}\tau_{2}\tau_{3}\tau_{4}^{-1}\tau_{5}\tau_{6}\tau_{7})F_{1}=1_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}}.

Conjugating this a second time by
R
leads (under the identification
\mathbb{R}^{7}\cong\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{3}
) to the action of
f_{1}(1)
as described above. This concludes the first part of the proposition. The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariance of
\tilde{\mathtt{J}}
follows from

(F^{-1}\circ\lambda_{0}(-1)\circ F)(w,v_{1}\otimes w_{1},v_{2}\otimes w_{2},v_{3}\otimes w_{3})=(\overline{w},v_{1}\otimes\overline{w}_{1},v_{2}\otimes\overline{w}_{2},v_{3}\otimes\overline{w}_{3}).\qed

Proposition 6.23
.

Let
\tilde{\mathtt{J}}\colon\thinspace(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0))\times_{\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{7}}(\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})\to\mathtt{J}^{*}E_{0}
be the isomorphism of the previous proposition and

\tilde{\mathtt{J}}^{*}a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}=\tfrac{\mathfrak{f}}{\sqrt{7}}(\textup{id}_{\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{3}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}i)\mathop{}\!\textup{d}s.

\displaystyle\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0),\mathfrak{so}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{C}^{7}))^{\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{7}}

\displaystyle\equiv\Omega^{1}((\mathbb{R}\times B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})/\mathbb{Z},\mathtt{J}^{*}E_{0}).

Conjugating

F
defined in

gives

the local framing

Proposition 6.27
.

There exists a lift of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action to

, satisfies the claim.

Proposition 6.29
.

For each
t\in(0,T_{K})
there exist a principal
\textup{SO}(14)
- and a
\textup{O}(14)
-bundle
\pi_{t}^{\textup{SO}}\colon\thinspace\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t})\to\hat{Y}_{t}
and
\pi_{t}^{\textup{O}}\colon\thinspace\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{t})\to\hat{Y}_{t}
, respectively, together with smooth 1-parameter families of connections
(\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t}))
and
(\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{t}))
that satisfy:

1.

\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t})\subset\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{t})
is a subbundle and the connections
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
reduce to
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}
.

2.

There exists a lift of
\hat{\lambda}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{Z}_{2}\to\textup{Isom}(\hat{Y}_{t},\tilde{g}_{t})
to
\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{t})
preserving
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}
.

3.

For every compact interval
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus\pi\mathbb{Z}
there exist
t
-independent constants
C>0
and
0<T<T_{K}
(which depend on
\mathfrak{F}
) such that for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
:

•

\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}F_{\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}})\wedge\psi_{t}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-5/2},t}{}}\leq Ct
for
\ell=0,1,2
, where
\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}F_{\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}
denotes the
\ell
-th derivative of the function
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto F_{\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}
,

•

for
\ell=0,1,2
and any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}L_{\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}})\underline{a}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{}}\leq C\|\underline{a}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}

for any
\underline{a}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{so}(\hat{E}_{t}))\oplus\Omega^{7}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{so}(\hat{E}_{t}))

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}L^{*}_{\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}})\underline{b}\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{}}\leq C\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}}

for any
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{6}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{so}(\hat{E}_{t}))\oplus\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{so}(\hat{E}_{t}))

where
\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}L_{\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}
(and similarly

) denotes the
\ell
-th derivative of the function

,

•

for any

and
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{\beta-1},t}{}}\leq C\|\xi\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{}},

•

for
t<T^{\prime}
any
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{so}(\hat{E}_{\theta,t}))\oplus\Omega^{7}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{so}(\hat{E}_{\theta,t}))

\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{-1/2},t}{}}\leq C\|L_{\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-5/2},t}{}}

All norms in this statement are taken with respect to the metric and the Levi–Civita connection on

and the (negative) Killing-form and

on

Proof.

In the beginning of
Section 6.2
we have defined the flat bundles

and
(\textup{O}(E_{0}),A_{0}^{\textup{O}})

\{(\pi_{0}\colon\thinspace\textup{SO}(E_{0})\to Y_{0},A_{0}),(\tilde{\mathtt{J}},\pi\colon\thinspace\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{\zeta})\to\hat{Z}_{\zeta},\hat{A}_{\zeta},\hat{\tilde{\rho}}_{\zeta}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{Z}\to\textup{Isom}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{\zeta})))\}
and

Section 4
(cf.

).

The existence of the bundles

and

and the respective families of connection

and
(\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{t}))
follows therefore from
Section 4
and
Section 4
. Note that since
(\textup{SO}(E_{0}),A_{0}^{\textup{SO}})\subset(\textup{O}(E_{0}),A_{0}^{\textup{O}})
and
(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{\zeta}),\hat{A}_{\zeta}^{\textup{SO}})\subset(\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{\zeta}),\hat{A}_{\zeta}^{\textup{O}})
are subbundles, we also have
(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t}),\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})\subset(\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{t}),\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})
by the construction of these bundles and connections in the proof of
Section 4
and
Section 4
. This proves the first point.

We have shown in
Section 6.1
and
Section 6.2
that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action on
Y_{0}
lifts to
\textup{O}(E_{0})
and preserves
A_{0}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathbb{R}
. Furthermore, we have proven in
Section 6.3
that there exists a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action on
\textup{O}((\mathbb{R}\times\hat{E}_{\zeta})/\mathbb{Z})
that preserves
\hat{A}_{\zeta}^{\textup{O}}
and that augments
\{(\pi_{0}\colon\thinspace\textup{O}(E_{0})\to Y_{0},A_{0}),(\tilde{\mathtt{J}},\pi\colon\thinspace\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{\zeta})\to\hat{Z}_{\zeta},\hat{A}_{\zeta},\hat{\tilde{\rho}}_{\zeta}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{Z}\to\textup{Isom}(\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{\zeta})))\}
to a set of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant gluing data (cf.
Section 6.3
). The existence of the lift of
\hat{\lambda}
to
\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{\theta,t})
preserving
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
follows therefore also from
Section 4
and
Section 4
. The estimates listed under the first three bullets of point three also follow from the same propositions (see also
Section 4
).

The estimate
\|\underline{b}\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{-1/2},t}{}}\leq c\|L_{\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}L_{\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\underline{b}\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-5/2},t}{}}
for every
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant element
\underline{b}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{so}(\hat{E}_{\theta,t}))\oplus\Omega^{7}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{so}(\hat{E}_{\theta,t}))
and sufficiently small
t\in(0,T)
follows from
Section 5.2
. Our description of
\hat{a}_{\zeta,\mathfrak{f}}
in
Section 6.3
together with
Section 6.1
, and
Section 2.3
imply here-for that
Section 5.2
is satisfied.

Similarly,
Section 6.1
implies that the condition in
Section 5.2
is satisfied which then implies
\|\xi\|_{C^{{2},\alpha}_{{-1/2},t}{}}\leq c\|\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}^{O}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}^{*}\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{\tilde{A}^{O}_{t}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}}\xi\|_{C^{{0},\alpha}_{{-5/2},t}{}}
for any
\xi\in\Omega^{0}(\hat{Y}_{t},\mathfrak{so}(\hat{E}_{t}))
and possibly smaller
t<T
.
∎

Remark 6.30
.

As in
Section 6.1
one can see that the connections
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}_{1}}
and
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}_{2}}
are gauge equivalent if and only if
\mathfrak{f}_{1}=\mathfrak{f}_{2}+2\pi m
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
(this is because the restriction of
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}_{i}}
to
\hat{Y}_{t}\setminus\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}^{t}
agrees with the flat connection
A_{0}^{\textup{SO}}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
). The corresponding statement about
\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{t})
holds also true, where
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}_{1}}
and
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}_{2}}
are gauge equivalent if and only if
\mathfrak{f}_{1}=\pm\mathfrak{f}_{2}+2\pi m
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proposition 6.31
.

Let
\pi_{t}^{\textup{SO}}\colon\thinspace\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t})\to\hat{Y}_{t}
be the principal bundle constructed in the previous proposition. Furthermore, for a fixed compact interval
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus\pi\mathbb{Z}
let
(\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t}))
be the family of connections described in the previous proposition. Then there are constants
0<T^{\prime}<T
and
C^{\prime}>0
such that for each
t\in(0,T^{\prime})
there exists a smooth family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons
(A_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}\in\mathcal{A}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t}))
with

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}(A_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}})-\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}(\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})\|_{C^{{1},\alpha}_{{-3/2},t}{}}\leq C^{\prime}t\qquad\textup{for $\ell=0,1,2$},

(6.2)

where
\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}(A_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}})
(and similarly
\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}(\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})
) denotes the
\ell
-th derivative of the function
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto A_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
. For sufficiently small
T^{\prime}
, all
A^{\textup{SO}}_{t}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
are infinitesimally irreducible.

Proof.

In the previous proposition we have also constructed the bundle
\pi_{t}^{\textup{O}}\colon\thinspace\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{t})\to\hat{Y}_{t}
together with the smooth family of connections
(\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}
. Furthermore, we have shown that
Section 3.2
,
Section 3.2
, and
Section 3.2
can be applied to
(\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}
. This implies the existence of a family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons
(A_{t}^{\textup{O}}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{t}))
that satisfies all the properties stated in the proposition (where (
6.2
) are taken with respect to
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
). Since
\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t})\subset\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{t})
is a subbundle and
\mathfrak{so}(14)=\mathfrak{o}(14)
, the instanton
A_{t}^{\textup{O}}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
reduces for any
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
to
\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t})
which is the instanton that we call
A_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}}\in\mathcal{A}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{\theta,t}))
.

Since
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
is the reduction of
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
, (
6.2
) follows from the corresponding estimate on
\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}(\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}})-\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{\ell}(A_{t}^{\textup{O}}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}})
. Furthermore, since the adjoint bundles of
\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t})
and
\textup{O}(\hat{E}_{t})
agree,
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
is infinitesimally irreducible if and only if
\tilde{A}_{t}^{\textup{O}}+\tilde{a}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
is.
∎

Remark 6.32
.

The construction of the connection
A^{\textup{O}}_{t}
in the previous proof is a technical step because the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action does not lift directly to
\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t})
. However, as already noted in
Section 6.1
we obtain an induced action on the adjoint bundle
\mathfrak{so}(\hat{E}_{t})
and the map
\Upsilon_{\tilde{A}^{\textup{SO}}_{t}}
(as defined in
Section 3.1
) associated to the connection
\tilde{A}^{\textup{SO}}_{t}
is equivariant with respect to this action. Thus, one can avoid the auxiliary construction of
A^{\textup{O}}_{t}
by appealing to
Point 2
of
Section 3.2
.

Proposition 6.33
.

Let
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus\pi\mathbb{Z}
be a compact interval and let
(A_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t}))
be the family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons of the previous proposition. For every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
the connection
A_{t}^{\textup{SO}}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
is non-flat.

Proof.

We will show that due to its topology the bundle
\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t})
does not admit any flat connection. Assume therefore that
A\in\mathcal{A}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t}))
is flat to produce a contradiction. Fix a point
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and define

\iota\colon\thinspace(t\tau_{\zeta})^{-1}(B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})\subset\hat{Z}_{\zeta}\to\{s_{0}\}\times(t\tau_{\zeta})^{-1}(B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})\to\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}^{t}\to\hat{Y}_{t}

to be the canonical inclusion (see the paragraph before
Section 2.1
for the definition of these sets). By the construction in
Section 4
, we have that
\iota^{*}\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t})\cong\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{\zeta})_{|(t\tau_{\zeta})^{-1}(B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})}
. Pulling back
A
, we obtain a flat connection on the (real) vector bundle
(\hat{E}_{\zeta})_{{|(t\tau_{\zeta})^{-1}(B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})}}
and therefore a flat unitary connection on

(\hat{E}_{\zeta}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})_{{|(t\tau_{\zeta})^{-1}(B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})}}\cong(\hat{E}_{\zeta}\oplus\overline{\hat{E}}_{\zeta})_{{|(t\tau_{\zeta})^{-1}(B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})}}\cong(2\oplus_{i=0}^{6}\hat{E}_{\nu_{i}})_{{|(t\tau_{\zeta})^{-1}(B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})}}

where
\pi_{\ell}\colon\thinspace\hat{E}_{\nu_{\ell}}\to\hat{Z}_{\zeta}
denotes to complex vector bundle associated to the representation
\nu_{\ell}\colon\thinspace\mathbb{Z}_{7}\to\textup{U}(1)
which maps
1\mapsto\textup{e}^{2\pi i\ell/7}
(this follows from the definition of
\hat{E}_{\zeta}
in the previous section). Since
(t\tau_{\zeta})^{-1}(B_{\kappa}(0)/\mathbb{Z}_{7})
and
\hat{Z}_{\zeta}
are homotopy-equivalent, this implies by Chern–Weil theory for the reduced Chern-character
\widetilde{\textup{ch}}=\textup{ch}-\textup{rk}
:

0=\widetilde{\textup{ch}}(\hat{E}_{\zeta}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})=2\sum_{i=0}^{6}\widetilde{\textup{ch}}(\hat{E}_{\nu_{i}})=2\sum_{i=1}^{6}\widetilde{\textup{ch}}(\hat{E}_{\nu_{i}})\in\textup{H}^{*}(\hat{Z}_{\zeta},\mathbb{R})

(6.3)

(where we have used in the last equation that
\pi_{0}\colon\thinspace\hat{E}_{\nu_{0}}\to\hat{Z}_{\zeta}
is the trivial vector bundle).

Next, we define vectors
v_{1},\dots,v_{6}\in\mathbb{R}^{6}
via

(v_{i})_{j}\coloneqq\int_{\hat{Z}_{\zeta}}\widetilde{\textup{ch}}(\hat{E}_{\nu_{i}})\widetilde{\textup{ch}}(\hat{E}_{\nu_{j}}^{*})\quad\textup{for $j=1,\dots,6$}

where the integral is to be understood as in
[
DW16
, Section 7]
via
\textup{H}^{k}(\hat{Z}_{\zeta},\mathbb{R})\cong\textup{H}^{k}_{\textup{cpt}}(\hat{Z}_{\zeta},\mathbb{R})
for
k=2,4
(cf.
[
DW16
, Section 7]
). Multiplying (
6.3
) with
\widetilde{\textup{ch}}(\hat{E}_{\nu_{j}}^{*})
for
j=1,\dots,6
and integrating yields

0=2\sum_{i=1}^{6}v_{i}.

However, this is in contradiction to
[
DW16
, Equation (1.8)]
which implies that
v_{1},\dots,v_{6}
are linearly independent.
∎

6.5
Injectivity of the associated curve into the moduli space

We fix a compact interval
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus\pi\mathbb{Z}
and let
(A_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}}\subset\mathcal{A}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t}))
be the corresponding family of
\textup{G}_{2}
-instantons constructed in
Section 6.4
. The following proves that for sufficiently small
t
no two connections
A_{t,\mathfrak{f}_{1}}
,
A_{t,\mathfrak{f}_{2}}
for distinct
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2}\in\mathfrak{F}
are gauge equivalent.

Proposition 6.34
.

There exists a
0<T^{\prime\prime}<T^{\prime}
such that for
t<T^{\prime\prime}
the curve

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{A}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t}))/\mathcal{G}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t}))

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto[A_{t,\mathfrak{f}}]

into the space of connections modulo gauge is injective.

Proof.

Assume that this was not the case. Then there exists a sequence
(t_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,T^{\prime})
with
t_{n}\to 0
and for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
two distinct
\mathfrak{f}_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{F}
and a gauge transformation
u_{n}\in\mathcal{G}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t_{n}}))
such that
u_{n}^{*}(A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}})=A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}}
. We will prove in the following proposition that this implies the existence of an
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
and
\xi\in\Omega^{0}(Y_{0},\mathfrak{so}(E_{0}))
such that
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}}\xi=(\partial_{\mathbb{R}}a_{0,\,\cdot\,})(\mathfrak{f})
(where
A_{0}
and
a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
are as in
Section 6.2
and
Section 6.2
). However, by
Section 6.2
this cannot happen and we have arrived therefore at a contradiction.
∎

Proposition 6.35
.

Suppose there exist
(t_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,T^{\prime})
,
(\mathfrak{f}_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathfrak{F}\times\mathfrak{F}
with
\mathfrak{f}_{n}\neq\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}
and for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
a gauge transformation
u_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{\hat{Y}_{t_{n}}}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t_{n}}))
such that

t_{n}\to 0\qquad\textup{and}\qquad u_{n}^{*}(A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}})=A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}}.

Then the following hold:

1.

There is an
\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}\in\mathfrak{F}
such that (up to taking subsequences)
\mathfrak{f}_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}
,

2.

The restriction of
(u_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
to
\hat{Y}_{t_{n}}\setminus(t_{n}\tau)^{-1}(S)\cong Y_{0}\setminus S
(where
S
denotes the singular set of
Y_{0}
) converges (up to taking a subsequence) in
C^{2}_{\textup{loc}}(Y_{0}\setminus S)
to
\pm\textup{id}\in\mathcal{G}_{Y_{0}}(\textup{SO}(E_{0}))

3.

There exists a
\xi\in\Omega^{0}(Y_{0},\mathfrak{so}(E_{0}))
such that
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}}}\xi=(\partial_{\mathbb{R}}a_{0,\,\cdot}\,)(\mathfrak{f}_{\infty})
.

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{F}
is compact, we can assume that up to taking subsequences
\mathfrak{f}_{n}\to\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}
and
\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}^{\prime}
. In order to prove the first point we therefore only need to show that
\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}
.

For this we focus on the domain
\hat{Y}_{t}\setminus(t\tau)^{-1}(S)
(where
S\subset Y_{0}
denotes the singular set and
t\tau\colon\thinspace\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}^{t}\to\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}
was defined prior to
Section 2.1
) which we will subsequently identify with
Y_{0}\setminus S
using
t\tau
. Likewise, we identify the bundles

\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t})_{|\hat{Y}_{t}\setminus(t\tau)^{-1}(S)}\cong\textup{SO}(E_{0})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}

(cf. the construction in the proof of
Section 4
).

We will show in the subsequent paragraphs that there exists a converging subsequence
u_{n|Y_{0}\setminus S}\to u
(in
C^{2}(Y_{0}\setminus S)
) where
u\colon\thinspace\textup{SO}(E_{0})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}\to\textup{SO}(E_{0})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}
is a (smooth) bundle isomorphism that satisfies
u^{*}(A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}^{\prime}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}=(A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}
. This implies that the monodromy representations of
(A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}^{\prime}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}
and
(A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}
are conjugated in
\textup{SO}(14)
(cf.
[
DK90
, Proposition 2.2.3]
). It is not difficult to see that
\pi_{1}(Y_{0}\setminus S)\cong\Gamma
(this follows, for example, by realising that
\Gamma
acts freely on the pre-image of
Y_{0}\setminus S
under the quotient map
\mathbb{R}^{7}\to\mathbb{R}^{7}/\Gamma=Y_{0}
and that this pre-image is simply connected) and that the monodromy representation of each
(A_{0,\mathfrak{f}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}
for any
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
corresponds to the homomorphism
f_{\textup{e}^{i{\mathfrak{f}}}}
as defined in
Section 6.1
. The first point of the proposition follows therefore from
Section 6.1
where we have proven that the homomorphisms associated to two distinct
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in S^{1}
are not conjugated in
\textup{SO}(14)
and therefore
\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}=\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}^{\prime}
. Similarly, the fact that
\pm\textup{id}\in\textup{SO}(14)
are the only elements whose conjugation fixes
f_{\theta}
for any
\theta\in S^{1}\setminus\{\pm 1\}
(also proven in
Section 6.1
) implies that
u=\pm\textup{id}
.

In order to prove the existence of a gauge transformation
u\in\mathcal{G}_{Y_{0}\setminus S}\textup{SO}(E_{0}))
as described in the previous paragraph, we write for any
t\in(0,T^{\prime})
and
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
the connection
(A_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}
as
(A_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}=(A_{0})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}}^{\circ}
for
a_{t,\mathfrak{f}}^{\circ}\in\Omega^{1}(Y_{0}\setminus S,\mathfrak{so}(E_{0}))
. Next, we use the isometric inclusions
\mathcal{G}(\textup{SO}(E_{0}))\subset\Gamma(\textup{End}(E_{0}))
and
\mathfrak{so}(E_{0})\subset\textup{End}(E_{0})
(induced by the respective inclusions
\textup{SO}(\mathbb{R}^{14}),\mathfrak{so}(\mathbb{R}^{14})\subset\textup{End}(\mathbb{R}^{14})
) to regard
u_{n}
and
a_{t,\mathfrak{f}}^{\circ}
as sections of this (linear) bundle. The condition that
u_{n}^{*}(A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}})=A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}}
implies

\displaystyle(A_{0})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}+a_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=(A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}=u_{n}^{*}(A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}

\displaystyle=(A_{0,1})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}+u_{n}^{-1}\circ\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}}(u_{n})+u_{n}^{-1}\circ a_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}^{\circ}\circ u_{n}

and therefore

\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0}}u_{n}=u_{n}\circ a_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}}^{\circ}-a_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}^{\circ}\circ u_{n}.

(6.4)

Next, we pick an exhaustion

\overline{Y_{0}\setminus\mathtt{J}(\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}})\coloneqq K_{1}\subset K_{2}\subset K_{3}\subset\cdots\subset Y_{0}\setminus S

by compact subsets. The restriction of the weighted norm
\|\cdot\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}_{{\beta},t}{(K_{i})}}
to any
K_{i}
is uniformly (in
t
) equivalent to the norm
\|\cdot\|_{C^{{k},\alpha}{(K_{i})}}
because the weight function
w_{t}
is bounded from below and above on this domain.
Section 2.1
implies therefore that the restriction
g_{t_{n}|Y_{0}\setminus S}
converge in
C^{\infty}_{\text{loc}}
to
g_{0|Y_{0}\setminus S}
. Similarly, (
6.2
) for
\ell=0
and the construction of
\tilde{A}_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
in
Section 4
and
Section 4
imply that for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
we have
(A_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{|K_{i}}\to(A_{0,\mathfrak{f}})_{|K_{i}}
in
C^{1,\alpha}(K_{i})
, or equivalently,
a_{t,\mathfrak{f}}^{\circ}\to a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
in
C^{1,\alpha}(K_{i})
(where
a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}\in\Omega^{1}(Y_{0},\mathfrak{so}(E_{0}))
is as in
Section 6.2
and
a_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}}^{\circ}\in\Omega^{1}(Y_{0}\setminus S,\mathfrak{so}(E_{0}))
was defined above so that
(A_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}=(A_{0})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}+a_{t,\mathfrak{f}}^{\circ}
).

In particular, this implies that for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
the
C^{1,\alpha}(K_{i})
-norms of
a_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}}^{\circ}
are uniformly bounded. Since
\textup{SO}(14)
is compact, we obtain an a priori bound on the
C^{0}
-norms of
u_{n}
and by (
6.4
) a uniform bound on their
C^{1}
-norms. Bootstrapping improves this to a
C^{2,\alpha}
-bound. As
K_{i}
is compact, the Arzelà–Ascoli Theorem implies that there is a
C^{2}
-converging subsequence
u_{n}\to u
in
K_{i}
and since
\textup{SO}(\mathbb{R}^{14})\subset\textup{End}(\mathbb{R}^{14})
is closed,
u\in\mathcal{G}_{K_{i}}(\textup{SO}(E_{0}))
. Taking a diagonal sequence over all
K_{i}
, we obtain a
u\in\mathcal{G}_{Y_{0}\setminus S}(\textup{SO}(E_{0}))
such that
u_{n}\to u
in
C^{2}_{\text{loc}}(Y_{0}\setminus S)
. Continuity then gives
u^{*}(A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}^{\prime}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}=(A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}})_{|Y_{0}\setminus S}
and bootstrapping via (
6.4
) improves the regularity of
u
to
C^{\infty}
.

In order to prove the last point note that after possibly multiplying
u_{n}
by
-\textup{id}
we can assume that
u=\textup{id}
. For each of the compact subsets
K_{i}\subset Y_{0}\setminus S
there thus exists an
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that for each
n>N
the restriction
(u_{n})_{|K_{i}}
lies in the image of the exponential map. Thus, for sufficiently large
n
there exists a unique
\xi_{n}\in\Omega^{0}(K_{i},\mathfrak{so}(E_{0}))
such that

(A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}})_{|K_{i}}=(A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}})_{|K_{i}}+\textup{e}^{-\xi_{n}}\circ(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}}\textup{e}^{\xi_{n}})=(A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}})_{|K_{i}}+\Upsilon(\xi_{n})\circ(\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}}\xi_{n})

where

\Upsilon(\xi_{n})\coloneqq\frac{1-\textup{e}^{-\textup{ad}_{\xi_{n}}}}{\textup{ad}_{\xi_{n}}}.

This implies

\displaystyle\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}}\big(\tfrac{\xi_{n}}{\mathfrak{f}_{n}-\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}\big)=\Upsilon(\xi_{n})^{-1}\circ\tfrac{A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}}-A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}}{\mathfrak{f}_{n}-\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}

and since
(A_{t,\mathfrak{f}})_{|K_{i}}\to(A_{0,\mathfrak{f}})_{|K_{i}}
in
C^{1,\alpha}(K_{i})
for every
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus\pi\mathbb{Z}
we have by
Section 6.1

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{\xi_{n}}{\mathfrak{f}_{n}-\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}\|_{C^{{2}}{(K_{i})}}\leq c\|\Upsilon(\xi_{n})^{-1}\circ\tfrac{A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}}-A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}}{\mathfrak{f}_{n}-\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}\|_{C^{{1}}{(K_{i})}}

(6.5)

for some
c>0
independent of
K_{i}
and
n>N
. Since
u_{n}\to\textup{id}
in
C^{2}(K_{i})
we obtain
\xi_{n}\to 0
in
C^{2}(K_{i})
. Furthermore, there exists an
\tilde{\mathfrak{f}}_{n}\in\mathfrak{F}
between
\mathfrak{f}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}
such that

\tfrac{A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}}-A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}}{\mathfrak{f}_{n}-\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}=\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{2}A_{t_{n},\tilde{\mathfrak{f}}_{n}}(\mathfrak{f}_{n}-\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}).

Since
\mathfrak{f}_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}
by the first point of this proposition and

(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}})_{|K_{i}}\to(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}})_{|K_{i}}\quad\textup{and}\quad(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{2}A_{t_{n},\tilde{\mathfrak{f}}_{n}})_{|K_{i}}\to(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}^{2}A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}})_{|K_{i}}\quad\textup{in $C^{1,\alpha}(K_{i})$}

by (
6.2
) in
Section 6.4
and the construction in
Section 4
, we obtain that the quotient
\tfrac{A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}}-A_{t_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}}{\mathfrak{f}_{n}-\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}
converges in
C^{1,\alpha}(K_{i})
to
(\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}})_{|K_{i}}
. The right-hand side of (
6.5
) is therefore uniformly bounded and the Arzelà–Ascoli Theorem implies that
\tfrac{\xi_{n}}{\mathfrak{f}_{n}-\mathfrak{f}_{n}^{\prime}}
converges (up to taking a subsequence) in
C^{1}(K_{i})
. Taking a diagonal sequence over all
K_{i}\subset Y_{0}\setminus S
we obtain a
\xi_{\infty}\in\Omega^{0}(Y_{0}\setminus S,\mathfrak{g}_{P})
such that
\xi_{n}\to\xi_{\infty}
in
C^{1}_{\textup{loc}}(Y_{0}\setminus S)
. By continuity
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}}}\xi_{\infty}=\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}}
over
Y_{0}\setminus S
and
Section 6.1
implies that
\|\xi_{\infty}\|_{C^{{0}}{(Y_{0}\setminus S)}}<c
. Integration by parts implies that
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}}}\xi_{\infty}=\partial_{\mathfrak{F}}A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}}
holds over the entire orbifold
Y_{0}
in the sense of distributions. Elliptic regularity for the (overdetermined elliptic) operator
\mathop{}\!\textup{d}_{A_{0,\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}}}
then implies that
\xi_{\infty}\in\Omega^{0}(Y_{0},\mathfrak{g}_{P})
and writing
A_{0,\mathfrak{f}}=A_{0}+a_{0,\mathfrak{f}}
for any
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
proves the third statement.
∎

Remark 6.36
.

A similar proof shows that the connections
A_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
for compact
\mathfrak{F}\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus\pi\mathbb{Z}
are all irreducible (i.e. the only
u\in\mathcal{G}(\textup{SO}(\hat{E}_{t}))
with
u^{*}(A_{t,\mathfrak{f}})=A_{t,\mathfrak{f}}
are
u=\pm\textup{id}
) once
t
is sufficiently small.
